## I. The Anarchistic Idea and Its Development / 一、无政府主义观念及其发展

===Original===
The Anarchistic Idea and Its Development

Anarchy signifies the negation of authority. Now, authority claims to justify its existence by the necessity of defending social institutions﻿—the family, religion, property, etc.﻿—and it has created a mass of tools to secure it in the exercise of its functions and its sanctioning privilege. Chief among these are the law, the magistracy, the army, the legislative and executive power, etc., so that, forced to reply to all these, the idea of Anarchy had to attack all social prejudices, go to the bottom of all human knowledge, in order to demonstrate that its conceptions were in conformity with the physiological and psychological nature of man and adequate to the observance of natural laws, whilst our present organization, established contrary to all logic and common sense, causes our societies to be unstable, overturned by revolutions, themselves occasioned by the accumulated hatred of those who are crushed under these arbitrary institutions.

In combating authority, then, the Anarchists had to attack every institution of which power had constituted itself the defender, and the necessity for which it seeks to demonstrate in order to justify its own existence. Thus the scope of Anarchistic ideas was widened. Starting out, with a simple political negation, the Anarchist has had to attack economic and social prejudices also, to find a formula which, while denying private property, the basis of our present economic order, should at the same time affirm our aspirations for a future organization. Hence the word Communism naturally came to be coupled with the word Anarchy.

Further on we shall see that certain lovers of the quintessence of abstraction have sought to claim that, from the moment Anarchy signified complete expansion of individuality, the words Anarchy and Communism protested against such a coupling. Against this insinuation we shall prove that individuality cannot develop except in the community; that the latter cannot exist unless the former evolves freely; and that they mutually complement each other.

===Chinese===
无政府主义观念及其发展

无政府主义（Anarchy）意味着对权威（authority）的否定。而权威却声称，保卫种种社会制度——家庭、宗教、财产等等——实属必要，并以此为自己的存在辩护；它还制造出大批工具，来保障自己行使职能、行使制裁特权。其中最主要者，是法律、司法当局、军队、立法权与行政权等等。于是，不得不对这一切作出回应的无政府主义思想，便不得不向一切社会偏见发起攻击，穷究全部人类知识的根底，以证明其种种观念既契合人的生理与心理本性，又能遵从自然规律；而我们现今的社会组织违背一切逻辑与常识而建立，致使我们的社会动荡不定、屡遭革命的倾覆——这些革命本身，又正是被这些专断制度碾碎的人们积聚的仇恨所引发。

因此，无政府主义者（Anarchists）在与权威作斗争时，不得不攻击权力自命为其捍卫者的一切制度——权力力图证明这些制度的必要，借以证明自身的存在。无政府主义思想的视野由此得以拓宽。无政府主义者出发时只怀有一种单纯的政治否定，随后却不得不进而攻击经济偏见与社会偏见，以求找到一个方案：它一方面否定作为现今经济秩序之基础的私有制（private property），另一方面又申明我们对未来组织的种种向往。于是，「共产主义」（Communism）一词自然而然地与「无政府主义」一词结合到了一起。

后文我们将看到，某些醉心于抽象之精粹的人士力图宣称：既然无政府主义意味着个性的完全舒展，「无政府主义」与「共产主义」二词便理当抗议这样的结合。针对这一暗指，我们将证明：个性唯有在共同体中才能发展；共同体唯有在个性自由展开时才能存在；二者彼此补足、互相成全。

===Original===
It is this diversity of problems to attack and to solve which has made the success of Anarchistic ideas, and contributed to their rapid growth; so much so that, launched forth by a group of unknown persons without means of propaganda, they today more or less invade art, science, and literature. Hatred of authority, social demands, date far back; they arise as soon as man is able to recognize that he is oppressed. But through how many phases and systems was it necessary that the idea should pass, before it could assume its present form!

One of the first who formulated it in its intuitional stage was Rabelais, in describing the life of the Abbey of Thelemes. But how obscure it still was! How little he believed it applicable to society in its entirety, since entrance into the community was reserved to a minority of privileged persons attended by a train of domestics attached to their person!

In 1793 the Anarchists were much talked of. Jacques Roux and “the madmen” appear to us to have been the ones who, during the Revolution, saw most clearly, and sought the best means of turning it to the benefit of the people. Hence the bourgeois historians have left them in the shade. Their history is still to be written; the documents buried in the archives and the libraries still await him who shall have the time and the courage to dig them up, bring them to light, and reveal the secret of things still very incomprehensible to us, in that tragic period of history. We can therefore scarcely form any appreciation of their program. One must come down to Proudhon before he sees Anarchy positing itself as the adversary of authority and power, and beginning to take definite shape. But as yet it is but a theoretical enemy; practically, Proudhon, in his social organization, leaves in existence, under different names, the administrative machinery which is the very essence of government. Up to the end of the empire Anarchy appears under the form of a vague mutualism, which, in France, during the first years that followed the Commune, foundered in the misled and misleading movement of cooperative associations for production and consumption. But before coming to this impotent solution, a sprout had detached itself from the springing tree. In Switzerland, the International had given birth to the “Jurassian Federation” in which Bakunin propagated the idea of Proudhon﻿—Anarchy the enemy of authority﻿—but developing, enlarging, incarnating it in social demands. From this epoch dates the true dawn of the present Anarchist movement.

===Chinese===
正是这种有待攻击和解决的问题的多样性，成就了无政府主义思想的成功，促成了它的迅速成长；以至于，它虽由一群没有任何宣传手段的无名之辈发端，如今却已或多或少地渗入艺术、科学与文学之中。对权威的憎恨、对社会的诉求，其来久远；人一旦能够认识到自己遭受压迫，它们便随之而起。然而，这一思想在具备今日的形态之前，还须经过多少阶段、多少体系！

在这一思想的直觉阶段，最早将其表述出来的人中，便有拉伯雷（Rabelais），他描绘了德廉美修道院（Abbey of Thelemes）中的生活。然而这思想当时何等朦胧！他何等不相信它能适用于整个社会——须知，进入那个共同体的资格，只保留给少数特权者，而这些特权者身后还跟着一批依附于其人的家仆！

1793 年，无政府主义者曾广为时人谈论。在我们看来，雅克·鲁（Jacques Roux）和「疯子们」（“the madmen”）正是大革命期间看得最清楚的人，也正是最着力寻找使大革命造福于人民的最佳手段的人。因此，资产阶级（bourgeois）历史学家把他们弃置在阴影之中。他们的历史尚有待书写；深埋在档案馆和图书馆中的文献，仍在等待那个有时间和勇气把它们发掘出来、公之于世、并揭示历史上那个悲剧时期中至今仍极令我们费解之事的隐秘的人。因此，我们几乎无法对他们的纲领作出任何评价。要一直下溯到蒲鲁东（Proudhon），人们才看到无政府主义以权威与权力的敌手自任，并开始具备确定的形态。但此时它还只是一个理论上的敌人；实际上，蒲鲁东在他的社会组织方案里，只是改换名目，便把那套构成政府之本质的行政机器保留了下来。直到帝国终结，无政府主义都以一种含糊的互助主义（mutualism）的形态出现；在法国，公社（the Commune）之后的最初几年间，这种互助主义在生产与消费合作协会那场既受人蒙蔽又蒙蔽他人的运动中归于失败。但在走到这个软弱无力的解决方案之前，一株新芽已经从那棵拔节而上的大树分离了出去。在瑞士，国际（the International）孕育出了「汝拉联合会」（“Jurassian Federation”），巴枯宁（Bakunin）在其中传播蒲鲁东的思想——无政府主义，权威之敌——只是对它加以发展、扩充，并使它体现于种种社会诉求之中。当今无政府主义运动的真正黎明，正是从那个时代开始的。

===Original===
Certainly many prejudices still existed, many illogical notions appeared in the ideas promulgated. The propagandist organization still contained many of the germs of authoritarianism; many of the elements of the authoritarian conception still survived. But what of it? The movement was launched; the idea grew, purified itself, and became more and more defined. And when, not quite thirteen years ago, Anarchy was affirmed at the Congres du Centre, in France, though still very feeble, though the act of a very weak minority (having against it not only those satisfied with the present social order, but also those pseudo-revolutionaries who only see in popular demands a means of grasping at power) the idea contained sufficient expansive force to take root without any other means of propaganda than the fervor of its adherents. It had sufficient vigor to induce the supporters of the capitalistic regime to injure and persecute it, and men of good faith to discuss it﻿—a proof of strength and vitality. Hence, in spite of the crusade of those who could consider themselves, in some degree, leaders of any of the divers divisions of public opinion, in spite of calumnies, excommunications, condemnations, in spite of the prison, the idea of Anarchy has made headway. Groups have been founded, propagandist organs have been created in France, Belgium, Italy, Spain, Portugal, Holland, England, Norway, America, Australia, in the Slavic tongue, in German, Jewish, Czech, Armenian﻿—a little everywhere and in all idioms. But what is more important, from the little group of malcontents by whom they were formulated, Anarchistic ideas have radiated through all classes of society. Wherever man displays his cerebral activity they have infiltrated. Art, science, literature, are impregnated with the new ideas and serve as vehicles for them. These ideas commenced at first in unconscious formulas, in illy-defined aspirations, more often caprices than real convictions. Today not only are Anarchistic aspirations formulated, but men know that it is Anarchy they are spreading, and boldly place the label on it.

The Anarchists are not, then, the only ones to find that things are bad, and to desire a change. These complaints, these aspirations, are expressed by the very persons who believe themselves defenders of the capitalistic order. Still more, men begin to feel that they ought not to confine themselves to sterile desires, but should work for the realization of what they demand. They begin to understand and proclaim action, propaganda by deed; that is to say, that having made the comparison between the pleasure which the satisfaction of acting as one thinks is bound to bring, and the annoyances which one must endure for the violation of a social law, they try, more and more, to conform their life to their manner of conceiving things, according to the degree of resistance which the particular temperament may offer to the harassments of social prosecution.

That Anarchistic ideas have been able to develop with this energy and rapidity, is because, while running counter to accepted opinions and established prejudices, while frightening, at the first exposition, those to whom they were addressed, they were answers, on the other hand, to their secret sentiments, their undefined aspirations. They offered to humanity, in a concrete form, that ideal of well-being and liberty which it had scarcely dared to outline in its hopeful dreams. At first they frightened their opponents, because they preached hatred or contempt of a number of institutions believed to be necessary to the life of society; because they demonstrated, contrary to received ideas, that these institutions are bad in the very essence, and not because entrusted to the hands of weak or wicked individuals. They taught the people that not only must the latter not be contented with changing the persons in power, with partially modifying the institutions which govern them, but that, before all, the people must destroy that which makes men bad, which makes it possible for a minority to make use of social forces to oppress the majority; they taught that what until now has been taken for the cause of the evils from which humanity suffers, is but the effect of a still more profound evil, that it is the very basis of society which must be attacked. Now, we have observed in the beginning that the basis of society is private property. Authority has but one excuse for existence: the defense of capital. Bureaucracy, the family, the army, the magistracy, flow directly from private property. The work of the Anarchists, then, has been to demonstrate the iniquity of the monopoly of the soil and of the fruits of the labor of past generations, by an idle minority; to sap authority by showing it to be detrimental to human development, by exposing it in its role of protector to the privileged, by proving the emptiness of the principles under cover of which it justifies its institutions.

===Chinese===
诚然，那时还存在许多偏见，所传播的观念中也出现许多不合逻辑的见解。宣传组织内部仍带着许多权威主义（authoritarianism）的胚芽；权威主义观念的许多要素依然存留。可这又有什么要紧？运动已然发动；思想在生长，在自我纯化，并且日益明确。而当将近十三年前无政府主义在法国的中央大会（Congres du Centre）上得到公开申明之时，它虽然仍十分孱弱，虽然只是极少数弱小者的作为（与它为敌的，不仅有满足于现存社会秩序的人，还有那些只把民众的诉求当作攫取权力之手段的伪革命者），这一思想却蕴含着足够的扩张力量，除信奉者的热忱之外再无别的宣传手段，便扎下了根。它有足够的力量，促使资本主义制度（capitalistic regime）的拥护者去伤害它、迫害它，也促使心怀诚意的人去讨论它——这正是力量与生命力的明证。因此，尽管那些自认为在某种程度上统领着舆论各派的人发动了讨伐，尽管有诽谤、逐出教门、判罪，尽管有监狱，无政府主义思想仍旧节节推进。在法国、比利时、意大利、西班牙、葡萄牙、荷兰、英国、挪威、美国、澳大利亚，在斯拉夫语中，在德语、犹太语、捷克语、亚美尼亚语中，团体纷纷建立，宣传刊物纷纷创办——几乎无处不在，无一种语言不有。而更重要的是，无政府主义思想已经从当初阐述它们的那个小小的不平者群体，辐射到社会的各个阶级。凡有人施展脑力活动之处，它们便已渗入。艺术、科学、文学都浸染了这些新思想，并成为它们的载体。这些思想起初表现为无意识的公式、轮廓模糊的向往，与其说是真正的信念，不如说是一时兴致。今天，无政府主义的向往不仅得到了明确的阐述，而且人们知道自己传播的正是无政府主义，并大胆地给它贴上了这个标签。

可见，发现世道败坏并渴望变革的，并非只有无政府主义者。这等怨诉、这等向往，恰恰出自那些自命为资本主义秩序捍卫者的人之口。更有甚者，人们开始感到，不应把自己局限于无所结果的愿望，而应为自己所要求的东西的实现而工作。他们开始理解并宣扬行动，宣扬「行动宣传」（propaganda by deed）；这就是说，当人们在按己意行事的满足所带来的快意，与违反一条社会法律必须忍受的种种烦扰之间作了权衡之后，便愈来愈力求——视各自的气质对社会追究的骚扰所能提供的抵抗力而定——使自己的生活契合于自己对事物的见解。

无政府主义思想之所以能以这样的力量和速度发展，是因为：它们虽然逆公认的意见和既成的偏见而行，虽然在初次陈述时令其受众惊惧，但另一方面，它们恰恰是对人们隐秘的情感、模糊的向往的回答。它们以具体的形式，向人类提供了那个它在满怀希望的梦境中几乎不敢勾画的福祉与自由的理想。起初它们令对手们惧怕，因为它们鼓吹憎恨或蔑视若干被公认为社会生存所必需的制度；因为它们与流行的观念相反，证明了这些制度就其本质而言即是恶的，而并非只因落入软弱或邪恶的个人之手才变成这样。它们教导人民：人民不仅不应满足于更换当权的人物、局部修改统治自己的制度，而且首先必须摧毁那使人变坏的东西，摧毁那使少数人得以利用社会力量压迫多数人的东西；它们教导说，迄今为止被当作人类所受种种祸害之原因的东西，其实只是一种更深刻的祸害的结果，必须攻击的恰恰是社会的根基本身。而我们在开篇已经看到，社会的根基就是私有制。权威的存在只有一个借口：保卫资本。官僚体制、家庭、军队、司法当局，皆直接源出于私有制。因此，无政府主义者所做的工作，便是揭露一个游手好闲的少数人对土地以及对以往各代人的劳动果实的垄断（monopoly）是何等不义；便是通过揭示权威有损于人类的发展、揭露其充当特权者保护者的角色、证明其借以使种种制度正当化的原则之空洞，来掘毁权威的根基。

===Original===
That which contributed to alienate the ambitious and intriguing from Anarchistic teachings was just that which led the thinker to study them, to question himself as to their message, viz., that they offered no place to personal preoccupations or paltry ambitions, and could in no way serve as a stepping-stone to those who see, in the demands of the workers, nothing but a means of carving themselves a position among the exploiters. The butterflies of politics have nothing to do in the ranks of the Anarchists. Little or no chance for petty personal vanities, no procession of candidatures opening a career to all kinds of hopes, and all sorts of recantations. In the political and authoritarian Socialist parties an ambitious person can bring about his “conversion” by insensible degrees; no one perceives that he has changed till long after the conversion is accomplished. Among the Anarchists this is impossible, because he who would consent to accept any office whatever in the present society, after having demonstrated that all those who are in office cannot remain there except on condition that they become defenders of the existing system, would at the same moment incur the epithet of renegade, for he could have no semblance of a reason to justify “evolution.” Thus that which provoked the hatred of intriguers at the same time awakened the spirit of investigation in honest men; and this explains the rapid progress of the Anarchistic idea.

What reply, indeed, can be made to those who prove to you that if you want a thing well done you must do it yourself, and delegate it to nobody? With what can you reproach those who make you see that if you wish to be free, you must commission no one to “direct” you? What answer is there to those who show you the causes of the ills from which you suffer, indicate the remedy to you, but do not make themselves the dispensers of it﻿—on the contrary, taking care to make people understand that the individual alone is able to comprehend his own needs, is the judge of what he should avoid?

Ideas strong enough to inspire men with a conviction which makes them fight and suffer for the propagation thereof, without expecting anything directly from those ideas, were, in the eyes of sincere men, worth being studied; and that is what has happened. Hence without heeding the clamors of some, the rancor of others, the attempts of governments, the idea grows and progresses without cessation, proving to the bourgeoisie that the truth can neither be suppressed nor silenced. Sooner or later it will be reckoned with.

===Chinese===
使野心家和钻营之徒疏远无政府主义学说的那个原因，恰恰正是促使思想者去研究它、叩问其讯息的原因，那就是：这些学说不给个人的盘算和卑微的野心留任何位置，绝不能充当踏脚石，供那些把工人的诉求仅仅看作在剥削者中间为自己谋一个位置的手段的人所用。政治场上的蝴蝶们在无政府主义者的行列里无事可做。这里几乎没有让渺小的个人虚荣心施展的机会，没有一连串的候选提名向各式各样的希望、各式各样的改宗变节敞开生涯之门。在各政治的、权威主义的社会主义政党里，野心家可以不知不觉地分几步完成自己的「改宗」；直到改宗完成许久之后，才有人察觉他已经变了。在无政府主义者中间，这是不可能的：因为谁若在证明了凡在职者唯有变成现存制度的捍卫者才能留任之后，竟还同意在现今社会中接受任何职位，他就会在那一刻背上变节者的骂名，因为他再也找不出一丝一毫貌似有理的借口来为这种「演变」辩护。于是，激起钻营者之仇恨的东西，同时唤醒了诚实的人的探究精神；这便解释了无政府主义思想的迅速进展。

确实，对于那些向你证明「要想把一件事做好，就必须亲自去做、不委托任何人」的人，能作出什么回答呢？对于那些使你看到「你若想自由，就不得委托任何人来『指挥』你」的人，又能拿什么去指摘呢？对于那些向你指出你所受苦难的种种原因、为你指明疗救之方、却并不让自己充当药方分发者的人——相反，还悉心使人明白，唯有个人自己才能理解自己的需要、才能裁断何者当避——又有什么答案可给呢？

一种思想若强大到足以唤起人的信念，使人肯为它的传播而战斗、而受苦，却不指望从这些思想中直接得到任何东西，那么在真诚的人看来，它便值得研究——事情也正是如此发生的。于是，不顾某些人的鼓噪、另一些人的怨毒、各国政府的种种企图，这一思想不停息地生长着、进展着，向资产阶级证明：真理既压制不住，也封锁不了。或迟或早，人们将不得不认真对待它。

===Original===
Anarchy has its victims﻿—its dead, its imprisoned, its exiled; but it remains alive and strong, the number of its propagandists constantly increasing;﻿—propagandists conscious of their acts, because they have understood all the beauty of the conception; accidental propagandists also, content with hurling their cries of hate against the institution which has clashed most with their secret sentiments and their instincts of justice and truth.

By its amplitude the Anarchistic idea shelters and draws to it all those who have the feeling of personal dignity, the thirst after the beautiful and true. Should not the ideal of man be released from all fetters, all constraint? Have not all the divers revolutions he has wrought, pursued this end? If he still submits to the authority of his exploiters, if the human mind still struggles under the pressure of the vulgarities of capitalistic society, it is because accepted ideas, routine, prejudice, and ignorance, have been till now stronger than its dreams and desires for emancipation, precipitating it, after it has driven away the reigning masters, into a fresh abandonment of them, at the very moment it expects to free itself. Anarchistic ideas have come to bring light into the minds not only of the workers, but also of thinkers of every category; helping them to analyze their own feelings, stripping bare the causes of misery and indicating the means of destroying them, showing to all the route to follow and the end to be attained, explaining why previous revolutions have been abortive. It is this close relation with the secret sentiments of individuals which explains their rapid extension, which gives them their strength and renders them irrepressible. Governmental fury, oppressive measures, the rage of frustrated ambition, may set themselves against the ideas and their propagators; today the opening is made; they can no longer be prevented from making headway, from becoming the ideal of the disinherited, the motors of their attempts at emancipation.

Capitalistic society is so paltry, so narrow; large aspirations find themselves so cramped in it; it annihilates so many good intents, so many hopes, crushing and killing so many individualities that cannot stoop to its narrow views, that, could it succeed in stifling the voice of every living Anarchist, its oppressions would raise up new ones, equally implacable.

===Chinese===
无政府主义有自己的牺牲者——死者、被囚者、流亡者；但它依然活着，依然坚强，其宣传者的数目不断增长——既有明白自己所作所为的宣传者，因为他们已经领会了这一观念全部的美；也有偶然的宣传者，他们只满足于向那个与其隐秘情感、与其正义和真理的本能冲突最烈的制度，高声喊出自己的仇恨。

凭着它的宏阔，无政府主义思想庇护并吸引着所有那些怀有个人尊严感、渴求美与真的人。人的理想难道不该挣脱一切枷锁、一切强制吗？他所成就的历次形形色色的革命，难道不都在追求这个目的吗？如果他至今仍屈从于剥削者的权威，如果人的头脑至今仍在资本主义社会的庸俗重压下挣扎，那是因为因袭的观念、惯例、偏见与无知，至今仍比人解放的梦想与渴望更强大，以至于在人赶走了当令的主子之后，恰在他指望解放自己的那一刻，又使人把这些梦想与渴望重新抛弃。无政府主义思想的到来，是要把光明带进不仅是工人的头脑，而且是一切门类的思想者的头脑；帮助他们剖析自己的情感，剥露苦难的根源并指出消灭苦难的手段，向所有人指明应循的道路与应达的目标，解释以往的革命何以尽数流产。正是这种与个人隐秘情感的紧密关联，解释了它们何以迅速传布，赋予了它们力量，并使它们不可遏止。政府的暴怒、高压的措施、野心受挫的狂恨，尽可以同这些思想及其传播者为敌；然而今天，缺口已经打开；再没有什么能阻止它们前进，阻止它们成为被剥夺者的理想，成为被剥夺者尝试解放的动力。

资本主义社会是如此鄙陋、如此狭隘；宏大的向往在其中是如此局促难伸；它扼杀多少善良的意图、多少希望，碾碎、毁灭多少不肯俯就其狭隘见识的个性——以至于，纵使它能扼杀每一个在世的无政府主义者的声音，它的压迫也只会唤起同样绝不妥协的新的无政府主义者。

## II Individualism and Solidarity / II 个人主义与团结

===Original===
Individualism and Solidarity

“Anarchy and Communism protest against being coupled together,” declared certain dishonest adversaries, little anxious to throw light upon the question. “Communism is an organization; an organization prevents the development of individuality;﻿—we will have none of it!” “We are individualists, we are Anarchists; nothing more!” exclaimed after them certain persons, sincere in the sense that they desired to appear more advanced than all their comrades, and having no originality their own. They entangled themselves in exaggerations, pushing the ideas to absurdity; and around them collected those whom the governing class has an interest in introducing among its adversaries, to divide or mislead them.

Now, behold those Anarchists launched into discussions Anarchy, Communism, the initiative, organization; the harmful or useful influence of groups; egoism and altruism; in fine, lot of things one more absurd than the other. For, after being thoroughly discussed by honest opponents, the end of it is that all want the same thing, though calling it by different names. As a matter of fact the Anarchists who demand Communism are the first to recognize that the individual has not been put into the world for society’s sake; that, on the contrary, the latter has been formed solely for the purpose of furnishing the former greater facility for evolution. It is quite plain that when a certain number of persons group together and unite their forces, they have in view the obtaining of a greater sum of enjoyment with a less expenditure of energy. In nowise have they the intention of sacrificing their initiative, their will, their individuality, for the benefit of an entity which did not exist before their union, which will disappear with their dispersion. To economize their forces while continuing to wrest from nature the things necessary to their existence, and which they could not obtain but by the concentration of their efforts, was certainly the motive which guided those human beings who first commenced to group themselves; or what, at least, must have been tacitly understood as such, if not completely reasoned out in their primitive associations, which associations might well be, even had to be, temporary and limited to the duration of the effort, falling apart when the result was once attained.

No Anarchist, therefore, thinks of subordinating the existence of the individual to the progress of society. Freedom of the people, complete freedom in all their modes of action, is all we ask. And if there be those who repudiate organization, who swear by the individual alone, who say that they despise the community, declaring that the egoism of the individual should be his only rule of conduct, and that the adoration of his ego should come before and above all humanitarian considerations﻿—believing themselves to be therein more advanced than others﻿—such people can never have studied the psychological and physiological nature of man, never have given themselves an account of their own feelings; they have no idea of what constitutes the real life of man, its physical, moral, and intellectual needs.

===Chinese===
个人主义与团结

「无政府主义（Anarchy）与共产主义（Communism）抗议被人相提并论」，某些不诚实的论敌如此宣称，他们全无意于把问题弄个明白。「共产主义是一种组织；组织妨碍个性的发展；——我们不要这种东西！」「我们是个人主义者（individualists），我们是无政府主义者（Anarchists）；仅此而已！」随后另有一些人跟着叫嚷，这些人所谓真诚，是说他们一心想显得比所有同志都更先进，自己却毫无独创之见。他们陷在夸张里，把种种观念推向荒谬；而聚拢在他们周围的，则是统治阶级为了分裂或迷惑其反对者、而有兴趣往反对者中间安插的那些人。

于是，看哪，那些无政府主义者一头扎进了种种讨论：无政府主义、共产主义、主动性（initiative）、组织；团体的有害或有益的影响；利己主义（egoism）与利他主义（altruism）；总之，一大堆一个比一个荒唐的东西。因为这些题目一经诚实论者的彻底讨论，结局无非是：人人想要的都是同一件东西，只是叫法不同罢了。事实上，主张共产主义的无政府主义者最先承认：个人并不是为了社会才被安置到世上来的；恰恰相反，社会的形成仅仅是为了给个人提供更大的进化便利。十分显然，当一定数目的人结成群体、联合其力量时，他们心目中所图的，是以较少的精力支出换取较大总量的享受。他们绝无意思为了一个在其联合之前并不存在、又将随其离散而消失的实体，去牺牲自己的主动性、自己的意志、自己的个性。一面继续向自然攫取生存所必需、而唯有集中努力才能取得的东西，一面节省自身的力量——这肯定就是引导最初开始结群的人们的那种动机；或者说，至少是他们在原始联合中纵使未曾充分推究、也必定默然认作如此的动机；那些联合尽可以是、甚至必定是临时性的，以努力持续的期间为限，结果一旦取得便告解体。

因此，没有任何一个无政府主义者想到要把个人的存在从属于社会的进步。人民的自由，一切行动方式上的完全自由，便是我们所要求的全部。倘若真有这样的人：他们排斥组织，唯孤立的个人是信，自称鄙夷共同体，宣称个人的利己主义应当是其唯一的行为准则，对自我的崇拜应当先于并高于一切人道考量——还自以为在此点上比别人先进——那么，这种人必定从未研究过人的心理与生理本性，从未把自己的感情好好检视一番；对于什么东西构成人的真实生活、构成其身体的、道德的和智力的需要，他们全然没有概念。

===Original===
Our present society exhibits some of these perfect egoists: the Delobelles, the Hjalmar Eikdals, are not rare; they are found not only in romances. Without meeting any great number of them, it is sometimes given to us to run up against these types who think only of themselves, who see nothing in life but their own persons. If there is a tempting bit on the table they appropriate it without scruple. They live largely outside while their folks at home are dying of hunger. They accept the sacrifices of all who surround them﻿—father, mother, wife, children﻿—as their due, while they shamelessly put on dignified airs and take their ease. The sufferings of others are not counted, provided that their own existence runs smoothly. Still worse, they do not even perceive that others suffer for them and through them. When they are fed and well-disposed, humanity is satisfied and refreshed! Behold the type of your perfect egoist in the absolute sense of the word! But we may also add it is the type of a very sorry individual. The most repugnant bourgeois does not even approach this type; he, at times, still has love for his own people, or at least something akin to it which takes its place. We do not believe that the sincere partisans of the most exaggerated individualism have ever had the intention of giving us this type as the ideal of future humanity; no more than the Communist-Anarchists have meant to preach abnegation and renunciation for the individual in the society which they anticipate. Disclaiming the entity “society,” they equally disclaim that other entity, the “individual,” which those who have carried the theory to absurdity tend to create.

The individual has a right to his entire liberty, to the satisfaction of all his needs; that is understood. Only, as there exist more than a billion of individuals on the earth, with equal rights if not with equal needs, it follows that all these rights must be satisfied without encroaching on one another; otherwise that would be oppression, which would render the success of the revolution futile.

What tends greatly to befog our ideas is that this adulterate society which governs us, based upon the antagonism of interests, has made people prey upon one another, and forces them to tear each other to pieces in order to secure to themselves the possibility of living. In the existing society one must be either robber or robbed; there is no middle way. Today the one who helps a neighbor runs great risk of being duped; hence the belief, among those who do not reason, that men cannot live without fighting each other. The Anarchists, however, say that society should be based on the strictest solidarity. In that society which they wish to realize, it must not be that individual happiness, were it only in its very least important division, be attained at the expense of another individual. Personal well-being must flow from the general well-being; when an individual feels himself injured in his autonomy or in his belongings, all other individuals must feel the same injury in order that they may remedy it. So long as this ideal is not realized, so long as this goal is not reached, societies will be but arbitrary organizations, against which persons who feel themselves wronged will have the right to revolt.

===Chinese===
我们现今的社会里就陈列着若干这类完美的利己主义者：德洛贝尔们（Delobelles）、雅尔马·艾克达尔们（Hjalmar Eikdals）并不罕见，他们并非只出现在小说里。这种人我们遇不到多少，但有时也有缘撞见这一类型：他们只想着自己，一生中除了自身什么都看不见。桌上有一块诱人的美味，他们便毫无顾忌地据为己有。他们大半在外过活，家里的人却在挨饿。他们把周围一切人的牺牲——父亲、母亲、妻子、儿女——当作自己应得之分收下，一面还厚颜无耻地摆出体面架子、安享清福。只要自己的日子过得顺遂，旁人的痛苦一概不计较。更糟的是，他们甚至觉察不到别人正为他们、因他们而受苦。他们吃饱了、心情舒泰了，人类也就餍足而畅快了！看哪，这就是字面绝对意义上的完美利己主义者的典型！但我们还可以补一句：这也是一种非常可悲的个人。连最令人厌恶的资产者（bourgeois）都远远够不上这种典型；他有时到底还爱自己的亲人，或者至少还有一种近似之物顶替着爱的位置。我们不相信，最夸张的个人主义的真诚信徒曾有意把这个典型当作未来人类的理想捧给我们；正如无政府共产主义者（Communist-Anarchists）并不曾打算在他们所预期的社会中向个人布讲自我克弃与弃绝。他们既拒斥「社会」这一实体，也同样拒斥另一个实体——「个人」，即那些把理论推到荒谬地步的人所倾向于制造出来的那个「个人」。

个人有权享有其全部自由，有权满足其一切需要；这是不言而喻的。只是，既然地球上存在着十多亿个人，需要纵不相等，权利却彼此平等，那么随之而来的便是：所有这些权利都必须在互不侵越的条件下得到满足；否则那就是压迫，而压迫将使革命的胜利归于徒劳。

大大搅浑我们观念的，是这样一件事：这个统治着我们、建立在利益对立之上的掺假社会，使人与人互为猎物，强迫人们为了给自己保住一线生存的可能而彼此撕咬。在现存社会里，人不做掠夺者便做被掠夺者；没有中间道路。今天，帮助邻人的人大有被骗之险；于是在那些不肯动脑筋的人中间，便生出了一种信念，以为人们不互相争斗便不能生活。然而无政府主义者却说，社会应当建立在最严格的团结（solidarity）之上。在他们想要实现的那种社会里，个人的幸福——哪怕只就其最无关紧要的一个方面而言——也决不容以牺牲另一个个人为代价而求得。个人的福祉必须从普遍的福祉中流出来；当一个个人感到自己的自主性或财物受了侵害，所有其他个人都必须感到同样的侵害，以便加以救治。只要这个理想未曾实现，只要这个目标未曾达到，种种社会便不过是专断的组织，凡感到自己受了冤屈的人都有权起而反抗它们。

===Original===
If men could live isolated, if they could return to the state of nature, there would be no discussion as to how they should live: each would live as he pleased. The earth is big enough to accommodate everybody. But would the earth, if left to itself, furnish sufficient for all to live upon? This is less certain. It would probably mean ferocious war between individuals, the “struggle for existence” of the early ages, in all its fury. It would be the cycle of evolution already run through and recommencing﻿—the stronger oppressing the weaker until superseded by the cunning, until money-value should displace force-value. If we have had to traverse this period of blood, of misery and exploitation, which is called the history of humanity, it is because man has been egoistic in the absolute sense of the word, without any corrective, without any mitigation. He has had in view from the outset of all his associations, nothing but the satisfaction of his immediate desires. Whenever he has been able to enslave a weaker fellow he has done so without scruple, seeing only the amount of work to be got out of the victim, without reflecting that the necessity of surveillance, the revolts he will have to suppress, will end, in the long run, in compelling him to perform an equally onerous labor, and that it would be better to work side by side, lending each other mutual aid.

It is thus that authority and property have succeeded in establishing themselves. Now, if we wish to overturn them, it cannot be done by beginning our past evolution over again. If this theory that the motive of the individual should be egoism pure and simple﻿—the adoration and culture of the ego﻿—were admitted, one would necessarily declare that the individual should launch into the melee and work to gain the means of self-gratification without concerning himself as to whether he crushes others at his side. To affirm this would be to confess that the coming revolution should be made by and for the strong, that the new society must be a perpetual conflict between individuals. If it were so we should have no reason to proclaim the idea of general enfranchisement. We should rebel against the existing society only because its capitalistic organization did not permit us likewise to possess.

It may be that among those calling themselves Anarchists there are some who regard the question from this standpoint. This would explain to us the defections and recantations of persons who, after having been most ardent, have deserted their principles to range themselves among the defenders of the existing society, because it offered them compensations. Certainly we do combat this society because it does not afford us satisfaction for all our aspirations; but we also comprehend that our own interests, rightly understood, would have this satisfaction of our needs extended to all the members of society. Man is always egoistic, he always tends to make of his ego the centre of the universe. But with the development of his intelligence he comes to understand that if his ego wishes to be satisfied there are other egos that equally wish to be satisfied, (those that have not been have made it understood that they had a right to be) whence sentimentalists and mystics have come to preach renunciation, sacrifice, devotion to one’s neighbor.

===Chinese===
倘若人能够离群索居地生活，倘若人能够退回自然状态（state of nature），那么关于人应当怎样生活，本不会有任何争论：各人爱怎么活就怎么活。地球大得很，容得下所有的人。可是，地球若任其自然，能否供给人人以生活之资？这就没有那么确定了。那多半意味着个人之间的凶残战争，意味着远古时代那种狂态毕露的「生存斗争」（struggle for existence）。那将是已经走完一遍又重新开始的演化循环——强者压迫弱者，直到被狡黠者取而代之，直到金钱价值取代强力价值。我们之所以不得不穿过这段名为人类历史的血污、苦难与剥削的时期，是因为人向来是绝对字义上的利己者，没有任何矫正，没有任何缓和。在他一切联合的起点上，他所悬的目标无非是眼前欲望的满足。每当他能够奴役一个较弱小的同类，他都毫无顾忌地做了，眼里只看见能从受害者身上榨出的劳动量，却不曾想到：监视的必要、他日后必须镇压的暴动，终将逼他付出同样繁重的劳动，而肩并肩地劳作、彼此互助（mutual aid）本来要好得多。

权威（authority）与私有制（property）正是这样确立起来的。如今我们若想推翻它们，却不能靠把我们过去的演化从头再走一遍。倘若承认这样一条理论——个人的动机应当是纯而又纯的利己主义，即对自我的崇拜与培植——那就必然等于宣告：个人应当投身混战，为攫取自我满足的手段而卖力，全然不顾是否把身旁的人踩在脚下。肯定这一点，就等于承认：行将到来的革命应当由强人为强人而做，新社会必须是个人之间的永恒冲突。倘若当真如此，我们便再没有理由宣告普遍解放（general enfranchisement）的思想了；我们反抗现存社会，便只是因为它的资本主义组织不曾容我们同样地去占有了。

在那些自称无政府主义者的人中间，也许有一些人正是从这个立足点来看问题的。这便向我们解释了某些人的叛逃与变节：他们一度最为炽烈，随后却抛下自己的原则，投到现存社会的辩护者行列里去，因为现存社会给了他们补偿。固然，我们确实是在同这个社会作战，因为它不能满足我们的一切抱负；但我们也明白，我们自身的利益——若加以正确的理解——恰恰要求把这种需要的满足扩展到社会的一切成员身上。人永远是利己的，永远倾向于把自己的自我当成宇宙的中心。然而，随着智力的发展，他渐渐懂得：既然他的自我要求满足，便还有别的自我同样要求满足（那些未曾得到满足的自我，已经使人懂得它们有权得到满足），感伤主义者与神秘主义者正是由此而来，开始宣讲弃绝、牺牲与对邻人的奉献。

===Original===
Social authority, while continuing to preach the oppression of the individual for the sake of the collectivity﻿—this dogma has contributed to its maintenance even as much as force has﻿—social authority has had to modify itself, to concede a larger share to individuality. For if narrow, badly understood egoism is opposed to the functioning of society, renunciation and the spirit of sacrifice are fatal to individuality. To sacrifice oneself for others, above all when they are indifferent to you, does not enter into everyone’s disposition. And besides it would, in the long run, be even prejudicial to humanity, for it would allow narrow minds, egoistic in the bad sense of the word, to rule; that type of humanity farthest from perfect would come to absorb the others. Altruism, properly so-called, could not, therefore, take root either.

But though egoism or altruism, separately, each pushed to its extreme, is pernicious to the individual and society, united they are resolved into a third term, which is the law of future societies. This law is **solidarity**.

Many of us will combine with the intention of realizing one of our aspirations. This association having nothing forced in it, nothing arbitrary, prompted only by some need of our being, it is quite evident that the more pressing the need the more force and activity shall we contribute to the association. All having cooperated in production, we shall all have rights in consumption; that is plain; but as the sum of needs will have been calculated (counting in those which must be foreseen) that the satisfaction of all may be attained, solidarity will have no trouble in securing to each his share. Is it not said that man’s nature is to have his eyes bigger than his stomach? Now, the more intense his desire is the greater an amount of activity will he devote to its realization. Thus he will come to produce not only sufficient to satisfy the co-participants, but also those in whom desire would not have been awakened but for the sight of the thing produced. Man’s needs being infinite, infinite will be his means of satisfying them, and it is this variety of needs which will concur in the establishment of general harmony.

===Chinese===
社会权威一面继续宣讲为了集体而压迫个人——这条教条对它的维持所起的作用，竟与强力不相上下——一面又不得不自我修正，向个性让出更大的份额。因为，倘若狭隘的、被误解了的利己主义有碍于社会的运转，那么弃绝与牺牲精神之于个性便是致命的。为他人牺牲自己，尤其当那些人对你漠不关心的时候，并非人人禀性中都有的事。况且归根到底，这甚至对人类有害，因为它会让那些心胸狭隘、在坏字义上利己的人得势；那种离完善最远的人类类型，会把其余的类型都吸收掉。因此，就其本来意义而言的利他主义也同样无法扎根。

然而，利己主义也好，利他主义也好，各自孤立地推向极端，对个人和社会都是有害的；二者一经结合，便归结为第三个名词，即未来社会的法则。这个法则便是**团结**。

我们中间的许多人，将怀着实现某一抱负的意图联合起来。这种联合既无丝毫强迫，也无丝毫专断，仅仅由我们存在的某种需要所促成，因此十分显然：需要越是迫切，我们投给联合的力量与活动便越多。既然人人都协作参加了生产，人人在消费中便都有权利；这是明摆着的；而由于需要的总量届时已经核算（把必须预见的那些也一并计入），以求一切人的满足皆可实现，团结便不难保证人人各得其分。人们不是说，人的天性总是眼睛比胃口大吗？况且，他的欲望越是炽烈，他为求实现而投入的活动量便越大。于是，他生产出来的东西将不仅足以满足共同参与者，还足以满足那些若非见到产出之物便不会唤起欲望的人。人的需要既是无限的，满足需要的手段也将是无限的，而正是这种需要的多样性，将协力促成普遍和谐（general harmony）的建立。

===Original===
In our present society, wherein we are accustomed to depend upon the toil of others to obtain the things necessary for existence, there is but one object: to procure money enough to enable one to buy what he wants. Now, as manual labor does not even enable one to keep himself from starving, he who has only this resource, seeks to obtain money by every means except work, becoming an official, journalist, or whatnot, including blackmailer. He who has a start goes into commerce and increases his income by robbing his contemporaries; he gambles in stocks, he speculates, or makes others work for him. People engage in all sorts of occupations, more or less dishonorable, except the one thing necessary that all might have their share﻿—*useful production*. So that each one pulls the cover over himself without concerning himself about those whom he lays naked, whence this unreasoned egoism which seems to have become the sole motive of human actions.

But as man grows refined, he comes also to live not only for himself and in himself. The type of the humane egoist, perfectly developed, is to suffer with the sufferings of those who surround him, to have his enjoyment spoiled by the reflection that others, owing to the vicious social organization in which we live, may suffer by it. Among the bourgeoisie there are persons whose sensitiveness is certainly highly developed; when the influences of environment, education, or heredity, leave them leisure to reflect upon social misery and turpitude; when they reckon up their existence, they try as much as possible to remedy misery with charity. Whence, philanthropic works! But the habit of believing society normally constituted, the habit of considering poverty eternal, the result of the laborer’s misconduct, engenders an unfeeling character, inquisitorial in its philanthropy. Because for the man born, educated, brought up in the hothouses of wealth and luxury, it is very difficult, even impossible, save under exceptional circumstances, to come to doubt the legitimacy of the situation he occupies. For the parvenu it is still more difficult, for he believes he owes his situation to his talent and his work. Religion, conceit, and the economists, have so reiterated that work is a punishment, that poverty is the result of the improvidence of those who are a prey to it, that how can you expect him who has never had to struggle against adversity not to believe himself of a superior essence! From the day he begins to doubt it, sets himself to study the social organization, if he is sufficiently endowed to understand its viciousness, his pleasures will be poisoned at their fountainhead. This man will suffer when he says to himself that his luxury necessitates the misery of a mass of workers, that every one of his possessions is purchased at the expense of the sufferings of those who are sacrificed to produce them. If this man’s combativeness is developed equally with his sensitiveness he will make one more rebel against the social order which does not secure moral and intellectual satisfaction even to him. For it must not be forgotten that the social problem is not confined to a simple material question. We certainly do contend, and that before everything else, that all should have enough to eat. But our demands are not limited to this; we also contend that each should be able to develop himself according to his faculties, and to procure those intellectual gratifications which the needs of his brain create. True that for many Anarchists the question stops there; and that is what has brought about these divers interpretations and discussions of egoism, altruism, etc. Nothing more urgent than the stomach question! Only it would be dangerous to the success of the revolution to stop there, for then one might just as well accept the Socialistic State, which could, and would, secure all in the satisfaction of their physical needs.

If the next revolution were to confine its objects to the sole problem of material life, it would greatly risk being arrested on the way, degenerating into a vast revel of gluttony, which, the orgy once over, would not be long in surrendering the insurgent: to the blows of capitalistic reaction. Happily this problem, paramount today to the workingmen whose future is rendered uncertain by more and more prolonged periods of idleness, as we admit, is not the only one which will be solved in the next revolution. Without doubt the first work of the Anarchists towards making the revolution a success, will be to seize social wealth, to call upon the disinherited to take possession of stores, machinery, and the soil; to install themselves in healthy localities, destroying the rat-holes in which they are forced to remain today. The revolutionists should destroy all the old parchments which guarantee the functioning of property; the offices of bailiffs, notaries, register of land surveys, register of deeds, the entire civil staff, should be visited and “cleaned out.” But to do all this work something more than famishing people is needed﻿—individuals, conscious of their individuality, jealous of all their rights, determined to conquer them and capable of defending them once they are acquired. This is why a question of subsistence only would be powerless to effect such a transformation; and it is also why there rise up, together with the right to subsistence which the Anarchists demand, all these questions of art, science, and philosophy, which they are forced to study, to fathom, to elucidate, and which are the cause of Anarchistic ideas embracing every branch of human science. Everywhere have arguments in favor of these ideas been found, everywhere there have risen up adherents who furnished their quota of demands, and reinforced the principles with their special knowledge. The sum of human learning is so great that the most privileged brains can appropriate only a portion; likewise the conception of Anarchism though condensed by certain minds which outline its bases and trace its program, cannot be elucidated but by the collaboration of all, by the help of each one’s knowledge. And this it is which gives it its strength, for it is the collaboration of all which enables it to sum up all human aspirations.

===Chinese===
在我们现今这个社会里，我们惯于依靠他人的劳作来取得生存必需之物，于是便只剩下一个目标：弄到足够的钱，让人想买什么就买得起什么。可是，既然体力劳动连让人免于饿死都办不到，那么只剩这一条路的人，便设法用劳动之外的一切手段去弄钱：做官、当记者，或者干些别的什么名堂，包括敲诈勒索。有点底子的人则去经商，靠掠夺同时代的人来增加收入；他炒股票、搞投机，或者让别人替自己干活。人们钻营着五花八门的行当，多少都有些不体面，唯独不去干那件为使人人得其分内之份所必需的事——*有用的生产*（useful production）。于是人人都把被子往自己身上拉，全不顾被他掀得赤身露体的那些人，由此便生出了这种不加思索的利己主义，它似乎已经成为人类行动的唯一动机。

然而，随着人变得精致，他也就开始不仅为自己、不仅在自己之中活着。发展完备的仁厚的利己主义者，其典型便是：与周围人的痛苦同其痛苦，因想到别人——由于我们所生活的这一邪恶的社会组织——可能由此受苦，而使自己的享乐败了味。资产阶级（bourgeoisie）中间确有感受性高度发达的人；当环境、教育或遗传的影响给他们留下闲暇去思索社会的苦难与卑污时；当他们盘点自己的生存时，他们便尽力用慈善来补救苦难。于是有了种种慈善事业！然而，相信社会建制正常的习惯，把贫困视为永恒、视为劳动者品行不端之结果的习惯，养成了一种冷漠无情的性格，一种在其慈善中带着宗教裁判所气息的性格。因为，对于在财富与奢侈的温室里出生、受教育、长大的人来说，除非处于例外的境遇，要怀疑自己所占据地位的合法性是非常困难的，甚至是不可能的。对暴发户（parvenu）来说就更加困难，因为他相信自己的地位应归功于自己的才能与劳动。宗教、自负与经济学家们如此翻来覆去地断言：劳动是一种惩罚，贫困是身受其苦者不事远虑的结果——你又怎能指望一个从未同逆境搏斗过的人不相信自己属于更高等的本质呢！从他开始怀疑这一点的那个日子起，他便着手研究社会组织；倘若他的天赋足以理解其邪恶之处，他的种种享乐便会在源头处中毒。这个人对自己说，他的奢侈必然以一大批工人的贫困为代价，他的每一件财物都是以那些为生产它们而被牺牲的人的痛苦为代价买来的，这时他便会痛苦。倘若此人的斗志与其感受性同样发达，他便将成为反抗社会秩序的又一名叛逆者——这个秩序甚至连道德与智力的满足也不曾保障给他。因为不可忘记：社会问题并不限于单纯的物质问题。我们确实主张——而且首先主张——人人都有饭吃。但我们的要求不止于此；我们同样主张，每个人都应当能够依其禀赋发展自己，都能够获取其头脑的需要所催生的那些智力上的满足。诚然，对许多无政府主义者来说，问题到此为止；也正因为如此，才引起了关于利己主义、利他主义等等的种种解释与讨论。没有什么比肚子问题更紧迫的了！只是，在这个问题上止步不前，对革命的成功将是危险的，因为那样一来，人们尽可以接受社会主义国家（Socialistic State）——它能够、也愿意保障一切人的物质需要得到满足。

倘若下一次革命把目标局限于物质生活这唯一的问题，它便大有中途受挫之虞，堕落为一场大规模的暴食狂欢；狂欢既散，不出多久，它便会把起义者拱手交到资本主义反动势力的打击之下。所幸，正如我们所承认的，这个今日对工人至关紧要的问题——他们的前途因愈拖愈长的失业期而变得渺茫——并不是下一次革命中将要解决的唯一问题。毫无疑问，无政府主义者为使革命成功而要做的第一件事，便是夺取社会财富，号召被剥夺者（the disinherited）占有仓库、机器和土地；迁入有益健康的地段，捣毁他们今日被迫栖身的那些鼠窟。革命者应当销毁一切保证私有制运转的旧契据；执达吏、公证人、土地测量登记处、契约登记处，连同整个文职官僚班子，都应当被一一「光顾」并「清洗一空」。然而，要完成这一切工作，光有饥肠辘辘的人群是不够的——所需要的是这样的个人：意识到自己的个性，珍视自己的全部权利，决意去赢得这些权利，并且在赢得之后有能力保卫它们。正因为如此，单凭一个生存问题是无力于完成这样一场变革的；也正因为如此，与无政府主义者所要求的生存权一道，才兴起了艺术、科学、哲学的种种问题——他们不得不去研究、去穷究、去阐明的问题；而正是这些问题，造成了无政府主义思想囊括人类科学每一分支的局面。处处都找到了有利于这些思想的论据，处处都站出了以各自的诉求充实其份额、以其专门知识巩固原则的拥护者。人类知识的总和如此浩大，最得天独厚的头脑也只能占有其中一部分；同样，无政府主义（Anarchism）的构想虽然经某些勾勒其基础、描画其纲领的头脑加以凝练，却唯有靠众人的协作、靠每一个人凭其知识的助力，才能得到阐明。而正是这一点赋予它力量，因为正是众人的协作，使它得以总括人类的一切抱负。

## III Too Abstract / III 过于抽象

===Original===

“You are too abstract!” This is an objection frequently raised against the Anarchists by many people. They say that since we address ourselves to the workers we should make more fruitful propaganda if we should take up less elevated subjects. By the preceding chapters we have seen that it is the development of the ideas themselves which has drawn us into the treatment of questions not always within the scope of those whom we address; this is a fatality to which we submit and against which we can do nothing. To those who are just beginning to nibble at the social question our writings may often appear dry; this we do not deny. But can we alter the fact that the questions which we treat and which must be treated, are dry in themselves? Can we prevent the principles which we defend, linked together as they are, identified with every branch of human knowledge, from leading those who wish to elucidate them to study things they did not before deem necessary? And, moreover, has not all this preparatory work to which they would condemn us, been already performed by our predecessors, the Socialists? Do not the capitalistic classes themselves work for the demolition of their society? Are not all ambitious radicals, Socialists more or less deeply dyed, bent upon demonstrating to the workers that the present society can do nothing for them; that it must be changed?

The Anarchists therefore have only to analyze this enormous work, to coordinate it, to extract its essence. Their role is limited to proving that it is not by changing governors that the ills from which we suffer may be cured; that it is not by merely modifying the machinery of the social organism that we shall prevent it from producing those evil effects which the very bourgeois, desirous of getting into power, knows so well how to show up. But our task is complicated precisely because the ideas which we advocate are abstract. If, indeed, we were willing to content ourselves with declamations and assertions, the task would be rendered easy, both for us and for our readers. The more difficult the problems to be solved the more need is there to acquaint ourselves with arguments and logic. It is easy to say and write, “Comrades, the bosses rob us! The bourgeoisie are drunkards! Rulers are scoundrels! We must rebel, kill the capitalists, set fire to the factories!” Moreover, before anyone wrote it the exploited had sometimes killed their exploiters, the governed had revolted, the poor had rebelled against the rich; yet the situation was in nowise altered. They had changed rulers. In 1789 property changed hands; subsequently the people revolted, hoping thereby to force it to change hands again. Yet the governing continued to oppress the governed, the rich continued to live at the expense of the exploited; nothing was altered. Since it was written the people have likewise revolted, and nothing is altered. Hence it is not a question of saying or writing that the laborer is exploited; it is necessary to explain to him above all how in changing masters he does not cease to be exploited, and how, were he to put himself in his master’s place, he would in turn become an exploiter, leaving behind him the exploited who would then make against him the same complaints he now makes against those he would like to have dispossessed. It is necessary to make him understand further how the capitalistic classes have interested him in the existing society, persuaded him to defend the privileges of his exploiters while he believes himself defending his own interests in an organization which, in fact, has nothing for him but promises never to be realized.

By its organization, based upon the antagonism of interests, our bourgeois society charges itself with the task of bringing the workers to a revolution. Now, the workers have always made revolutions, but have forever allowed the benefits thereof to be juggled away, because they “did not know.” The role of the propagandist, then, is to teach the workers; and to teach them one must give demonstrations to them. Assertion makes believers, but not conscious ones. At the time when, even for the most advanced Socialists, authority was the basis of all organization, there was nothing wrong in having mere believers. On the contrary it facilitated the task of those who set themselves up as directors. One could go ahead with assertions; one was believed according to the degree of authority he had been clever enough to acquire; and as the directors did not exact of their proselytes a knowledge of *why* they were to act, but only to “believe” strongly enough to make them blindly obey received orders, they had no need of killing themselves to furnish arguments. Believing in providential men who were to think and act for them, the mass of proselytes did not need to learn much. Had not the leaders a plan of social organization already prepared in their heads, which they would hasten to execute once they were carried into power? To know how to fight and kill each other, that was all they asked the common herd to know and to do. The leaders once in power the dear people had nothing to do but wait; everything would come to them at the proper time without their troubling themselves about it.

===Chinese===

“你们太抽象了！”这是许多人屡屡向无政府主义者（Anarchists）提出的指责。他们说，既然我们以工人为宣传对象，就应当拣选一些不那么高深的题目，宣传才会有更丰硕的成果。从前几章可以看出，正是观念自身的发展，把我们引向了对某些问题的处理，而这些问题并不总在我们所面向的读者所能领会的范围之内；这是一种我们唯有俯首听命、无力抗衡的必然。对于刚刚开始浅尝社会问题的人，我们的著作时常显得枯燥，这一点我们并不否认。可是，我们所处理的、也必须处理的问题，本身便是枯燥的，这一事实能够改变吗？我们所捍卫的这些原理彼此勾连，与人类知识的每一分支融为一体，凡愿阐明它们的人，势必被引去研究他们先前认为不必要的事物——这难道是我们所能阻止的吗？再者，他们非要我们承担的这番准备工作，难道不早已由我们的前辈——社会主义者（Socialists）——完成了吗？难道资本家阶级（capitalistic classes）本身不正在为摧毁他们自己的社会而效劳吗？难道一切野心勃勃的激进派——浸染程度或深或浅的社会主义者——不都一心要向工人证明：现今的社会对他们毫无助益，非加以改变不可吗？

因此，无政府主义者所要做的，不过是对这一浩繁的工程加以分析、加以协调、提炼其精髓。他们的职责仅在于证明：我们身受的种种弊病，绝非更换统治者所能治愈；仅仅修整社会有机体的机器，也决计阻止不了它产生那些恶果——恰恰是那些渴求上台掌权的资产者，最擅长揭露这些恶果。然而，正因为我们所倡导的观念是抽象的，我们的任务才变得复杂。倘若我们甘愿满足于高谈阔论和空口断言，那么这任务对我们、对我们的读者，都会变得轻松容易。要解决的问题越是艰难，就越有必要熟习论证与逻辑。说一说、写一写“同志们，老板们在掠夺我们！资产阶级（bourgeoisie）是酒鬼！统治者是恶棍！我们必须造反，杀掉资本家，烧掉工厂！”——这何其容易。况且，在这些话被写下来之前，被剥削者早已时而杀死剥削者，被统治者早已起来暴动，穷人早已反抗富人；然而局面毫无改变。他们只是换了一批统治者。1789 年，财产易了主；此后人民又起来暴动，指望借此迫使财产再度易主。然而统治者依旧压迫被统治者，富人依旧靠榨取被剥削者过活；什么都没有改变。这些话写下之后，人民同样起来暴动过，而什么都没有改变。可见，问题不在于说或写“工人受剥削”；首要的是向他解释：换了主人，他如何依旧逃不脱被剥削；倘若他坐上主人的位置，又将如何反过来变成剥削者，而他身后留下的被剥削者，便会像他今日控诉那些他想剥夺之人那样起来控诉他。还必须使他进一步明白：资本家阶级如何使他利益相系于现存社会，如何诱使他在捍卫剥削者特权的同时，还自以为是在捍卫自身的利益——尽管这个组织事实上留给他的，除了永远不会兑现的许诺之外，一无所有。

我们的资产阶级社会（bourgeois society）以其建立在利益对立（antagonism of interests）之上的组织方式，自行承担起把工人引向革命的任务。然而，工人从来都在进行革命，却总是任凭革命的成果被人变戏法似的骗走，因为他们“不知道”。那么，宣传者的职责便是教育工人；而要教育他们，就必须向他们提供论证。断言能造出信徒，却造不出自觉的信徒。在连最先进的社会主义者也把权威（authority）视为一切组织之基础的时代，拥有单纯的信徒原无不可；相反，这还方便了那些自立为指挥者的人。尽可以靠断言行事；一个人被相信到什么程度，取决于他巧妙挣得的权威有多少；既然指挥者并不苛求皈依者（proselytes）明白*为什么*要行动，只要求他们“相信”到足以盲目服从接到的命令，他们自然也就不必呕心沥血去提供论证。皈依者大众既然指望天命之人（providential men）替他们思考、替他们行动，也就无需学得太多。领袖们脑中不是早已备好一套社会组织的方案，只消被推上权力宝座便要即刻施行吗？懂得厮杀、懂得互相残杀，这便是他们要求平民大众懂得并做到的一切。领袖一旦掌权，可爱的人民便只消等待；一切自会在恰当的时机降临到他们头上，无需他们为此操心。

===Original===

But Anarchistic principles have come to overthrow all this. Denying the necessity of providential men, making war upon authority, and claiming for each individual the right and the duty to act under the pressure of his own impulses only, of submitting to no constraint or restriction of his autonomy; proclaiming individual initiative as the basis of all progress and of every truly libertarian association, Anarchism cannot content itself with making believers; it must, above all, aim to convince, that its converts may know why they believe, that the arguments with which they have been furnished may have struck home, that they may have weighed, discussed and considered the value of these for themselves. Hence a propaganda more difficult, more arduous, more abstract, but also more effective.

From the moment the individual relies solely on his own initiative, he must be enabled to exercise it effectively. That such initiative may adapt itself freely to the action of other individuals, it must be conscious, reasoned, based upon the logic of the natural order of facts. That all these separate acts may converge to a common end, they must be animated by a common idea, well understood and clearly elaborated; whence it follows that nothing but a close, logical, and thoroughly defined discussion of the principles can open the minds of those who adopt them, and lead such to reflect for themselves. Hence our method of procedure, which, instead of causing us to get a lot of rhetorical fireworks out of any idea we take up, leads us to turn it over in all its aspects, to dissect it, even to its final atoms, in order to extract from it the greatest possible amount of argument.

Ah, it is no small thing to overthrow a society as we talk of doing, above all when it is desired that this social upheaval shall be universal as we wish it to be! It is clear that the people who compose this society, however cruel it may be to them, are not going to see the necessity of its overthrow as we do, all in a moment, having been accustomed to look upon it as the palladium of their safety and the means of their well-being. They know very well that this society does not furnish them what it has promised, but they cannot understand the necessity for its total destruction. Has not everyone his little reform to propose, which is to grease the wheels and make the machine run to the satisfaction of all? They want, therefore, to know whether this upheaval will be profitable or prejudicial to them, whence arise a mass of questions leading to the discussion of every branch of human knowledge, in order to know whether they will survive in the cataclysm we would provoke. And hence the perplexity of the worker who sees unfolding before him a multitude of questions which they took good care not to teach him at school, discussions which it is very hard for him to follow, subjects which for the most part he hears treated of for the first time;﻿—questions, however, which he must study to the bottom and solve if he desires to be able to profit by this autonomy which he demands, if he does not want to use his initiative to his own detriment, and, more than all, if he wishes to get on without providential men.

===Chinese===

而无政府主义的诸项原理（Anarchistic principles），正是要推翻这一切：它们否定天命之人的必要，向权威宣战，主张每个个人都有权利、也有义务仅凭自身冲动的驱使而行动，不屈从于任何对自身自主性（autonomy）的强制或限制；它们宣告个人主动性（individual initiative）是一切进步的基础，也是每一种真正自由意志的联合（libertarian association）的基础。无政府主义（Anarchism）不能以满足于制造信徒为务；它首先必须力求说服，使它的皈依者知道自己为何而信，使提供给他们的论证确实击中要害，使他们得以亲自权衡、讨论并考量这些论证的价值。由此便有了一种更艰难、更辛苦、更抽象、但也更有效的宣传。

个人从仅凭自身主动性行事的那一刻起，就必须使他能够有效地行使这种主动性。要使这种主动性能自由地调适于其他个人的行动，它就必须是自觉的、经过推理的、建立在事实之自然秩序的逻辑之上的。要使这一切分散的行动汇聚于一个共同的目的，它们就必须由一种为人充分理解、经过清晰锤炼的共同观念来贯注；由此可见，唯有对原理作严密、合乎逻辑而界定透彻的讨论，才能开启采纳者的心智，并引导他们自行思索。因此便有了我们这样的行事方法：它不是让我们从所取的任何观念里炮制出一大堆辞令烟花，而是引我们从各个方面推敲这一观念，解剖它，直解剖到它最终的原子，以便从中提炼出尽可能多的论证。

啊，要推翻一个社会——如我们口中所谈论的那样——绝非小事，尤其当人们期望这场社会剧变像我们所期望的那样成为普天之下的剧变时，更是如此！十分显然，组成这个社会的人民，无论这个社会对他们何等残酷，绝不可能像我们这样于一瞬之间看出它被推翻的必要，因为他们早已习惯于把它看作自身安全的守护神、自身福祉的凭借。他们十分清楚，这个社会并没有兑现它的许诺，但他们无法理解将它彻底摧毁的必要。谁没有自己的一点小小改良要提出来，好给轮子上点油，让机器运转得人人满意呢？于是，他们想弄清这场剧变于他们究竟是利是弊；由此生出成堆的问题，引向对人类知识每一分支的讨论，以便弄清在我们想要挑起的这场巨变中他们能否幸存。于是便有了工人的困惑：他眼前展开一大堆问题——学校里小心翼翼不教给他的问题，他极难跟上的讨论，大半头一回听人谈及的题目；——然而，这些问题他却必须穷究到底、一一解决，倘若他想要真正受用于他所要求的这种自主性，倘若他不想让自己的主动性于己有损，而尤其是，倘若他希望离开天命之人也能前行。

===Original===

When a question, however abstract it may be, presents itself to the investigations of the Anarchist propagandist, he cannot make it otherwise than abstract; nor can he pass it by in silence, under the pretext that those to whom he speaks have never heard of it. To explain it in plain, clear, precise, and concise language; to avoid “thousand-legged words,” as one of our comrades puts it﻿—that is words which are understood only by the initiated;﻿—to avoid burying one’s thought in high-flown and redundant phraseology, or seeking after phrases and effects, this is all that can be done by those who have it at heart to propagate the principles, to spread them and make them understood among the masses; but we cannot mutilate them with the excuse that they are not accessible to the people. If it were necessary to evade every question which the majority of readers are not able to understand upon first enunciation, we should be condemned to return to declamation, to the art of stringing out meaningless phrases one after the other, and saying nothing. This role is too well played by the bourgeois rhetoricians for us to attempt to supersede them in it. If the workers want to emancipate themselves they must understand that this emancipation will not come of itself; that they must obtain it; and that self-education is one of the forms of the social struggle. The possibility and the continuance of their exploitation by the capitalistic class proceed from their ignorance. They must know how to free themselves intellectually if they wish to be able to free themselves materially. If they already recoil before the difficulties of mental emancipation, which depends solely upon their own willingness, what then will it be before the difficulties of a more active struggle in which it will be necessary to expend an altogether incommensurate force of character and amount of will! Useless and injurious as it is, the bourgeoisie has nevertheless succeeded in concentrating in the brains of a few all the scientific knowledge necessary to the present development of humanity. If we do not want the revolution to be a step backward, the worker must be able intellectually to replace the bourgeoisie which he wishes to overthrow; his ignorance must not be an obstacle to the development of sciences already acquired. If he does not know them thoroughly he must be able to comprehend them when he finds himself in their presence.

To be sure we quite understand all this impatience; we can imagine that those who are hungry would like to see the dawning of the day when they will be able to appease their hunger; we are perfectly aware that those who submit to the yoke of authority only by suppressing their anger, are impatient to shake it off, desirous of listening to words in conformity with their condition of mind, reminding them of their hatreds, their desires, their aspirations, their thirst for justice. But however great this impatience, however legitimate the demands and the need of realizing them, the idea advances only by degrees, penetrates the mind and lodges there only when matured and elaborated. When we consider that the bourgeoisie which we wish to overthrow took centuries of preparation before it overturned the royalty, it should cause us to reflect upon the work of elaboration which we have to do. In the fourteenth century, when Étienne Marcel attempted to seize the power for the benefit of the bourgeoisie, already organized in corporations, the bourgeois class already felt itself to be strong; for a long time it had been aspiring to authority and had organized itself for that purpose, had educated and developed itself, had worked for its enfranchisement by endeavoring to obtain from the baronage the freedom of the communes. It was not, however, till four centuries later that it succeeded in winning the long coveted boon.

Assuredly we hope not to have to wait so long for our enfranchisement and the overthrow of capitalistic exploitation. Its complete collapse at the end of so short a period of power is hurrying it on to a speedy fall. Yet if the bourgeoisie was able in 1789 to substitute itself for “divine right,” it was because it had prepared itself intellectually for such substitution; and the more rapid the downfall the more haste should we workers make to prepare ourselves intellectually, not to replace the power which we must destroy but to organize ourselves so as to prevent any aristocracy’s substituting itself for that which has given way. The idea of free individual initiative once being established, people should be enabled (we cannot repeat it too often) to learn how to reason and to combine their initiative. If they have not the will to deliver themselves from their own ignorance, how will they be able to make others understand when they themselves have not been able to learn? Let us have no fear, then, of discussing the most abstract questions; each solution obtained is a step forward on the pathway of emancipation. Leaders being discarded, the knowledge hitherto in their possession must be diffused among the masses; and there is but one means of bringing it within their reach, which is, that, while continuing to go forward we persuade them to interest themselves in questions which interest us. Once more: Let us make ourselves as clear as possible; but let us not mutilate ourselves for then, instead of bringing the masses to us we should be brought to them; instead of going forward we should go backward﻿—truly a queer way of understanding progress.

===Chinese===

一个问题，无论何等抽象，一旦呈现在无政府主义宣传者的探究面前，他便无法不将其作为抽象问题来处理；他也无法借口听众从未听说过，便对它默然放过。用平实、明晰、精确而简洁的语言把它讲清楚；避免“千足词”（thousand-legged words）——我们的一位同志如此称呼它们，即只有入门者才看得懂的词；避免把思想埋进浮夸冗赘的辞藻，或一味追求警句与效果——凡矢志传播这些原理、使之传布并为大众所理解的人，所能做的也就尽在于此；但我们决不能借口人民难以企及，便阉割这些原理。倘若凡属多数读者初次听闻时无法理解的问题都必须回避，那我们就注定要退回到高谈阔论，退回到把空洞词句一个接一个串起来的伎俩，实则什么也没有说。这套角色，资产阶级的辞令家们演得再好不过了，我们大可不必妄图取而代之。工人若想解放自己，就必须明白：这种解放不会自行到来；必须由他们亲手赢得；而自我教育正是社会斗争的形式之一。资本家阶级之所以能够剥削他们、并使剥削延续至今，正源于他们的无知。他们若想在物质上获得解放，就必须先懂得如何在精神上解放自己。倘若仅仅取决于自身意愿的精神解放，其困难已令他们退缩，那么面对一场更积极的斗争——其中须付出全然无可比拟的性格力量与意志力量——他们又将如何自处！资产阶级尽管于社会无用而且有害，却毕竟成功地把人类当前发展所必需的全部科学知识，尽数集中于少数人的头脑之中。如果我们不愿革命沦为一次倒退，工人就必须能够在智力上取代他想要推翻的资产阶级；他的无知决不能成为已获得的科学继续发展的障碍。倘若他尚未透彻掌握这些知识，他也必须做到：一旦置身其中，便能领会它们。

诚然，我们十分理解这种种急切；我们能够想象，忍饥挨饿的人多么盼望见到赖以充饥的那一天破晓；我们完全明白，那些唯有强压怒火方能屈从于权威桎梏的人，是何等急欲挣脱它，何等渴望听到契合他们心境的言辞——那些让他们想起自己的仇恨、自己的欲望、自己的抱负、自己对正义之渴求的言辞。然而，无论这急切何等深重，无论这些要求以及实现这些要求的需要何等正当，观念只能循序渐进；唯有经过成熟与锤炼，它方能渗入头脑并扎根其间。试想，我们所要推翻的资产阶级，也曾历经数百年准备方才推翻王权；这足应促使我们反省自己所必须从事的这番锤炼功夫。十四世纪，当艾蒂安·马塞尔（Étienne Marcel）试图替已经按行会组织起来的资产阶级夺取权力时，资产阶级已自觉力量强大；它觊觎权威为时已久，并为此而自行组织，自我教育，自我发展，力求从男爵贵族手中取得城市公社的自由，为自身的解放经营布置。然而，直到四个世纪之后，它才终于赢得这份觊觎已久的果实。

我们当然希望，自身的解放与资本主义剥削（capitalistic exploitation）的推翻，不致要等那么久。资本主义剥削掌权不过如此短促的一段时间便已濒于全面崩溃，这正催促它加速垮台。然而，资产阶级之所以能在 1789 年取“君权神授”（divine right）而代之，正因为它早已在智力上为这场取代作好准备；而且垮台来得越快，我们工人就越应加紧在智力上装备自己——不是为了去取代那必须摧毁的权力，而是要组织起来，防止任何一个贵族集团取那个已然垮台的权力而代之。自由个人主动性的观念一经确立，就应当使人们（这一点我们重复多少遍也不为过）学会推理，学会把各自的主动性结合起来。倘若他们连摆脱自身无知的意愿都没有——自己尚且学不会，又怎能指望他们使别人明白呢？所以，让我们不要畏惧讨论最抽象的问题；每求得一个解答，便是向解放之路迈进一步。既然抛弃了领袖，原先握于他们之手的知识就必须散播到大众之中；而要使大众够得着这些知识，办法只有一个：在我们继续前进的同时，说服他们去关心那些我们也在关心的问题。再说一遍：让我们尽可能把自己表达得清楚明白；但切勿阉割我们自己，否则，不是我们把大众拉向我们，而是我们被拉向大众；不是前进，而是倒退——这实在是对进步的一种奇怪理解。

## IV Is Man Evil? / IV 人性本恶吗？
===Original===
Is Man Evil?

It is upon the contention that “man is too evil to know how to govern himself,” that authoritarians base their justification of the power they wish to establish. “Power is necessary to reform mankind” is the reply to the Anarchists when they speak of establishing a society based upon solidarity, entire equality, and absolute autonomy for the individual, without authority, rule, or constraint. “Man is evil.” Undoubtedly! But may he become better, and may he become worse? Is any change in his present condition possible, either for good or ill? Can he be improved or deteriorated physiologically and morally? And if evolution in one sense or another be possible (as history proves that it is) does the heritage of ancient laws, the harness of old institutions, tend to make men better, or do they help to make him worse? The answer to this question will tell us which of the two, modern man or the social state, must be reformed first.

Nowadays no one denies that the physical environment has an enormous influence upon the physiological constitution of man; now, with still greater reason the intellectual and moral environment must influence his psychological constitution.

Upon what is our present society based? Does it tend to create harmony among men? Is it so managed that the adversity of one shall be felt by others, in order that all may be led to diminish or prevent it? Does personal well-being flow from general well-being, and is no one interested in disturbing the operation thereof? This society of kings, priests, merchants, and employers﻿—does it permit all generous ideas to come forth, or does it not rather tend to stifle them? Has it not at its command, for the purpose of crushing the weak, this brute force﻿—money﻿—which puts the most generous and least egoistic at the mercy of the most greedy and least scrupulous?

===Chinese===
人性本恶吗？

权威主义者（authoritarians）正是以“人过于邪恶，不懂得如何治理自己”这一论断为基础，来为他们想要建立的权力作辩护。当无政府主义者（Anarchists）谈到要建立一个以团结（solidarity）、完全平等和个人的绝对自主性（autonomy）为基础，没有权威（authority）、没有统治、没有强制的社会时，得到的回答便是：“要改造人类，权力是必需的。”“人是恶的。”毫无疑问！可是，他可能变得更好，不也可能变得更坏吗？他目前的状况有没有任何改变的可能——无论变好还是变坏？他在生理上和道德上能够被改善，不也可能被败坏吗？倘若朝这个或那个方向的进化是可能的（历史已证明其可能），那么，古代法律的遗产、旧制度的枷锁，究竟是趋于使人变得更好，还是在助长他变得更坏？对这一问题的回答将告诉我们：现代人与社会状态，二者之中究竟哪一个必须先受改造。

如今没有人否认，物质环境对人的生理构造有着巨大的影响；那么更有理由说，智力环境与道德环境也必然影响着他的心理构造。

我们现今的社会究竟建立在什么之上？它是否趋于在人与人之间创造和谐？它的安排，是否做到使一个人的不幸为他人所感受，从而引导所有人去减轻或防止这种不幸？个人福祉是否源出于普遍福祉，因而没有谁有兴趣去扰乱它的运行？这个由国王、教士、商人和雇主组成的社会——它是否容许一切慷慨的思想脱颖而出，还是毋宁说趋于扼杀它们？为了碾压弱者，它手中不是正掌握着这种蛮力——金钱——吗？这蛮力使最慷慨、最不利己的人，任凭最贪婪、最无顾忌者的摆布。
===Original===
To study the mechanism of capitalistic society is sufficient to discover that it can produce nothing good. Aspirations towards the good and the beautiful must be perennial in the human race, not to have been choked by the rapacity and narrow, unreasoning egoism which official society has inculcated in it from the cradle. This society, as we have observed in the preceding chapter, is based upon the antagonism of interests, and makes every individual the enemy of his neighbor. The seller’s interest is opposed to the buyer’s; the stock-raiser and the agriculturist ask for nothing better than a “good epidemic and a good hailstorm” among their neighbors, in order to raise the price of their commodities, when they do not have recourse to the State which “protects” them, while seizing, by virtue of “superior right,” the products of their competitors; the development of mechanical appliances tends to greater and greater division of the workers, throwing them out of employment and leading them to disputes among themselves for the chance to take each other’s jobs, and the number of these is increasing largely beyond the demand. In fine, everything in our traditional “society” tends to split up mankind.

Why is there idleness and misery at the present moment? Because the stores are glutted with products. How is it that it has not yet occurred to anybody to set them on fire or take possession of them, and thus procure that employment which is refused, by creating among the workers themselves the markets which their exploiters go so far to seek?﻿—“Because we are afraid of the soldiers and militia,” does someone say? This fear is real, but it does not of itself suffice to explain the apathy of the starving. How many occasions present themselves in the course of one’s life to do wrong without the slightest risk, and yet one does not commit it for other reasons than for fear of the soldiery. And besides, the starving, if they should all unite, are numerous enough, in Paris, for instance, not to be afraid of the troops, to hold the police-force in check for a whole day, empty the stores, and have a good feast for once. In the case of those who go to prison for tramping and begging, is it in reality the fear of prison which makes them beg for that which it would cost them no more to take? It is because in addition to cowardice there is a sentiment of sociability which prevents people from returning evil for evil, and makes them submit to the heaviest shackles in the belief that these are necessary to the functioning of society. Does anyone believe that force alone would suffice to ensure respect for property, were it not mingled in the people’s minds with a character of legitimacy which makes them accept it as the result of individual labor? Have the severest penalties ever prevented those who, without troubling themselves whether it were legitimate or not, have wished to live at the expense of others, from carrying out their intents? What would it be, then, if people, studying over their misery and discovering its cause in property, were of a nature so given to evil as is popularly alleged? Society would not endure another moment; there would then be “the struggle for existence” in its most ferocious expression, a return to pure barbarism. It is precisely because man has tended towards what is better, that he has allowed himself to be ruled, enslaved, deceived, exploited, and still repudiates violent measures to effect his final enfranchisement.

===Chinese===
只要研究一下资本主义社会（capitalistic society）的机制，便足以发现它产生不出任何好的东西。对善与美的向往必定在人类中长存不灭，才不致被官方社会从摇篮起便灌输给人的贪婪与狭隘而无理性的利己主义（egoism）所扼杀。正如我们在前一章已经指出的，这个社会建立在利益对立（antagonism of interests）之上，使每个个人都成为自己邻人的敌人。卖者的利益与买者相对立；畜牧者和农耕者巴不得邻人那里闹一场“好瘟疫和好冰雹”，好抬高自家商品的价钱；再不然，便去求助于那个“保护”他们的国家（the State）——而这个国家正凭借“更高权利”，攫取着他们竞争者的产品；机械装置的发展则趋于使工人日益分化，把他们抛出工作岗位，引得他们为抢走彼此的饭碗而互相争执，而这般人的数目正大为超出需求。总之，我们这个传统“社会”中的一切，都趋于把人类撕裂。

当前为什么会有失业与困苦？因为货栈里堆满了产品。怎么竟还没有人想到放一把火把它们烧掉，或者把它们夺取过来，通过在工人自身中间创造出那些他们的剥削者远赴千里去寻找的市场，从而赢得那份被拒绝给予的工作？——“因为我们害怕士兵和民团”，有人会这样说吗？这种恐惧是真实的，但它本身并不足以解释饥民的冷漠。人一生中该有多少机会可以毫无丝毫风险地去作恶，然而人不作恶，其理由并非出于对军人的恐惧。况且，饥民们倘若全都联合起来——以巴黎为例——其人数足以使他们不怕军队，足以把警察牵制上整整一天，搬空货栈，痛痛快快地大吃一顿。至于那些因流浪行乞而坐牢的人，实际上，难道是害怕监狱，才使他们去乞讨那夺过来丝毫不必多费力气的东西吗？这是因为，在怯懦之外，人们还有一种社会性的情感，它阻止人们以恶报恶，使人们甘受最沉重的镣铐，并相信这镣铐乃社会运转所必需。若不是财产在人们心中掺入了一种合法性的色彩，使他们把财产当作个人劳动的成果而加以接受，谁会相信单凭强力便足以确保财产受到尊重呢？最严酷的刑罚，可曾阻止过那些根本不计较其合法与否、一心想靠他人血汗过活的人实现其意图？那么，倘若人们细究自身的困苦，发现其根源在于私有制（property），而他们的天性又果真如俗世所断言的那样趋向于恶，那局面又会怎样？社会连一刻也维持不下去；那时将出现以最残暴形态表现出来的“生存斗争”（the struggle for existence），退回纯粹的野蛮状态。恰恰是因为人一直在趋向于更好的东西，他才听任自己被统治、被奴役、被欺骗、被剥削，而且至今仍在拒绝以暴力手段来实现自己最终的解放。
===Original===
This declaration that “man is born to evil,” and that there is no change to hope for, means, when analyzed: “Man is bad society is therefore bad, and there is nothing to hope for, either from one or the other. What is the use of losing one’s time in seeking for a perfection which humanity cannot attain? Let us look out for ourselves as best we can. If the sum of gratifications we obtain is made up of the tears and blood of the victim we have sown along our route, what does it matter to us? One must crush others to escape being crushed himself. So much the worse for those who fall.”﻿—Well, let the privileged ones, who have thus far managed to bolster up their sway, to send the workers to sleep, to transform them into defenders of their masters’ privileges, first by promising them a better life in the other world, then, when they ceased to believe in God, by preaching to them morality, patriotism, social utility, etc., and today by making them hope to gain, through universal suffrage, a multitude of reforms and improvements impossible to effect, (for the ills which flow from the very essence of the social organization cannot be prevented so long as we attack the effects only, without finding the cause, so long as society itself be not transformed)﻿—let the exploiters of the poor, then, proclaim the unadulterated right of force, and we shall see how long their sway will last! Force will balance force.

When man first began to group together with his fellows, he must still have been much more of an animal than a human being; ideas of morality and justice did not exist in him. Having had to struggle against other animals, against all nature, his first groups were formed out of the necessity for an association of forces, not by the desire for solidarity. No doubt these associations were, as we have already said, temporary at the start, limited to the capture of the game to be hunted or the overthrow of the obstacle to be conquered, later to the repulse or killing of an assailant. It was only by thus practicing association that men were brought to understand its importance; and the societies thus formed continued to live and became permanent. But on the other hand this life of continual struggle could not help developing the sanguinary and despotic instinct in people. The weaker had to submit to the rule of the stronger when they did not serve the latter as food. It could not have been till much later that cunning gained a precedence equal to that of force.

===Chinese===
这一断言——“人生而为恶”，无可指望其改变——分析起来意味着：“人是坏的，社会因而也是坏的，无论对人还是对社会，都没有任何可以指望的东西。何必浪费时间去追求一种人类无法企及的完善？我们还是各自顾好自己吧。倘若我们所获得的种种满足，是由我们沿路撒下的受害者的眼泪与鲜血积成，与我们又有什么相干？人必须碾压别人，才能免于被碾压。谁跌倒了，谁就更加活该。”——好得很，那么，就让那些特权者——他们迄今一直设法支撑自己的统治，哄工人入睡，把工人变成其主人特权的捍卫者：起初许诺他们在彼岸世界过上更好的生活；后来见工人不再信上帝，便改向他们宣讲道德、爱国主义、社会效用等等；如今又让他们指望通过普选（universal suffrage）赢得无数不可能实现的改良与改善（因为，只消我们还仅仅打击现象而未找出原因，只消社会本身尚未得到改造，源于社会组织本质的种种弊病便无从防止）——就让这些穷人的剥削者，径直去宣告那赤裸裸的强力之权吧，且看他们的统治还能维持多久！强力自会与强力相抗衡。

人最初开始与同类聚群而居时，必定还远更像一头动物，而不是一个人；道德与正义的观念在他身上尚不存在。由于不得不与其他动物、与整个自然作斗争，他最初的群体是出于联合力量的必要而形成的，并非出于对团结的渴望。毫无疑问，正如我们已经说过的，这些联合起初是临时性的，限于捕获将猎取的猎物，或推倒将克服的障碍，后来则是击退或杀死来犯者。人们正是通过这般践行联合，才被引导去懂得它的重要；由此形成的社会得以继续存续，变成了永久性的。但另一方面，这种连绵不断的斗争生活，免不了发展人们身上嗜血而专横的本能。较弱者若不充当较强者的食物，便只得屈从于较强者的统治。狡诈取得与强力相埒的地位，那必定是很久以后的事了。
===Original===
When we study man in his earliest appearance it must be admitted that he was then a wicked enough animal indeed; but since he has reached his present development and formed conceptions of which he was formerly incapable, what reason is there why he should stop and go no further? To attempt to deny that man may still progress is to be as much in error as if one had affirmed, at the time he dwelt in caves and had nothing but a club or a stone weapon as a means of defense, that he would not one day become capable of building the opulent cities of today, of utilizing electricity and steam. Why shall man, who has reached the point of guiding the selection of domestic animals in the direction of his needs, not reach the point of guiding his own tastes in the direction of the good and beautiful of which he begins to have conceptions? Little by little man has evolved and does evolve every day. His ideas are constantly modified. Physical force, though sometimes thrusting itself upon him, is no longer admired in the same degree. Ideas of morality, of justice, of solidarity have developed; they have so much weight that the privileged, to succeed in maintaining their privileges, are obliged to make people believe themselves exploited and gagged in their own interest. This deception cannot last. People begin to feel themselves too cramped in this illy-balanced society. Aspirations which began to come to light centuries ago, at first isolated and incomplete, today begin to assume definite shape; they are found even among those who may be classed among the privileged of the present organization. There is not a single person who has not at times uttered his cry of revolt or indignation against this society, still governed by the dead, which seems to have undertaken the task of crushing all our sentiments, acts, aspirations, and from which we suffer the more in proportion to our development. Ideas of liberty and justice are becoming more defined; those who proclaim them are still in the minority, but a minority strong enough to make the possessing classes uneasy and afraid.

Man, then, like all other animals, is but the product of an evolution worked out under the influence of the environment in which he lives and the conditions of existence he is forced to submit to or combat; only, more than other animals, or at least in a higher degree, has he come to reason upon his origin, to formulate aspirations for his future. It depends upon him to conjure this fatality of “evil” alleged to be attached to his existence. By succeeding in creating for himself other conditions of life he will succeed in modifying himself also. For the rest, without going further the question may be summarized thus: “Has every individual, good or bad, the right to live as he likes, to revolt if exploited, or if others seek to bind him to conditions of existence repugnant to him?” The pets of fortune and those who are in power claim to be better than others; but it would suffice that “the bad” should overthrow them and establish themselves in their place, thus inverting the roles, to have equal reason with the first for being “good.” The system of private property, by putting all our social wealth in the hands of a few, has permitted these to live as parasites at the expense of the mass whom they have enslaved, and whose product only serves to keep up their show and idleness, or to defend their interests. This condition, recognized as unjust by those who submit to it, cannot last. The workers will demand free possession of what they produce, and will revolt if the denial of the request continues. Vainly does capitalism seek to entrench itself behind the argument that “man is evil;” the revolution will come. Then it will appear either that man is indeed incapable of perfectibility, (we have just seen the contrary) in which case there would be a war of appetites, and whatever theirs might be the capitalistic classes would be doomed in advance, since they are the minority﻿—or that man is evil because institutions help to make him such; in which case he may elevate himself to a social state which will contribute to his moral, intellectual, and physical development, and manage to transform society in such a way as will effect the solidarity of all its interests. But however it be, *the revolution will come*! The sphinx interrogates us and we answer without fear, for we Anarchists, destroyers of laws and property, we know the key to the enigma.


===Chinese===
研究人类最初的面貌时必须承认，他那时确实是一头够邪恶的动物；然而，既然他已达到今天的发展程度，形成了从前无力形成的种种观念，又有什么理由认为他应当止步、不能再前进呢？试图否认人类仍可进步，其错误之大，不亚于有人在他穴居洞窟、除一根木棒或一件石器之外别无防身之器的年代，断言他永远不会有朝一日建得起今日这些富庶的城市、用得上电与蒸汽。人既已能按自己的需要引导家畜的选育，为什么就不能进而引导自己的趣味，使之趋向他开始有了观念的善与美呢？人在一点一点地进化，而且每天都在进化。他的观念不断被修正。强力虽仍不时强行加诸于人，却不再受到同等程度的钦慕了。道德、正义、团结的观念已经发展起来；它们的分量如此之重，以至特权者为要维持其特权，只得让人们相信：自己受剥削、被钳口，乃是为了他们自身的利益。这种欺骗持续不了多久。人们开始感到，在这个严重失衡的社会里，自己被挤压得太局促了。数百年前开始显露的种种向往，起初孤立而不完整，今天开始呈现出确定的形态；甚至在那些可归入现存组织特权者之列的人中间，也能发现它们。没有一个人不曾时而对这个依旧由死人统治的社会发出反叛或义愤的呼喊——这个社会仿佛领受了这样的任务：碾碎我们全部的情感、行动与抱负；而我们的发展愈深，便愈是受它的苦。自由与正义的观念正变得愈加明确；宣示这些观念的人仍居少数，但这少数已强大到足以令有产阶级不安、畏惧的程度。

人，和所有其他动物一样，不过是在他所生活的环境、以及他被迫屈从或必须抗击的生存条件的影响之下，演化而成的产物；只不过，与其他动物相比，或至少在更高的程度上，他已学会推究自己的起源，为自己的未来拟出种种抱负。祛除这据称附着于他存在的“恶”之宿命，全凭他自己。他只要成功为自己创造出别样的生活条件，也就能成功地改变自己。至于其余，不必再往下深究，问题可以概括如下：“每一个个人，无论善恶，是否都有权照自己的意愿生活，受剥削时是否有权反叛，当他人企图把他捆绑于他所嫌恶的生存条件时，是否有权反抗？”命运的宠儿与掌权者们自称比别人更好；但只消“恶人”把他们推翻、自己取而代之，把角色倒转过来，他们便与先前那些人一样，有同样充分的理由充当“善人”了。私有制（private property）体系把我们全部的社会财富（social wealth）置于少数人手中，容许这些人像寄生虫一般，靠他们所奴役的大众过活，而大众的产品，只不过用来维持他们的排场与游惰，或捍卫他们的利益。这种状况，连屈从于它的人都公认其不公正，是不可能长久的。工人将要求自由占有他们所生产的东西；倘若这一要求继续遭到拒绝，他们便将起来反叛。任凭资本主义躲在这一论据——“人是恶的”——背后筑垒自守，也是枉然；革命必将来临。到那时，或者将表明人确实无可完善（我们方才已看到相反的情形），那样便会有一场欲望的战争，而无论欲望为何，资本家阶级（capitalistic classes）都注定事先败亡，因为他们是少数；或者将表明，人之所以恶，是因为制度助他成恶，那样，他便可以把自己提升到一种有助于其道德、智力与体力发展的社会状态，并能设法如此改造社会，使社会全部利益团结一致。然而，无论如何，*革命必将来临*！斯芬克斯向我们发问，我们毫无畏惧地作答；因为我们无政府主义者——法律与私有制的破坏者——知道这则谜语的谜底。

## V Property / V 私有制

===Original===
Property

Before proceeding with the exposition of our ideas it will be well to review the institutions which we wish to destroy, to discover upon what bases capitalistic society rests, the positive value of these bases, and why and how society is transformable only on condition that the entire organization be changed; why no improvement will be possible so long as this transformation is not wrought. From this study the reasons why we are Anarchists and revolutionists will naturally follow.

Protection to private property and hereditary transmission of the same in families﻿—this is the principle upon which existing society rests. Authority, the family, the magistracy, the army, and every hierarchic and bureaucratic organization, which stifles and devours us, proceed from this principle. There is religion also, but we leave it aside, since science, bourgeois though it be, has killed that.

===Chinese===
私有制

在继续阐述我们的观念之前，不妨先审视一遍我们意欲摧毁的那些制度，弄清资本主义社会（capitalistic society）究竟建立在哪些基础之上，这些基础本身具有多少确实的价值，以及社会为何、又如何只有在整个组织悉数改变的条件下才是可以改造的；为何在这场改造完成之前，任何改良都无从指望。我们之所以是无政府主义者（Anarchists）和革命者，其缘由自会从这项研究中顺理成章地显现出来。

保护私有制（private property），保护它在家族中的世袭传承——这便是现存社会赖以立足的原则。权威（authority）、家庭、司法当局（the magistracy）、军队，以及一切扼杀我们、吞噬我们的等级制与官僚制的组织，皆源于这一原则。宗教也在其列，但我们且把它搁置不论，因为科学——尽管是资产阶级的科学——早已把它杀死了。

===Original===
We do not propose to give the history of property.
