That has been done, again and again, by Socialists of all schools; all have shown that it is nothing else than the result of robbery, fraud, and the right of force. Here, therefore, we have only to notice certain facts which demonstrate its iniquity and exhibit the evils which flow from it; which prove that proposed reforms are but snares to deceive the exploited, and that, to prevent the evils we wish to cure, we must attack their principal source, the present proprietary and capitalistic organization.

Science shows us today that the earth owes its origin to a nucleus of cosmic matter, primevally detached from the solar nebula. This nucleus, by the effect of its rotation upon its axis and around the central star, became condensed to such a degree that the compression of the gases led to their conflagration; and this globe, son of the sun, like that which had given it birth, must then have shone with its own light in the Milky Way like a very small star. The globe cooled, having passed from the gaseous to the liquid state, then to a slimy condition, then, becoming more and more dense, to complete solidification. But in this primitive furnace the association of different gases was effected in such a fashion that their various combinations had given birth to those fundamental materials which form the composition of the earth: minerals, metals, free gases suspended in the atmosphere. The operation of cooling progressing by degrees, the action of air and water upon the minerals helped to form a coating of vegetable earth. During this time the association of hydrogen, oxygen, carbon, and nitrogen, begot in the depths of the waters, a species of organic jelly, without definite form, without organs, without consciousness, but already endowed with the faculty of changing place by pushing out prolongations of its substance on the side towards which it wished to go, or rather on the side upon which some attraction made itself felt; endowed, too, with this additional faculty of assimilating foreign bodies taken into its substance and thereby nourishing itself. Finally, endowed with one last faculty: having reached a certain degree of development, the power of dividing itself in two and giving birth to a new organism, in every respect similar to its progenitor.

===Chinese===
我们并不打算在此讲述私有制的历史。各派社会主义者（Socialists）已一再做过这项工作；他们全都表明：私有制无非是掠夺、欺诈与强力之权（the right of force）的产物。因此，我们在此只需指出若干事实，以证明它的不义，展示由它奔流而出的种种祸害；证明所提议的种种改良不过是欺骗被剥削者的圈套；证明要防止我们意欲治愈的这些祸害，就必须攻击它们的主要源头，即现今的私有与资本主义的组织。

今日的科学告诉我们：地球起源于一团太初之时从太阳星云中分离出来的宇宙物质。这一物质核心由于绕轴自转并环绕中央恒星运转，凝缩到了这样的程度，以致气体的压缩引发了燃烧；于是这个太阳之子，与生育它的那个星体一样，当时想必曾以自己的光在银河（Milky Way）中闪耀，宛如一颗极小的星。这颗星球渐渐冷却，从气态过渡到液态，继而呈黏滑之状，随后愈益致密，终至完全凝固。而在这座太初的熔炉里，各种气体以这样的方式相互化合，以致它们的种种组合孕育出了构成地球组分的那些基本材料：矿物、金属、悬浮在大气中的游离气体。冷却的过程逐步推进，空气和水对矿物的作用促成了地表沃土层的形成。与此同时，氢、氧、碳、氮的化合在水体深处孕育出一种有机胶质（organic jelly）：它没有确定的形态，没有器官，没有意识，却已禀有变更位置的机能——朝它想去的方向、或不如说朝某种吸引所及的方向，伸出自身物质的延展；它还禀有另一种机能：把纳入体内的外来物体加以同化，借以滋养自身。最后，它又禀有一样机能：发育到一定程度之后，能够一分为二，产出一个在各方面都与亲体相似的新有机体。

===Original===
Behold the modest beginnings of humanity! So modest that it is only very much later, after a long series of evolutions, after the formation of a certain number of types in the chain of beings, that we come to distinguish the animal from the vegetable. To trace the whole series up to man would be to rewrite here the history of evolution, which modern science explains in a manner so clear and comprehensible to those who are willing to judge without prejudice, that we can only refer the reader to it, contenting ourselves with instancing merely the principal facts in support of our demonstration concerning the arbitrary monopolization of a part of the soil by a certain set of individuals, who take possession of it for their own profit and that of their descendants, to the injury of others less favored and of future generations. It is perfectly plain that this explanation of the appearance of man upon the earth destroys all the marvelous story of his creation. No more God, nor creative entity! Man is but the product of an evolution of terrestrial life which is itself but the product of a combination of gases which gases have in turn undergone an evolution before attaining the power of combining in the density and proportions necessary to the development of vital phenomena.

The theory of the supernatural origin of man being set aside, the idea that society, such as exists, with its divisions of rich and poor, governed and governing, proceeds from a divine will, no longer holds good. Authority, so long propped up by its “supernatural origin,”﻿—a fable which has contributed at least as much as brute force to maintain it﻿—was in its turn exhausted under the discussion and menaced with ruin; today it entrenched itself behind universal suffrage and majority rule. But authority could maintain itself intact only so long as it was not discussed. We shall see further that it no longer has any means to support itself save force. Hence we may say that property and authority, being placed under discussion, are on the highroad to extinction, for what is discussed is scarcely revered any longer; that which force alone sustains force can destroy.

The vegetable sustains itself at the expense of the mineral and the atmosphere, the animal at the expense of the vegetable and, later, at the expense of the animal itself. But there are no preconceived ideas in this, having in view the establishment of any hierarchy among beings, on the part of a creator, or of a nature-entity, who should have created the vegetable to serve as food for the animal, the animal and vegetable to feed man or to be slaves to the human race in order to create the happiness of the elect. There is only an evolving sequence of natural laws, which so resulted that the condensation of gases having formed minerals, there was nothing but vegetable life which could assimilate the mineral and transform it into an organic combination capable of hastening the birth of animal life.

===Chinese===
请看人类这微末的开端！微末到如此地步，以致只是在很久以后，历经一长串演化、历经存在之链中若干类型的形成之后，我们才得以把动物与植物区分开来。若要把这整条序列一直追溯至人，便无异于在此重写一部进化史——现代科学对这段历史的讲解，对于愿意摒弃成见加以判断的人是何等清晰易懂——故我们唯有请读者自行参阅，而满足于只列举若干主要事实，以支撑我们关于下述行为的论证：某一伙人专断地垄断（monopolization）一部分土壤，为自身及其子孙的利益据为己有，却损害了较少得到眷顾的他人与未来的世代。十分显然，这一关于人类出现于地球的解释，摧毁了关于他被创造的整篇神异故事。不再有上帝，也不再有什么创世的实体！人不过是地球生命之进化的产物，而地球生命本身又不过是气体化合的产物——这些气体在获得以生命现象发展所必需的密度与比例相互化合的能力之前，也各自经历了一场演化。

人类的超自然起源论既被撇开，那种认为社会——如现今存在的社会，连同它贫与富、被治者与治者的划分——出自某种神意的观念，便再也站不住了。权威曾长年倚仗其“超自然起源”的支撑——这则寓言在维持权威上所出的力，至少不亚于蛮力——如今它又在讨论之下衰竭失据、濒于倾覆；于是今日它退守到了普选（universal suffrage）与多数统治的背后。然而，唯有在无人讨论它的时候，权威才能完好地维持自身。我们往后还将看到：如今它除了强力之外，再无别的手段可以支撑自己。因此我们可以说：私有制与权威既被置于讨论之下，便已踏上去往消亡的坦途，因为凡受讨论的东西便难得再受崇敬；而单凭强力维持的东西，强力也能将其摧毁。

植物靠矿物与大气维系自身，动物靠植物、后来又靠动物自身维系。但这中间并无任何预定的观念，并非造物主、或什么自然实体怀着在众生之间确立某种等级的用意，创造了植物给动物充作食物，创造动物与植物供养人类、或充当人类种族的奴隶，以造就特选者的幸福。这里只有自然规律的演进序列：气体的凝缩既已形成矿物，便唯有植物生命能够同化矿物，把它转化为一种足以加速动物生命诞生的有机化合物——事情不过是如此演变而来。

===Original===
The evolutional origin of man being admitted it becomes evident to all that, when the first thinking beings appeared upon the earth, there could no longer be any need of a tutelary providence to facilitate their development, and consequently nobody to assign to some a directing power over their fellows, to others property in the soil, and to the great mass misery and privation, respect for their masters, with the sole function of producing for the benefit of the latter. However “the struggle for existence” having begun as the sole law for individuals, to eat in order not to be eaten was their sole preoccupation. But when they commenced unconsciously to practice this other and higher vital law, *assistance in struggle*, heredity having developed in them the instincts of combativeness, of oppression towards the victim, (everything being a prey to man, even man himself) it follows according to all the evidence that this spirit of struggle and domination, stored up in the brain by past generations, sought to gain like precedence in the organized collectivity. Those persons who possessed it in the highest degree imposed their will upon those who possessed it in a lesser degree. This authority, being established, followed the fluctuations of human intelligence, and transformations of the social organization were effected accordingly as force, the religious spirit, or commercialism, were triumphant. Authority under its divers modes of operation, therefore, has maintained itself up to the present time, and will maintain itself until man, freed from error and prejudice, reconquers himself entirely, renouncing the imposition of his will upon others in order not to have to submit to the will of those stronger than himself.

But the divine origin of authority and property being denied by bourgeois science itself, the bourgeoisie have sought to give them solid and natural bases. The economists have taken social facts resulting from a bad organization, and setting them up as “natural laws,” making them the cause of what exists while they are but the effects, decorating these absurdities with the name of science, they have pretended to legitimize the most monstrous social crimes, the most heinous piracies of capitalism, blaming the causes of poverty upon the poor themselves, setting up the most monstrous egoism as a law of social conservation, when, on the contrary, as we have seen in one of the preceding chapters, egoism is but a cause of conflict, of loss of energy, and retrogression, if it be not tempered and softened by this other more evolved and humane law of solidarity.

===Chinese===
人类的进化起源既获承认，人人便都看得分明：当地球上出现最初那些会思考的存在时，他们的发展再也无需什么监护性的天意（tutelary providence）来加以护持；因而，也再没有什么人可以有权把支配同伴的权力指派给一些人，把土壤的财产指派给另一些人，而指派给广大群众的，唯有贫困与匮乏、对主人的敬重，以及专为造福于前两种人而生产的唯一职能。然而，“生存斗争”（the struggle for existence）既已作为个体的唯一法则开场，为了不被吃掉而吃掉对方，便是他们唯一的牵挂。可是，当他们开始不自觉地践行这另一条更高的生命法则——*斗争中的互助*（assistance in struggle）——的时候，遗传（heredity）既已在他们身上发展出好斗的本能与对猎物施压的本能（万物皆为人之猎物，连人自己也不例外），那么循一切迹象可知，这种由历代积蓄留存于头脑中的斗争与统治精神，也就要在组织起来的集体（collectivity）中夺取同样的优越地位。禀有这种精神至最高度的人，便把自己的意志强加于禀有程度较低的人。这权威一经确立，便随人类智识的起伏而波动；社会组织的种种变革，也视蛮力、宗教精神或商业精神何者得势而相继发生。于是，权威在其种种运作方式之下一直维持至今，并将继续维持下去，直到人摆脱了谬误与成见、完全重新征服自身，直到他放弃把自己的意志强加于人，从而也无须再屈从于比自己更强者的意志。

然而，权威与私有制的神圣起源既已连资产阶级科学自身也加以否认，资产阶级（bourgeoisie）便设法给它们安上坚实的自然基础。经济学家们把起因于恶劣组织的社会事实拿来，奉为“自然规律”（“natural laws”），把它们说成现存事物的原因，而它们其实只是结果；再给这些荒谬之物装点上科学之名，他们便妄图使最骇人听闻的社会罪行、资本主义最穷凶极恶的海盗行径合法化，把贫困的罪责推到穷人自己身上，把最骇人的利己主义（egoism）尊为社会保存的法则——而恰恰相反，正如我们在前文某一章里已经看到的：利己主义若不经由另一条更进化、更人道的法则——团结（solidarity）——加以节制与缓和，便只是冲突、精力损耗与倒退的一个根源。

===Original===
Bourgeois society being founded upon capital, and this being represented by money, the economists, in order to mask the peculiar role it plays in the work of production and exchange, have reduced everything to capital. The man who impregnates his wife and begets children, expends capital; but he creates some also, for the child, become a man, will be﻿—capital! The muscular power which the workman spends in production﻿—capital! (Observe, by the way, that besides their arms the workmen, in the performance of no matter what sort of work, bring to bear an amount of intelligence often superior to that of the contractor; but as it would then be necessary to count two portions of capital for the workman, and as that would embarrass the economists in their calculations, they pass this over in silence). Yet as all this reduction of human activity to capital does not explain the origin of money-capital, the economists have discovered that “money-capital is that portion of labor which industrious and provident persons have not immediately consumed and have held in reserve for future needs.” Right here the calculation becomes interesting. All capital put into use, the economists dogmatically affirm:﻿—

- 
					

It ought to produce a sum equal to its own value, that it may reconstitute itself completely;

- 
					

As this employed capital runs risks, it should produce a surplus value, which represents an insurance-premium to cover the said risks.

Now, the workman, who is paid right along for his labor and consequently runs no risks, has a right to the first claim only, permitting him to replace his capital expended; that is to say, to feed, clothe, and lodge himself and finally to repair the strength which he has depleted. He should not produce more children than the excess of his wages permits him to bring up.

===Chinese===
资产阶级社会（bourgeois society）既以资本为根基，而资本又以货币为代表，经济学家们为了掩盖资本在生产与交换的作业中所扮演的特殊角色，便把一切都化约为资本。男子使妻子受孕、生儿育女，是支出资本；但他也创造资本，因为孩子长大成人之后便是——资本！工人在生产中耗费的肌肉力量——资本！（顺便请注意，工人无论从事何种工作，除了双臂之外，所投入的智力往往还高于承包人；但倘若这样就得为工人算两份资本，而那会使经济学家们的计算陷入窘境，所以他们对此缄口不提。）然而，把人类活动尽数化约为资本，终究解释不了货币资本（money-capital）的来历，于是经济学家们又发现：“货币资本乃是勤勉而节俭之人未曾即刻消费、而留存下来以备将来之需的那一部分劳动。”恰在这里，这笔计算变得有趣起来。凡投入使用的资本，经济学家们斩钉截铁地断言：——

- 它应当产出一笔与其自身价值相等的金额，以便彻底地自我重置；

- 这笔投入使用的资本既然冒着风险，就应当产出一笔剩余价值（surplus value），它代表着弥补上述风险的一笔保险费（insurance-premium）。

至于工人，他既随时领取劳动报酬、因而不冒任何风险，便仅有权主张第一项要求：所得恰足以补偿他耗费的资本；也就是说，恰足以吃饭、穿衣、安身，并最终修复他耗损的气力。他抚养的孩子，也不该多于其工资盈余所允许的数目。

===Original===
But the employer﻿—Oh! it is a different affair with him! In the first place he invests an original capital, the money necessary to pay the workmen, settle for purchases, etc., which represents the pleasures of which he has deprived himself. This capital, like that of the workman, ought to bring in sufficient to replace itself, but in addition the insurance premium for the risks it runs, which constitutes the profit of the exploiter. Secondly, if it be an industrial enterprise, there are buildings and machines for the employees﻿—still more capital to be reproduced and to bring in its insurance premium. But this is not all. The exploiter’s intelligence is capital, too, and none of the least. A capitalist must know how to make judicious employment of his capital, how to manage his business and himself; he must inquire as to what products it is advantageous to produce, where they are in demand, etc. This third capital must be restored out of the enterprise. Observe that if the investor be an engineer, a scholar, or a doctor, the premium must be much greater, because, costing more dearly to establish, they consequently cost much more dearly to repair.

This subtle distinction established, transforming into capital the divers elements of production, the division seems fair: the capitalist pockets three-fourths of the product for his share and the trick is played. The workman has received his pay, why should he complain? Let him economize also and he may likewise invest his savings in enterprises and triple his share! Let him stop spending his money foolishly in saloons! Let him not have so many children! The struggle is hard; they must learn how to curtail their pleasures if they want to increase them later, pack of loafers that they are!

===Chinese===
可是雇主——哦！那就是另一回事了！首先，他投入一笔原始资本，即支付工人工资、偿付购货款项等所必需的货币，这笔资本代表着他为此舍弃的种种享乐。这笔资本和工人的资本一样，理应带来足够的收益以重置自身，但除此以外，还要加上它所冒风险的保险费，这便构成了剥削者的利润。其次，倘若这是工业企业，还有供雇员使用的厂房与机器——又是更多有待重置、有待收取保险费的资本。而这还不是全部。剥削者的智力也是资本，而且绝非最无足轻重的一种。资本家必须懂得如何明智地运用自己的资本，如何经营他的生意和他自己；他必须打听生产什么产品有利可图、什么地方有需求，等等。这第三种资本也必须从企业的收益中得到补偿。请注意，倘若投资者是工程师、学者或医生，保险费还必须高得多，因为这类人的造就既更为昂贵，其修补自然也就昂贵得多。

这一精微的区分既已确立，生产的各种要素又都被变成了资本，分派看上去便很公道：资本家把产品的四分之三装进腰包，戏法就此变完。工人已经领到工资，还有什么可抱怨的？让他也节俭吧，他同样可以把积蓄投进企业，使自己的份额翻上三倍！让他别再把钱愚蠢地挥霍在小酒馆里！让他少生些孩子！斗争是严酷的；这群游手好闲的家伙，若想日后再增加享乐，就得先学会削减眼下的享乐！

===Original===
Would the gentlemen economists, who talk to us of the greater intelligence of the capitalists, venture to affirm that those who, by a stroke on the bourse, by stock-jobbing and monopolies, sweep away millions, have expended an intelligence a million times superior, we will not say to that of the workman who may pass as an artist at his trade, but to that of even the humblest workman in the lowest trade? Take a workman, supposing him to be one of the most favored, earning good wages as compared with the least favored, having no periods of enforced idleness or sickness. Can this workman live the larger life which ought to be assured to those who produce, in order to satisfy their physical and intellectual needs while working? Nonsense! He cannot satisfy the hundredth part of his needs, however limited they be. He must reduce them still further if he wants to save a few pennies for his old age. And however great his parsimony he will never manage to save enough to live without working. The savings accumulated during his productive period will hardly amount to compensation for the deficit which old age brings, unless he receive an inheritance or some other windfall which has nothing to do with work. And for every one of these privileged workers how many wretched ones are there who have nothing to appease their hunger! The development of machinery has permitted the exploiters to reduce the number of their hands; the unemployed, become more and more numerous, have diminished wages and multiplied periods of enforced idleness; sickness reduces them still further, so that “the wage-earner in good circumstances” tends more and more to become a myth, and, instead of hoping to get out of his misery, the worker must expect, if capitalistic society endures much longer, to sink still deeper in it.

Now, let us suppose that the well-situated workman, instead of continuing to invest his savings in values of any kind, sets himself up in business on his own account after he has gathered together a certain amount. This is becoming more and more impossible, thanks to machinery, which requires the concentration of enormous capital and leaves no room for the isolated workman; but we will assume its possibility and suppose that this workman-employer works alone. If the postulate of political economy be true, that every faculty of man is employed capital, and that it produces a fortune for him who puts it into use, here is an individual who invests money-capital, force-capital, and intelligence-capital; having to divide with nobody it will not be long till he sees his money-capital increase tenfold in his hands, and becomes in his turn a millionaire.

===Chinese===
经济学家先生们向我们侈谈资本家的智力何等高超，他们可敢断言：那些凭交易所里的一击、凭股票投机与垄断而卷走数百万的人，所耗费的智力也高出百万倍——且不说高于那个在本行堪称艺匠的工人，甚至高于最低贱行当里最卑微的工人？试取一名工人，姑且设他属于最受眷顾之列：与最不受眷顾者相比工资优厚，没有被迫赋闲的时期，也没有疾病。这名工人可过得上那种更宽广的生活——那种本应确保给生产者的生活，好让他在劳动的同时满足身体与智力的需要？废话！他的需要纵然再有限，他也满足不了其中的百分之一。他若想为晚年攒下几个铜板，还必须把需要压缩得更小。而无论他何等吝俭，他永远攒不够足以不再劳动而生活的钱财。他在生产年期里积攒的储蓄，几乎抵不上年老所带来的亏空，除非他继承一笔遗产，或碰上什么与劳动全不相干的意外之财。而每有这样一名受眷顾的工人，又有多少悲惨的人连充饥的东西都没有！机器的发展使剥削者得以裁减人手；失业者愈来愈多，压低了工资，也使被迫赋闲（enforced idleness）的时期成倍增加；疾病又使他们进一步减少，以致“境况宽裕的工资劳动者”（“the wage-earner in good circumstances”）越来越沦为神话；工人非但别指望摆脱苦难，倘若资本主义社会再延续得更久，他只能预期自己陷得更深。

现在，姑且假设这位处境优裕的工人不再把积蓄继续投于任何形式的资产，而是在攒起一定数额之后，独立开业，自任其主。由于机器要求巨额资本的集中、再不容孤立的工人立足，这种可能正变得愈来愈小；但我们姑且承认其可能，并假设这位工人兼雇主独自经营。倘若政治经济学的公设当真成立——人的每一种官能都是投入使用之资本，都会为运用它的人产出一笔财富——那么这里就有一个人，同时投入了货币资本、力量资本与智力资本；既然无需与任何人分肥，那么用不了多久，他就会眼见自己的货币资本在手里增至十倍，进而自己也成了百万富翁。

===Original===
In practice the workman who works alone on his own account is rarely to be found. The small employer, with two or three workmen, lives perhaps a little better than those he employs, but he must work as hard, if not harder, constantly pressed as he is to meet his obligations; he can expect no improvement, happy if he manage to maintain himself in his comparative comfort and escape failure. Big profits, big fortunes, life “driven four-in-hand,” are reserved to the big proprietors, big shareholders, big manufacturers, big speculators, who do not work themselves but employ workmen by hundreds; which proves that capital is indeed accumulated labor, but the labor of others accumulated in the hands of one person﻿—a robber! For the rest the best proof that there is something fundamentally vicious in the social organization, is that machinery, a development begotten by all our acquired knowledge transmitted from generation to generation, and which consequently ought to benefit every human being, rendering all lives broader and easier by the fact that it increases their power of production and furnishes them the means of producing much more while working fewer hours﻿—machinery has brought nothing but an increase of misery and privation to the workers. The capitalists are the only ones to benefit by the advantages of mechanical inventions, which enable them to reduce the number of their employees, and with the help of the antagonism thus established, competition between the unemployed and the employed, they profit by lowering the wages of the latter, poverty forcing the former to accept the price offered, even though it be less than the amount necessary to their maintenance and restoration of energy; which proves that the pretended “natural laws” are violated by their own operation, and that consequently if they be laws they are far from being natural.

On the other hand it is certain that the capitalists, with all their capital, all their machinery, could produce nothing without the cooperation of the workers, whilst the latter, by coming to an understanding among themselves and uniting their forces, could produce very well without the assistance of capital. But setting that aside the conclusion we want to draw is this:﻿—From the moment it is admitted that the capitalists cannot put their capital into use without the cooperation of the worker, the latter becomes the most important factor in production, and in all logic ought to receive the greater share of the product. Now, how comes it that it is the capitalists, on the contrary, who absorb the greater share of the product? The less they produce the more they get! And the more the workers produce the more they increase their chances of idleness, and have consequently less chances to consume! How comes it that the more the stores are crammed with products the more the producers die of hunger, and what ought to be a source of general wealth and enjoyment becomes a source of misery for those who labor?

===Chinese===
实际上，独自经营、自食其力的工人殊难觅得。雇着两三个工人的小雇主，日子或许过得比他所雇的人略好一点，但他干得同样苦，甚至更苦，因为偿付债务的压力无时不迫在眉睫；他无从指望任何改善，能保住这份相对的安稳、不致破产，已是万幸。丰厚的利润、巨额的财富、“四马并驾”的生活，是留给大私有主、大股东、大制造商、大投机者的，他们自己不劳动，却成百地雇用工人；这证明资本的确是积累起来的劳动，只不过是他人劳动积累于一人之手——而这人是强盗！再者，社会组织必有根本弊病的最好明证便是：机器本是世代相传的全部既有知识所孕育的发展，因而理应造福于每一个人，理应借增进人们的生产力、使人们得以在更短的工作时间内生产多得多的东西，而使一切生活变得更宽广、更轻松——然而机器给工人带来的，除了苦难与匮乏的加深之外一无所有。机械发明的种种好处，唯独资本家受用：这些发明使他们得以裁减雇员人数，又借助于由此造成的对立——失业者与在业者之间的竞争——他们压低后者的工资而坐收其利，贫困迫使前者接受雇主开出的价钱，哪怕这价钱低于维持生计、恢复精力所必需的数额；这证明那些所谓的“自然规律”被它们自身的运转所破坏，因而，倘若它们果真是规律，也远谈不上自然。

另一方面，有一点确凿无疑：资本家纵有全部资本、全部机器，若没有工人的协作，便什么也生产不出来；而后者只要彼此达成谅解、联合各自的力量，无需资本之助反倒能生产得很好。但撇开此点不谈，我们要引出的结论是：——既然承认资本家若无工人的协作便无法运用其资本，工人便成为生产中最重要的因素，照一切逻辑都应分得产品的较大份额。然而，何以恰恰相反，吞没产品较大份额的竟是资本家？他们生产得越少，得到的越多！而工人生产得越多，赋闲的机会反而越多，因而消费的机会反而越少！何以店铺里的产品堆得越满，生产者们却越是饿死？何以本应是普遍财富与普遍享受之源的东西，竟成了从事劳动的人们的苦难之源？

===Original===
From all this it follows clearly that private property is accessible only to those who exploit their fellows. The history of humanity shows that this form of property was not that of the first human associations; that it was only at a much later period of their evolution, when the family commenced to emerge from promiscuity, that private property begins to be seen in property common to the clan or tribe. This would prove nothing against its legitimacy if such appropriation had operated otherwise than arbitrarily; we speak of it merely to prove to the bourgeoisie; who have tried to make an argument in its favor by claiming that property has always been what it now is, that that argument no longer has any value in our eyes. For the rest, did those who declaim so much against the Anarchists for demanding expropriation by force distress themselves very much about expropriating the nobility in 1789, and frustrating the peasantry who had made themselves useful by hanging country squires, destroying charter-houses, and seizing seigniorial wealth? Did not the confiscations and sales, either fictitious or at absurd prices, which were made, have for their object the spoliation of the former possessors and the peasants who hoped for a share, in order that the bourgeoisie might monopolize the spoil to their own profit? Did they not make use of the out-and-out right of force, which they masked and sanctioned by legal comedies? Was not this spoliation iniquitous, (admitting that which we demand to be so, which it is not) seeing that it was not done for the benefit of the collectivity, but helped solely to enrich certain traffickers, who straightway hastened to make war upon the peasants that had thrown themselves into the assault upon the castles, by shooting them or treating them as brigands? The bourgeoisie, then, are badly out of place in crying out against robbery when we want to force them to make restitution, for their property is itself but the fruit of robbery.

===Chinese===
由这一切清楚地可见：私有制唯可供剥削其同类者问津。人类的历史表明，这一财产形式并非最初人类联合体的财产形式；只是在他们进化中晚得多的时期，当家庭开始从杂交（promiscuity）状态中脱胎而出时，私有制才在氏族或部落的公有财产中开始显露形迹。倘若这种占有当初并非纯凭专断而行，这一史实本不足以构成对它正当性的否定；我们提及此事，只是为了向资产阶级证明：他们硬说财产从来就是如今这副模样、并借此为它制造论据，而这论据在我们眼中已再无任何价值。再者，那些因无政府主义者要求以强力实行剥夺（expropriation）而高声叫嚷的人们，当年在 1789 年剥夺贵族、挫败那些曾靠绞死乡绅（country squires）、捣毁加尔都西修道院（charter-houses）、劫夺领主财富（seigniorial wealth）而效过力的农民时，可曾有过半点不安？那些或系虚构、或以荒谬价格进行的没收与出售，其目的不正是掠夺旧日的占有者以及指望分得一份的农民，好让资产阶级独占赃物、自肥其利吗？他们所行的，不正是赤裸裸的强力之权，只不过用一场场法律滑稽剧加以掩饰和追认吗？这场掠夺难道不是不义的吗（姑且承认我们的要求是不义的——实则并非如此）——既然它并不是为了集体（collectivity）的利益而行的，反倒只是养肥了一伙奸商，而这伙人转脸便急匆匆向那些投身攻打城堡的农民开战，枪杀他们，或把他们当作土匪处置？可见，当我们想要强制他们归还时，资产阶级却高声控诉抢劫，这实在是极不得体，因为他们的财产本身，便不过是抢劫的果实。

## VI The Family / VI 家庭

===Original===

The Family

Property, the family, authority, have developed along parallel lines; of this there is no doubt. Granting that men united their efforts under the pressure of a common need, of some obstacle to be overcome against which individual efforts had exhausted themselves in vain, it follows that the benefits resulting from this cooperation of forces were shared in common. These associations being temporary, confined to the immediate result to be obtained, there is likewise no doubt that the first human association must have been, as it still is among certain mammiferous animals﻿—some of the anthropoid apes﻿—the nucleus of the family; that is to say, a group of one or a number of females and their young around the strongest male, who, in order to preserve his authority, expelled from the horde all the young males that had become old enough to give him umbrage. But as to whether this authority of the male was complete and assumed sway over every group from the start, it would be rash to decide; for though we find, among savages, examples in which the association having increased in numbers, by the grouping together of several family centers the authority of the male is preponderant, yet by a number of very convincing examples, by a number of customs such as that of couvade it would seem that the mother-right of primogeniture was the first recognized.1 Certain peoples exist in which the children belong to the tribe of the mother; others in which the authority of the male is already recognized, but in which his sister’s children inherit his possessions to the exclusion of his own. This would go to establish a transition from maternal to paternal authority. Another transitional characteristic is this custom of couvade, by which, when the woman is in confinement, it is the man who goes to bed, swallows drugs, and receives congratulations upon his delivery. In this one feels that the man, in order to affirm his authority over his progeny, needs facts to prove his paternity. He would not need them if it were not contested by anterior customs, which have perhaps disappeared, but the memory of which is perpetuated by the practice of retroactive customs that the former gave rise to.

As to the union of men and women, how many times has its form not been changed? At the outset, the very beginnings of humanity, there is no form of marriage; the most complete promiscuity reigns between the sexes; the male cohabits with the first female that comes in his way, who on the other hand accepts or submits to the caresses of all the males that take her. As men develop and become a little less brutal, though a great amount of promiscuity still reigns, they commence to distinguish a primitive sort of relationship. They have not yet learned to distinguish the terms father, mother, brother, sister, very definitely, but unions between tribes having the same totem, or the same common origin, are forbidden; the women, however, continue to belong to all the men, and the latter to all the women, of the tribe. Later on, the male having been acknowledged, the latter begins to recognize certain degrees of consanguinity and affiliation; but marriages continue to take place between brothers and sisters, the son inherits any member of his father’s harem without scruple. A still further step in evolution must be taken before the mother of the heir ceases to be included in the inheritance.

===Chinese===

家庭

私有制（property）、家庭（the family）、权威（authority），三者是沿着平行的路线发展起来的；这一点无可怀疑。且承认：人们是在某种共同需要的压力之下、在个别努力徒然耗尽仍无法克服的障碍面前，才联合起各自的力量的，那么由此而来的结果便是，这种力量合作所产生的利益曾归众人共享。这些联合既是临时性的，又以眼前要取得的成果为限；同样无可怀疑的是，最早的人类联合想必正如它今日在某些哺乳动物——若干种类人猿——中间仍然存在的那样，乃是家庭的核心；也就是说，是一头或数头雌性及其幼崽围聚在最强壮的雄性周围而成的群体；这头雄性为了保全自己的权威，把所有长大到足以令他忌惮的年轻雄性统统逐出原群。然而，雄性的这种权威是否自始便是完备的、是否一开始就支配着每一个群体，贸然断定便失之轻率；因为尽管我们在野蛮人中间发现这样一些例子——联合体人数增多、若干家庭中心聚合为一，雄性的权威便占居上风——但凭着许许多多极有说服力的例证，凭着诸如产翁制（couvade）之类的种种习俗，最早得到承认的似乎仍是母权（mother-right of primogeniture）。1 某些民族那里，子女属于母亲的部落；另一些民族那里，雄性的权威虽已得到承认，但继承他财产的却是他姊妹的子女，他自己的子女反倒被排除在外。这足以证明存在一个从母权向父权过渡的阶段。另一个过渡性的特征便是产翁制这种习俗：按照这种习俗，女人分娩时，卧床、服药、因“分娩”而接受道贺的却是男人。由此可以感到，男人为了申明自己对所生子女的权威，需要有事实来证明自己的父亲身份。倘若他的父亲身份不曾受到更早习俗的争议，他本无需这些事实；那些更早的习俗或许早已消失，但其记忆却借着由它们引发的种种回溯性习俗的奉行而延续了下来。

至于男女的结合，其形式又何尝不曾屡经改变？在初始之际，在人类的发端，不存在任何婚姻形式；两性之间通行的是最彻底的杂交；雄性与迎面而来的第一头雌性交合，而雌性这边，则接受或屈从于所有要她的雄性的爱抚。随着人类逐渐发展、变得稍微不那么野蛮，虽然大量的杂交依然盛行，人们开始辨别出一种原始的亲族关系。他们还未能十分明确地分辨父亲、母亲、兄弟、姊妹这些名分，但拥有同一图腾（totem）或同一共同起源的部落之间的结合已被禁止；然而部落里的女人仍旧属于全体男人，男人也仍旧属于全体女人。再往后，男性既已得到承认，人们便开始辨认出血亲与亲子关系的某些亲等；但兄弟姊妹之间照样通婚，儿子继承父亲妻妾中的任何一个，都毫不迟疑。还须再向前跨出一步，继承人的母亲方才不再被列入继承的财产之中。

===Original===

Observe also that if there be peoples among whom a single male may possess several females, on the other hand there exist some among whom the females possess several males. But these progressive steps, these changes of custom, do not follow logically, one after the other, mutually eliminating each other, as a more complex one appears. Rather these customs are founded one upon the other, fused and confused in such a manner that they can no longer be recognized. Their combinations are so multiplied, customs are so superimposed upon each other, eliminating one here, another there, that it is only by studying the observations of former travelers and still existing tribes that we are enabled to form an approximate idea of human evolution.

From all this, then, it follows that property has rested upon other bases than those upon which it is today supported, has had another method of division, and owes its present destiny only to force, cunning and robbery; for it is quite clear that the family having begun in common association, individual property could not then exist, and, consequently, that what originally belonged to all could not become the property of individuals without some sort of spoliation. In like manner the family has been quite different from what it now is. And the bourgeoisie, which claims that these two institutions rest upon unassailable and immovable bases do not know what they are talking about, since there is no reason why that which has evolved at all should not evolve further. Their affirmation would prove only one thing, which is that if these two institutions were not to progress any more, they must be very near their decadence. For it is a law of life that that which no longer advances, perishes and disintegrates, in order to give birth to other organisms having a period of evolution to run through. And the truth of this axiom is so apparent that the bourgeoisie have been forced to recognize it by admitting divorce as a corrective to marriage, which they would have preferred to maintain indissoluble. True, divorce is applicable only in special cases, can be obtained only by means of a lawsuit, of proceedings without number, and requires the expenditure of a great deal of money, but it is none the less an argument against the stability of the family, since, after having so long repudiated it, they have at length recognized it as necessary, and since it has so powerfully shaken the family by breaking up marriage, which sanctions the family. What more candid confession in favor of free unions could be asked? Does it not become plainly evident that it is useless to seal with a ceremony what another ceremony may unseal? Why have an old woman in pants with a belt around his waist to consecrate a union which three other old women in gowns and caps can declare null and void?

The Anarchists, therefore, reject the institution of marriage. They say that two beings who love each other have no need of the permission of a third in order to go to bed together. From the moment that their wishes so incline them, society has no reason to spy upon them and still less to intervene in the matter. Further the Anarchists say this: “By the mere fact that they have given themselves to each other, the union of a man and a woman is not therefore indissoluble; they are not condemned to finish their days together if they become antipathetic to each other. What they have made of their own free will they can unmake of their own free will. Under the empire of passion, the pressure of desire, they saw each other’s good qualities only; they shut their eyes to each other’s defects; they became united; and behold, their life in common effaces the good qualities, brings out the defects, sharpens the angles which they cannot round off. Is it necessary that these two beings, because in a moment of passionate effervescence they deceived themselves with illusions, should pay a whole lifetime of suffering for the error of a moment, which made them take for a profound and eternal passion what was but the result of an over-excitation of the senses? Nonsense! It is time to return to more healthy notions!”

===Chinese===

还须看到，固然有男子一人可以占有数名女子的民族，另一方面也存在着女子占有数名男子的民族。但这些渐进的步伐、这些习俗的变迁，并不合乎逻辑地一环接一环、随着更复杂形态的出现而互相取代。毋宁说，这些习俗层层相叠、彼此糅杂混同，以致再也无法辨认。它们的组合如此纷繁，习俗如此层层叠压，这里排除一个、那里排除一个，以致唯有借助昔日旅行者的观察记录与今日尚存的部落，我们才能对人类的演化形成一个近似的观念。

由这一切便可断定：私有制曾经依托的基础，不同于它今日所依托的基础；它曾有过另一种分配方式；它今日的命运，全然得自强力、狡诈与劫夺；因为十分清楚：家庭既以共同的联合发端，个人的私有财产在当时便不可能存在；因而，原本属于一切人的东西，若不经某种掠夺，便断然不会变成个人的财产。同样地，家庭也曾与它今日的模样大相径庭。而资产阶级（bourgeoisie）竟宣称这两大制度建立在无可攻讦、不可动摇的基础之上——他们根本不知道自己在说什么，因为凡曾经演化过来的东西，就没有理由不再继续演化。他们的断言只能证明一件事：倘若这两大制度当真不再进步，那它们必定已濒于衰朽。因为这是一条生命法则：不再前进的东西，便归于死亡与解体，好让位于尚须经历一段演化历程的其他有机体。这条公理的真确又如此显豁，以致资产阶级不得不以承认离婚——作为对婚姻的一种矫正——来承认它，尽管他们本宁愿把婚姻维持为不可解除之物。诚然，离婚只在特殊情形下方可适用，只有经由诉讼、经由数不胜数的程序才能获准，还须耗去大笔金钱；但它依然不失为一条反对家庭稳固性的论据，因为他们在长年拒斥之后终究承认了它的必要，也因为它以解除那赋予家庭以合法地位的婚姻，如此有力地撼动了家庭。还能要求什么比这更坦白的、有利于自由结合（free unions）的自供呢？用一项仪式封缄起来的东西、另一项仪式又可以拆封，这难道不是明白地昭示了封缄之无用吗？何必要一个身穿裤子、腰间束着腰带的老太婆来为一桩结合祝圣，而另外三个穿长袍、戴软帽的老太婆却又能宣布这结合无效作废呢？

因此，无政府主义者（Anarchists）拒斥婚姻这一制度。他们说，两个相爱的人，要同床共枕，无需第三者的准许。从他们的意愿如此倾向的那一刻起，社会便没有理由窥伺他们，更没有理由插手其间。无政府主义者更进而这样说：“仅仅由于彼此献出了自身，男人与女人的结合并不因此便不可解除；倘若彼此变得情意相恶，他们也并未被判处必须相守到老。出于自由意志缔结的东西，尽可以出于自由意志加以解除。在激情的支配下、在欲望的催迫下，他们彼此只看见对方的长处，对彼此的缺陷闭上了眼睛；他们结合了；可是看哪，共同的生活抹去了长处，显出了缺陷，把那些无法磨平的棱角磨砺得愈发锋利。这两个人，难道只因在一阵炽情的沸腾里被幻象所惑，便须为这转瞬的过错付出整整一生的苦难——这过错使他们把感官过度亢奋的产物，当成了深邃而永恒的激情？无稽之谈！该回到更健全的观念上来了！”

===Original===

Has not the love of man and woman always been stronger than all laws, all prudery, all the reprobation, which men have sought to attach to the performance of the sexual act? In spite of the blame cast upon the woman who deceives her husband﻿—we do not here speak of the man, who has always known how to take the biggest half in matters of morals﻿—in spite of the role of Pariah which our modest society reserves for the unmarried mother, has it ever, for a single moment, prevented women from making cuckolds of their husbands, or girls from giving themselves to whoever pleases them, or knows how to profit by a moment when the senses speak louder than reason? History and literature talk of nothing else than men and women cuckolded and girls seduced! The creative impulse is the prime motor of man; we hide it, but we yield to its pressure. For the few passionate souls who, weak and timorous, commit suicide together with the beloved being, (sometimes not daring to break with prejudices or not having the moral force to struggle against the obstacles put in their way by custom and the idiocy of imbecile parents), there are countless numbers who mock at prejudices﻿—*in secret*. All these prejudices have only helped to make us frauds and hypocrites; that is all.

Why be stubborn in seeking to regulate what has escaped long centuries of oppression? Rather let us recognize, once for all, that the feelings of mankind elude all regulations, and that entire liberty is necessary in order that they may unfold completely and normally. Let us be less puritanic and we shall be more candid, more moral.

The man who owns property, wishing to transmit the fruit of his rapine to his descendants, (the woman having been considered up till now as inferior, and rather as property than as an associate) it is evident that man has fashioned the family with a view to insuring his supremacy over woman; and to be able to transmit his possessions to his descendants at his death, he had to make the family indissoluble. Based upon interests, and not upon affection, it is plain that some force and sanction were necessary to prevent separations under the shocks occasioned by the antagonism of interests. Now, the Anarchists, who have been accused of wanting to destroy the family, want only to destroy this antagonism; to base the family upon affection in order to render it more permanent. We have never set it up as a principle that a man and woman who desire to finish their days together shall not do so, for the reason that we want unions to be free. We have never said that the father and mother should not bring up their children, because we demand that the liberty of the latter shall be respected, and that they shall no longer be considered as things﻿—property﻿—by their progenitors. Certainly we do want to abolish the legal family; we want men and women to be free to give themselves and take themselves back whenever they please. We want no more of a stupid and uniform law, regulating relations in regard to feelings so complex and varied as those which result from love. If a human being’s feelings be inclined to inconstancy, if his love cannot fix itself upon one object, as those who want to regulate sexual relations pretend, what does it matter to us? What can we do about it? Since up to the present hour repression has succeeded in preventing nothing and has only given us new vices, let us leave human nature free; let it evolve in whatever direction its tendencies and aspirations incline it. It is intelligent enough today to find out what is useful or harmful to it, to discover by experience its proper line of evolution. The law of evolution acting freely, we are certain that it will be the fittest, the best endowed, who will have the chance to survive and reproduce themselves. If, on the other hand, the human tendency be, as we think, inclined to monogamy, the permanent union of two beings who, having met each other, having learned to know and esteem each other, end by becoming one, their union growing intimate and complete, their wills, thoughts, and desires being identical, such will have still less need of laws to constrain them to live together. Will not their own desires be the surest guarantee of the indissolubility of their union?

===Chinese===

男女之爱，难道不始终比人们力图加诸性行为的一切法律、一切假道学、一切非难更有力量吗？尽管欺骗丈夫的女人要遭受责难——我们在这里且不说男人，他们在道德事务上向来精于攫取最大的那一份——尽管我们这个端谨的社会为未婚的母亲安排了帕利亚（Pariah）贱民的角色，这一切可曾有一时一刻阻止过女人给丈夫戴上绿帽、阻止过姑娘们把自己交付给任何中意的人、或任何懂得趁感官的呼声盖过理智的时机加以利用的人？历史与文学谈论的，除了戴绿帽的男人女人、被诱奸的姑娘，还有什么别的！创造的冲动是人的第一推动力；我们把它藏起来，却屈从于它的催迫。有那么少数几个热情的灵魂，因为软弱怯懦，与所爱之人一同自尽（有时是不敢冲破成见，有时是缺少道德力量去对抗习俗和白痴父母的愚蠢在他们路上设置的障碍）；与此相对，则有无数的人讥笑成见——*秘密地*讥笑。这一切成见无非把我们造成了骗子和伪君子；如此而已。

何必执意要去规范那业已逃脱了漫长世纪之压迫的东西呢？毋宁让我们一劳永逸地承认：人类的情感规避一切章法，唯有完全的自由，才能使它们充分而正常地舒展。让我们少一些清教徒气，我们便会更坦率、更有道德。

拥有财产的男人既想把劫夺来的果实传给后代——而女人直到今日始终被视为卑下者，与其说是伙伴，不如说是财产——那么显而易见，男人缔造家庭，为的便是确保自己对女人的至上地位；而为了能在死后把财产传给后代，他又必须使家庭不可解除。家庭既建立在利益而非情感之上，那么显然就需要某种强制与制裁，来防止它在利益对立（antagonism of interests）的冲击之下离散。而无政府主义者——他们一直被指责想要摧毁家庭——想摧毁的只是这种利益对立；他们要把家庭建立在情感之上，好使它更为恒久。我们从来不曾立下这样一条原则，说一对渴望相守终老的男女不得相守终老——只因为我们要结合自由。我们也从不曾说过父亲和母亲不应当抚养他们的子女，尽管我们要求尊重子女的自由，要求他们不再被生身者视作物件——视作财产（property）。固然，我们确实要废除法定家庭；我们要男人和女人都自由，愿意何时献出自己、何时收回自己，全凭他们高兴。我们再也不要一条愚蠢而划一的法律，来规制由爱而生的那样复杂纷繁的情感关系。倘若一个人的情感天性无常，倘若他的爱无法固定于一个对象——一如那些想要规制性关系的人所假装的那样——这与我们何干？我们又能拿它怎么样？既然时至今日，压制从来一无所成、只给我们添了新的恶癖，那就让人性自由吧；让它循其倾向与抱负所指引的方向演化。今日的它已足够聪明，能辨明什么于己有益、什么于己有害，能凭经验发现自己恰当的演化路线。进化法则自由地发挥作用，我们便确信：得到存活繁衍之机会的，将是最适者、禀赋最厚者。反之，倘若人类的禀性如我们所想，倾向于一夫一妻制（monogamy）——倾向于两个存在持久的结合：他们相遇之后，学着相识相重，终至合而为一，结合日臻亲密完满，意志、思想、欲望归于同一——那么这样的结合就更加不需要法律来强制他们共同生活了。他们自身的欲望，难道不正是其结合之不可解除的最可靠保证吗？

===Original===

When men and women no longer feel themselves riveted to each other, if they truly love each other, this love will result in leading them reciprocally to seek to merit the love of the being they have chosen. Feeling that the beloved companion may fly away from the nest the day that he or she no longer finds in it the satisfaction once dreamed of, each will try all means to attach the other completely to him or herself. As with those species of birds, in which, during the mating season, the male arrays himself in new and splendid plumage, in order to appear seductive to the female whose favors he wishes to attract, human creatures will cultivate those moral qualities which will make them beloved and render their society agreeable. Based upon such sentiments, unions will become more indissoluble than the most severe laws or the most violent repression could make them.

We have not attempted a criticism of existing marriage, which is equivalent to the most shameless prostitution:﻿—Business marriages in which affectionate sentiments play no part; marriages of accommodation, arranged, especially among bourgeois families, by the parents, without consulting those who are to be united; unequal marriages in which we see aged semi-paralytics, thanks to their money, uniting their old, decaying carcasses with the freshness and beauty of very young girls, or old hags purchasing with a pile of dollars the complaisance of young pimps, who pay with their skins and a little shame for their thirst of getting rich. Such criticism has been made again and again; what is the use of reverting to it? It suffices to have demonstrated that sexual union has not always been arrayed in the same formalities, and that it cannot attain its greatest dignity save by ridding itself of all fetters. What is the use of seeking for anything else?2

===Chinese===

当男人和女人不再感到彼此铆死在一处时，倘若他们真心相爱，这份爱便会引得他们相互砥砺，去求得自己所选的那个人的爱。感到心爱的伴侣可能在某一天——当巢中再也找不到往日梦寐的满足的那一天——振翅而去，双方便都会千方百计，把对方完完全全系住在自己身上。正如某些鸟类，一到交配季节，雄性便披上新艳华美的羽衣，以便在它想赢得垂青的雌性面前显得妩媚动人，人也将修养那些能使自己被人所爱、使自己的陪伴令人愉悦的道德品质。建立在这样情感之上的结合，将比最严苛的法律或最暴烈的压制所能造成的结合，更加不可解除。

我们并不打算对现存婚姻作一番批判——它无异于最无耻的卖淫：——买卖婚姻，其中全无深情的位置；权宜婚姻，尤其在资产阶级家庭中由父母一手安排，全然不问将要结合的当事人；不对等的婚姻，其中我们见到年迈的半瘫者，倚仗金钱，把自己衰老朽烂的躯壳同妙龄少女的青春美貌结合起来，或者老妖婆们用一堆美元买下年轻拆白党的殷勤——这些人为着发财的渴念，付出自己的皮肉和一点点耻辱。这样的批判人们做过一遍又一遍；何苦再来重提？只要已经证明：性结合并不总是披着同样的一套仪式，而且唯有挣脱一切枷锁，它才能达致它最高的尊严——这就够了。还到别处寻求什么呢？2

## VII Authority / VII 权威

===Original===
Authority

The question of property is so mixed up with that of authority that in treating of the former in its special chapter we could not do otherwise than treat of the origin and evolution of the latter. We shall not therefore return to these, but shall concern ourselves only with the present period, with the authority which is claimed to be based upon universal suffrage, the law of the majority. As we have seen, the divine origin of property and authority being sapped, the bourgeoisie has had to seek a new and more solid basis for them. Having themselves destroyed the basis of divine right, and helped to combat that of the right of force, they sought to substitute therefore that of money, by causing the chambers to be elected under the quit-rent regime, that is to say by a certain category of individuals who paid the highest taxes. Later there was some question of including “qualifications;” this came from the excluded fraction of the bourgeoisie. But all that could be of no long duration. From the moment that authority was put under discussion it lost its strength, and those who had hitherto taken no part in the choice of their masters, were not slow to demand the right to give their opinion upon this choice. The bourgeoisie, who feared the people, did not want to make any concession; they had the power, they wanted to keep it. In order to obtain universal suffrage the workers had to revolt. The bourgeois members whom they carried into power were eager to trick them out of this newly-acquired right, to cut the claws of the monster which they thought would devour them. It was only in the long run, through seeing it in operation, that they came to understand that it was not dangerous to their privileges, that it was but a fiddle upon which one must know how to play, and that this famous weapon for enforcing demands, which the workers believed themselves to have acquired, (they had paid for it with their blood) was but a perfected instrument of authority, which enslaved those who made use of it at the very moment they expected to emancipate themselves.

Indeed what is universal suffrage if not the right of the governed to choose their master, the right of choosing the rod to be whipped with? The voter is sovereign﻿—so far as to be able to choose his master! But he has not the right to dispense with him; for the one that his neighbor will have chosen will be his. From the moment he deposits his ballot in the box he has signed his abdication; he has no more to do but bend to the caprices of the masters of his choice; they will make the laws, will apply them to him, and throw him into prison if he resist.

===Chinese===
权威

私有制（property）的问题与权威（authority）的问题纠结如此之深，以致前文在私有制的专章里论述它时，我们不得不连带论及权威的起源与演变。因此，这里不再回头重述，而只关注当前的时期，关注那种据称建立在普选（universal suffrage）——多数法则（the law of the majority）——之上的权威。如我们所见，私有制与权威的神圣来源一经挖断，资产阶级便不得不为它们另觅一个新的、更牢固的基础。他们既亲手摧毁了君权神授（divine right）的基础，又协力打击过强力之权（right of force）的基础，便进而想用金钱的基础取而代之：他们让议院在免役租制（quit-rent regime）下经选举产生——也就是说，只由缴纳最高税额的某一类人来选举。后来又有人议起把“选举资格”纳入考量；这呼声出自资产阶级中被排除在外的那一部分人。但这一切都维持不了多久。权威一经推上讨论的台面，便失去了它的力量；而那些直到那时为止在选择主人一事上向来无份的人，也随即起来，要求就这一选择发表意见的权利。畏惧人民的资产阶级不愿作任何让步；权力在他们手里，他们就要牢牢抓住。工人为了争得普选，不得不举行起义。工人捧上台的那些资产阶级议员，急欲把他们从这新到手的权利里骗出去，急欲剪除这头他们以为会把自己吞掉的怪兽的利爪。只是到了后来，眼看它长期实际运作，他们才明白它并不危及他们的特权，它不过是一把提琴，只消懂得如何拉奏便是；而这件工人自以为已经到手的著名的、用以强制实现诉求的武器——他们是用血把它换来的——不过是权威的一件更臻完善的工具，就在使用者指望借它解放自己的那一刻，它奴役了他们。

其实，普选若不是被治者（the governed）选择自己主人的权利，不是挑选自己挨哪根鞭子的权利，又是什么？选民是主权者——主权到竟能挑选自己的主人！可他无权不要主人；因为邻人挑中的那一个，同样要做他的主人。从他把选票投进票箱的那一刻起，他便签署了自己的退位书；此后他别无他事，只有俯首听命于自己选定的那些主人的任性摆布；他们制定法律，把法律加诸他身，他若反抗，便把他投进监狱。

===Original===
We do not wish to institute a trial of universal suffrage at this point, nor to examine all the correctives, all the improvements that different people have wished to bring to bear upon it, to obviate the caprices of the elected and secure the sovereignty of the voter by giving him the means of forcing the former to keep his promises. It would lead us too far, and is, besides, of no importance to us, since we wish to prove that there ought not to be a majority-law any more than a divine right, and that the individual ought not to be subjected to any other rule than that of his own will. And, moreover, in analyzing the operation of universal suffrage we shall come to the proof that it is not even the majority that governs, but a very small minority, issuing from a second minority, which is itself but a minority chosen from among the governed masses. That women and children, who submit equally to the laws, should be excluded from the right of sharing in the vote, is purely arbitrary. If we deduct further those who for one reason or another do not make use of this “right,” we find ourselves in the presence of a first minority, recognized most arbitrarily as the only ones fit to choose masters for all. In the second place, it is theoretically the majority which on election day decides who is elected out of this original circumscription; but practically the choice of the voters is divided among six, eight, ten, and often more candidates, not counting those who, not finding their opinions represented among the crowd of candidates, vote contrary to their ideas. The successful candidate is, therefore, once more but the product of a second minority. In the third place, those elected being once assembled, it is again the majority which, theoretically always, is supposed to decide among them; but here again opinions being divided into groups and subgroups innumerable, it follows in practice that small cliques of ambitious persons, standing between the extreme parties, decide the vote by lending their voices to those that offer them the most for it. From the little just said it is apparent that the pretended sovereignty of the voter comes down to a very small affair; but it must be observed that in order not to befog the reader we have simplified our criticism, and supposed that every voter acted logically and conscientiously. But if we include in the account all the intrigues, jobbing, ambitious calculations; if we take note that before being ratified the laws must come before another assembly, the senate, which in turn is elected by another category of voters; if we take into account that the legislative power is composed of five hundred and some odd deputies, and that each voter casts his ballot for only one, and that consequently his will goes for less than one five-hundredth of the general will, still further reduced by the veto of the senate, we shall end by perceiving that individual sovereignty enters in so infinitesimal a quantity into the national sovereignty that at last we do not find it at all.

Yet all this is still of minor importance. Universal suffrage has a still more disastrous effect, viz.: that it gives birth to the reign of nonentities and mediocrities, as we shall prove. Every new idea in advance of its epoch is, by the very fact of such advance, always in the minority at the start. Very few and far between are the minds open enough to adopt and defend it. This is an acknowledged truth, and the conclusion is that people with truly broad and intelligent ideas are always in the minority. The bulk of the masses professes average current ideas; it is they who compose the majority; it is they who will choose the representative, who, in order to be elected, will take good care not to offend the prejudices of his constituents or to shock received opinions. On the contrary, in order to succeed in collecting as many people as possible under his banner, he must round off his sharp corners and select a stock of commonplaces to get off before those whose suffrages he covets. That he may not frighten them, he must outdo them in stupidity. The more flat, mediocre, and insipid he is, the more chance he has of being elected.

===Chinese===
我们无意在此对普选开庭审判，也无意逐一检视各方人士想要加在它身上的一切矫正、一切改良——那些旨在杜绝当选者的任性、并赋予选民迫使当选者信守诺言的手段、以保障选民主权的办法。那会把我们引得太远，况且对我们无关紧要；因为我们要证明的是：正如不该有君权神授一样，也不该有什么多数法则；个人除了自己的意志之外，不应当服从任何别的规则。再者，在剖析普选的运作时，我们反倒会证明：进行统治的甚至不是多数，而是一个极小的少数——它出自第二个少数，而这第二个少数本身，又不过是从被治大众中选出的一个少数。同样服从法律的妇女与儿童，竟被排除在分享投票的权利之外，这纯属武断。倘若再扣除那些因这样那样的缘故并不行使这项“权利”的人，我们面对的便是第一个少数——它被最武断地认定为唯一有资格为全体挑选主人的人。其次，理论上，是多数在选举之日从这最初划定的圈子里决定谁当选；可实际上，选民的选择分散于六个、八个、十个乃至更多的候选人，更不必说那些在候选人堆里找不到自己意见的代表、从而违背本愿去投票的人。因此，当选者又一次不过是第二个少数的产物。第三，当选人一旦齐聚一堂，理论上仍然应当由他们之中的多数来作决定；可是这里意见又分成无数的集团与子集团，于是实践中，是几小帮野心家夹在各极端政党之间，把嗓音卖给出价最高的人，从而左右表决的结果。单凭方才这一点点论述已然可见，选民那种号称的主权归结下来是何等微末的一件事；但必须指出，为了不把读者搅得眼花，我们把批评简化了，假定每个选民都行事合乎逻辑、出于良知。可是，倘若把种种阴谋、勾营与野心的算计都记入账内；倘若我们注意到法律在获得批准之前还须提交另一个议院——参议院（senate）——而参议院又由另一类选民选出；倘若我们考虑到立法权由五百几十名议员组成，而每个选民只能投一人一票，因此他的意志在总意志中所占尚不足五百分之一，再经参议院的否决削而又削，我们终将看清：个人主权渗入国民主权的份量微乎其微，微到末了我们竟全然找不到它。

然而这一切还只是次要的弊害。普选还有一个更灾难性的后果，即：它孕育出空洞无物之辈与平庸之徒的统治——这一点我们将加以证明。每一种超前于自身时代的新观念，恰恰因其超前，起先必定居于少数。胸怀足够开阔、能采纳并捍卫它的头脑，寥寥无几、散落各处。这是公认的道理；由此可得结论：真正怀有博大而明智的观念的人，永远居于少数。大众的主体信奉的是寻常的流行意见；构成多数的正是他们；将要挑选代表的也正是他们，而那代表为了当选，必定小心翼翼，既不触犯自己选民的成见，也不惊动世人接受的定论。恰恰相反，为了尽可能多地把人收拢到自己旗下，他必须磨圆自己的棱角，备好一套陈词滥调，在他垂涎其选票的人面前一一抛出。为了不吓着他们，他必须在愚蠢上胜过他们。他越是平板、平庸、乏味，当选的机会就越大。

===Original===
If the workings of all manner of groups be thoroughly examined﻿—committees, representative congresses, associations for mutual help, societies of artists, litterateurs, etc.﻿—you will always see the offices in these hierarchic organizations, elected by universal suffrage, held by persons who, setting aside their ambitious desire of showing themselves off, getting themselves talked about, or creating a situation at the expense of their colleagues, and a certain capacity for intrigue, are the most mediocre of the lot. For no original mind that occupies itself solely with the realization of its ideal, can do otherwise than clash with all those﻿—and they are legion﻿—who follow the laws of holy routine. Everybody cries out, “Look at the jackass!” He who seeks the truth and would make it prevail, has no time to stoop to the shabby wire-pulling behind the scenes. He will surely be beaten in the electoral lists by him who, having no original ideas, accepting those received by the greater number, will have less trouble in insinuating his projecting angles (which he has not) in a manner to offend no one. The more one wishes to please people the more the average line of ideas adopted must be disembarrassed of new and original conceptions, and consequently the aforesaid line will be found trite, tame, and mediocre. This is all there is of universal suffrage﻿—a sonorous ass’s skin, giving out nothing but noise under the blows of those who wish to make it speak!

But though authority is discussed, jeered at, lashed, it is, unfortunately, far from having disappeared from our customs. People are so used to being led by a string that they would imagine themselves lost the moment there was no longer anybody to keep them tied. They are so accustomed to seeing the gendarme’s cap, the belted paunch of the mayor, the meddling and official insolence of the bureaucracy, the sorry-looking countenances of judge and policeman, appear in their lives at every turn, that they have reached the point of becoming accustomed to these filthy promiscuities, considering them as things which are certainly disagreeable, on which, when occasion offers, they never miss playing a dirty trick with satisfaction, but which they cannot imagine disappearing without humanity’s being dislocated at once! Strange contradiction of the human mind! Men submit to this authority with reluctance, they scoff at it, violate it when they can, and believe themselves lost when anyone talks of doing away with it. A matter of habit, it seems!

But this prejudice is so much the more illogical, if we may use the term, so much the more stupid, when the ideal of each individual in regard to “good” government, is to have one which he would have the chance to cashier the moment it tried to prevent him from acting as he pleased. It was to flatter this ideal that the bourgeoisie invented universal suffrage.

===Chinese===
倘若彻底考察一下各类团体的运作——委员会、代表大会、互助会、艺术家与文人的社团等等——你总会看到，这些等级制组织中经普选产生的职位，握在这样一些人手里：撇开他们那种渴望出风头、惹人谈论、或靠牺牲同仁为自己谋一个位置的野心，以及某种玩弄阴谋的本领不谈，他们正是全场最平庸的人。因为，凡是独运匠心的头脑，既然一心只顾实现自己的理想，便免不了同那一切奉行神圣常规之法则的人冲突——而这等人成群结伙。人人都要叫嚷：“瞧这头蠢驴！”寻求真理并想使之获胜的人，没有工夫俯就去干幕后那些下作的钻营勾当。他在选举名单上必定败给另一种人：后者没有独创之见，接受多数人认可的意见，也就更容易把自己那些（根本不存在的）嶙峋棱角，以不得罪任何人的方式悄悄安放进去。一个人越想讨好众人，他所采行的中道思想路线就越必须清除新颖独创的观念；于是上述那条路线便只能是陈腐、驯顺而平庸的。普选的全部内容不过如此——一张敲起来铮铮作响的驴皮，在那些想让它说话的人的敲打之下，除了噪音什么也发不出来！

然而，权威尽管被人议论、讥嘲、鞭笞，不幸的是，它远没有从我们的习俗中消失。人们太习惯于被一根绳子牵着走，以至于一旦再没有人牵着，他们便会觉得自己迷失了。他们太惯于在生活中随时随地撞见宪兵的帽子、市长那束着腰带的便便大腹、官僚体制那种好管闲事的官样无礼、法官与警察那副愁苦面孔，以至于已经到了把这类污七八糟的混杂共处视作固然讨厌、有机会时也从不放过偷偷对它使一回坏、聊以快意，却又无法想象它们一旦消失而人类竟不立刻散架的地步！人的头脑，何等离奇的矛盾！人们对这种权威俯首得不情愿，讥笑它，能违犯时就违犯它，可一旦有人谈到废除它，他们又自以为末日将至。看来不过是习惯使然！

而这一成见，倘若可以用这个词的话，愈发地不合逻辑，也愈发地愚蠢，因为每个人关于“好”政府的理想，无非是要有一个一旦试图阻止他随心行事、他就有机会立刻把它撤掉的政府。资产阶级正是为了迎合这一理想，才发明了普选。

===Original===
If the republic has enjoyed so much credit among the workers; if, after so many deceptions, universal suffrage is still considered by the governed as a means of enfranchisement, it is because they have been made to believe that by changing the men in power they could change the system of exploitation which oppresses us into a system from which welfare and felicity for all would result. Profound error, which allows the intriguing to lead the workers astray, in pursuit of illusory reforms, incapable of bringing about any change in their situation, and accustoming them to expect everything from a change of personnel in this machine for oppression called the State;﻿—an error which, in every revolution, has permitted schemers to juggle away popular victories, to install themselves in the sinecures of those who have been swept away by the revolutionary tempest, and to form a new caste of exploiters by creating around them new interests, which, once established, have succeeded in imposing their authority, reducing to silence those who had had the naivete to carry them to the pinnacle of power!

What an abyss of contradictions is the human mind! If one discusses with individuals even slightly intelligent, they will readily agree that if all men were reasonable there would be no need of government. They themselves could get along easily without it. But unfortunately all men are not reasonable; some would abuse their strength to oppress others, to live at others’ expense and do nothing. To guard against these inconveniences some authority is necessary “to keep them straight.” Which in concrete terms comes back to saying that, taken in a lump, people are too bad to come to an understanding among themselves, but that, taken individually or in fractions, they know how to govern others, and that we must make haste to put the power into their hands, in order that they may enforce their will upon all. O unhappy logic! How human reasoning doth trip thee up!

So long as there are persons to give commands, will they not necessarily be in antagonism with those they command? Will not those in power, if they be sincere, have ideas of their own to further? And these ideas, though they may be good, may also be very bad. Drowned in the mass, they will remain without power; with authority in the hands of those who profess them, they will be thrust upon those who reject them. And the more sincere the individuals in power the more pitiless would they be against those who should revolt against their way of seeing things, being convinced that they were working for the good of humanity.

===Chinese===
共和国之所以在工人中间享有那么高的声望；普选之所以在屡屡失望之后仍被被治者视为一种解放的手段，是因为有人使他们相信：只要换掉当权的人，就能把压迫着我们的剥削制度，改换成一种会给所有人带来福祉与幸福的制度。深刻的错误！正是它，让钻营之徒得以把工人引入歧途，去追逐那些丝毫改变不了他们处境的虚幻改革，并让他们习惯于把一切指望寄托在这台名为国家（the State）的压迫机器的人事更迭上；——也正是这个错误，在每一次革命中，都让阴谋家得以把人民的胜利变戏法般骗走，得以把自身安插进被革命风暴扫倒者的肥缺，得以在自己周围再造新的利益，从而形成一个新的剥削者等级；这些利益一旦确立，便成功地强加了它们的权威，叫那些天真到把他们捧上权力顶峰的人哑口无言！

人的头脑真是一座矛盾的深渊！倘若同多少有点头脑的人讨论，他们都会欣然承认：只要人人讲道理，便不需要政府；他们自己也完全可以没有政府而照样过活。可惜人并不个个讲道理；总有人要滥用力量去压迫他人，靠他人过活而自己无所事事。为防范这些弊端，便需要某种权威来“管束他们”。而落到实处，这无非等于说：人们笼而统之地看，坏得彼此无法达成谅解；可是单个地或成帮地看，他们却懂得如何统治别人；于是我们必须赶紧把权力塞到他们手里，好叫他们把自己的意志强加于全体。不幸的逻辑啊！人的推理是怎样绊倒了你！

只要还有发号施令的人，他们岂不必然同受其号令的人处于对立之中？当权者倘若真诚，岂会没有自己想要推进的主张？而这些主张尽管可能是好的，也可能很坏。淹没在大众之中时，它们始终无权无势；一旦权威落入信奉它们的人手中，它们便要硬塞给那些拒斥它们的人。而且，当权的个人越是真诚，对待那些起而反抗其看问题方式的人就越是无情，因为他们深信自己是在为人类的善而效力。

===Original===
In the preceding chapter we saw that our political slavery is determined by our economic situation. We have soldiers, judges, ministers, etc., because we have bankers and proprietors; the one entails the other. If we succeed in overthrowing those who exploit us in the workshop, if we succeed in ridding ourselves of those who have got us by the entrails, there will no longer be any need of the force which protects them; it will have no more reason for existence. In fact there is a necessity for government, for laws, for deputies to make these laws and a magistracy to apply them, for a police-force to maintain the decisions of the magistracy, because those who possess need some force to defend what they have seized against the claims of those they have dispossessed.

But **the worker**﻿—what has *he* to defend? What matters to him all this governmental paraphernalia, the expense for whose maintenance he alone bears, without deriving any profit therefrom, and which is there solely to teach him that he has no rights save that of starving in the midst of the abundance he has created? In the somber days of revolt, when misery grown more intense urges the workers into the street en masse, it is again these “social” institutions which stand before them and bar their route to the future. We must, therefore, destroy them, and take good care to reconstitute no new aristocracy, which could have but one purpose: to enjoy the most and the quickest at the expense of its protégés. What matters the choice of the hand that strikes you? It is not to be struck at all, that one should aim at! Let us not forget that whatever the name in which the new authority clothes itself, however benign it may seek to appear, whatever be the amendments it proposes, whatever be the mode of recruiting its personnel, we shall none the less have to encounter the following dilemma: Either its decisions will have the force of law and be obligatory upon all, in which case all our existing institutions will be needed to apply them and enforce respect for them﻿—hence renunciation of liberty﻿—or people will remain free to discuss governmental decisions, conform to them if they please, or send authority hunting a job if it annoys them﻿—in which case liberty remains intact, but the government is useless though remaining a fetter and a menace!

Conclusion: **No government.**

===Chinese===
在上一章我们看到，我们的政治奴役是由我们的经济处境决定的。我们之所以有士兵、法官、部长等等，是因为我们有银行家和有产者；有此一方，必致彼一方。倘若我们成功地推翻了工场里剥削我们的人，倘若我们成功地摆脱了那些攥住我们五脏六腑的人，那么保护他们的那种强力便再也没有必要；它将再无存在的理由。事实上，之所以需要政府，需要法律，需要制定这些法律的议员、适用这些法律的司法当局（the magistracy）、维持司法当局之裁决的警察，是因为占有者需要某种强力，来保卫他们攫取到手的东西，以对抗被他们剥夺者的索还之求。

可是**工人**——*他*有什么可捍卫的？这套政府的全套排场，与他何干？维持它的全部费用唯独由他一人承担，他却从中得不到任何好处；这排场摆在那里，仅仅是为了教他知道：除了在他亲手创造的丰裕之中挨饿，他没有任何权利。在阴沉的起义日子里，当加深了的苦难驱使工人们成群涌上街头，又是这些“社会”机构横在他们面前，堵住他们通往未来的去路。因此，我们必须摧毁它们，并且千万留神，切莫再重建任何新的贵族统治——它只可能有一个目的：靠牺牲被保护者，最快最多地捞取享受。打你的那只手由谁来挑，有什么要紧？人应当力争的，是根本不挨打！且让我们不要忘记：无论新的权威给自己披上什么名号，无论它力图显得多么温良，无论它提出什么样的修正案，无论它以什么方式招募自己的人员，我们终究都免不了要面对如下的两难：要么，它的决定具有法律效力，对全体都有约束力，这样的话，就需要动用我们现有的一切机构来执行这些决定、强制人们尊重它们——于是便是自由的放弃；要么，人们仍旧自由，可以讨论政府的决定，愿意遵从便遵从，权威若惹人厌烦，便打发它另谋差事——这样的话，自由完好无损，可政府虽仍旧是一道锁链、一重威胁，却已毫无用处！

结论：**不要政府。**

## VIII The Magistracy / VIII 司法当局
===Original===
The Magistracy

Authority, as we have seen, springs from that right which arrogates force to itself. But man having widened the field of his thought it became necessary for this authority to justify its existence. Combining with religious sentiment and the support of the priests, it claimed to be of divine origin, assumed the form of an exclusive caste, and eventually succeeded in resisting the brutal power of the king and the nobles: thus the magistracy was founded. And when the bourgeoisie seized the power, in 1789, they took care not to destroy this pillar of social order. (Moreover, did not the nobility of gowns belong much more properly to the bourgeoisie than to the nobility of the sword?) They were thus relieved of the task of searching for a mode of recruit more in accord with the new aspirations.

Divine right having gotten a powerful shock in the decapitation of Louis XVI, the magistracy could not continue to lean upon the said right without the risk of likewise passing under this equalizing leveler. Hence they invented, or rather deified, the “law.” The magistracy was constituted its guardian and incorruptible administrator, so-called. The trick was done; the most redoubtable and necessary institution for the defense of privilege succeeded in preserving itself, and becoming the priestess of this new entity, the law, created by the new masters. The submission of France to the regime of the “law” is, in fact, one of the conquests of ’89 whose benefits the bourgeois historians are exceedingly fond of setting forth. The codification of authority, according to these, its censer-bearers, had the immediate effect of legitimizing the most shameless arbitrariness. From then on Frenchmen were all to be equal; the people no longer had anything to demand. Thereafter there was to be but one master, before whom, it is true, all had to bow, which had the effect of equalizing their situations. This master was the “law.” But we who are not satisfied with words, when we try to find out what the workers have gained by this transformation, see that they have got just one more duping. In fact, in the time of the absolute monarchy, when the king and the nobles constrained the peasant to serve them, there was no way of deceiving oneself about it; the formula “for such is our good pleasure” showed whence they derived their rights: they claimed them by the right of the sword only, counting much more upon that than the divine will; consequently it was upon force that their claim was based. Their orders were obeyed, their claims were submitted to; but because the people were in no condition to resist them. There were at least no imbeciles to come and say to us﻿—repeating the phrases of the interested﻿—that we must obey because it is “the law,” and it is the duty of everyone to conform thereto until it be changed.

===Chinese===
司法当局

权威（authority），如我们所见，发源于那种把强力僭据为己有的权利。但人类既已拓宽了思想的疆域，这种权威便不得不为自己的存在寻找辩护。它与宗教情感、与教士的支持相结合，自称源出神意，取得了排他等级的形式，终于成功地顶住了国王与贵族的残暴权力：司法当局（the magistracy）便是这样建立起来的。而当资产阶级（bourgeoisie）于 1789 年夺得权力时，他们小心翼翼，不去摧毁这根社会秩序的支柱。（何况，长袍贵族（nobility of gowns）难道不是本来就更多属于资产阶级，而较少属于佩剑贵族（nobility of the sword）吗？）如此一来，他们便省去了另觅一种更合乎新愿望的补员方式的麻烦。

君权神授（divine right）之说既已在路易十六（Louis XVI）身首异处之时遭到沉重一击，司法当局若还要倚傍上述权利，便难免同样被送到这把把众人拉平的铡刀之下。于是他们发明了“法律”，或者不如说，把“法律”奉为神明。司法当局于是被确立为这“法律”的守护人，立为它那所谓的不可收买的管理者。把戏就此得逞；这件为捍卫特权所最可畏、最不可少的机构，成功地保全了自己，并当上了这个新实体的女祭司——这“法律”，乃新主子们所造。法国归顺于“法律”之治，实际上是“‘89 年”诸项征服之一，而这些征服的好处，正是资产阶级史家极其乐于铺陈的。依这些替权威捧香炉的人之见，权威的法典化立即收到的效果，便是把最无耻的专断合法化。从此，法国人全都平等了；人民再没有什么可要求的了。此后只应有一个主子；诚然，在这主子面前人人皆须躬身，而这恰好收到了使众人处境平等的效果。这主子便是“法律”。但我们这些不以辞句为满足的人，一旦设法查明工人由这场变革究竟得到了什么，便看出他们不过是又受了一场愚弄。事实上，在绝对君主制时代，当国王与贵族强制农民为他们服役时，谁也无法在这件事上自欺；“朕意如此”（for such is our good pleasure）这一套语，表明了他们的权利从何而来：他们只是凭刀剑之权主张这些权利，而且对刀剑的倚仗远过于对神意的倚仗；因此，其权利要求所依托的正是强力。他们的命令得到服从，他们的索取得到顺从；但那是因为人民全然无力抵抗。至少那时还没有白痴跑来对我们说——重复着利益相关者的套话——我们必须服从，因为这是“法律”，而且在法律更改之前，人人都有遵从的义务。
===Original===
If it be admitted that the law may change it is thereby presumed that the law may become retrogressive; and to acknowledge that, is to admit that from its very nature it may injure someone, for there are always individuals in advance of their generation. The law, then, is not just; it has not that respectable character with which men have sought to invest it. If this law injures my interests or violates my liberty why should I be compelled to obey it, and what is the unalterable compact which can justify these abuses? In scientific matters when the savants after great research and labor at length formulate what is called a natural law, it is not because a majority or “chamber,” composed of persons believing themselves superior to the rest of mortals, has decided, by virtue of its members’ will, that natural forces were ordered to conform to such or such a mode of evolution. We should laugh in the face of the imbecile who would make such a pretense. When a natural law is proclaimed, it is because it has been discovered that if a certain phenomenon be produced, if a certain chemical combination had been effected, it is by virtue of such and such a force, or the existence of such and such affinities; the environment in which the phenomenon took place being given, it was impossible for it to be otherwise. Given forces set in motion under given conditions produce given results; this is mathematical. Therefore the newly-discovered law does not come upon the scene to govern the phenomenon, but to explain its causes. These laws may be discovered, doubted, and even denied; the divers substances which compose our earth will none the less continue to combine according to their properties or affinities, the earth will turn, without any force being needed to protect the evolution thereof, or punish, those who might want to “violate the laws.”

In our society it is otherwise. These laws seem to be made to be violated; because those who made them consulted only their personal preferences, the interests of those whom they represented, and the average degree of moral evolution in their epoch, without taking into account the character, tendencies, and affinities of those who were to submit to them﻿—which, moreover, would be impossible, the diversity of individual character and tendencies being given. Each estate has its laws; nor can there be any single and universal law in sociology, as there is in physics, under penalty of its becoming arbitrary and inapplicable. In fact there is not, in our society, a single law which does not injure some of its members, either in their material interests or their ideas; not a single law which each triumphant party has not been able to turn against its adversaries. Power once obtained, every illegal party becomes legal, for it is that party which, through its creatures, administers the “law.” We may then conclude that the law being nothing but the will of the strongest, one is obliged to obey it only when too weak to resist it; that nothing really legitimizes it, and that this famous “legality” is only a question of more or less force. So when these rogues oppose the workers with their supreme argument, “legality,” the latter may laugh in their faces and ask if anyone ever came to consult the toilers about the making of those laws. And even if the people should have adhered to these laws for a time, the latter could have no effectiveness except so long as those who accepted them continued to believe them useful, and were willing to conform to them. It would be funny if, under the pretext that at a given moment of our life we had agreed to a certain line of conduct, we were forced to adopt it for the rest of our existence, without being able to modify it, because to do so would be to displease a certain number of persons who, for one cause or another finding profit for themselves in the existing order, would like to crystallize the present condition. But what is more ridiculous still, is to desire to subject us to the laws of past generations, the pretense that we should believe we owe respect and obedience to fancies which it pleased certain nincompoops to codify and set up as laws fifty years ago! The presumption of wanting to enslave the present to the conceptions of the past!

At this point we hear the recriminations of all the makers of laws and those that get their living out of them; they naively fall into line and cry out with the others that society could not exist if there were no longer any laws; that people would be cutting each other’s throats if they had no tutelary authority to keep them in fear and respect of acquired rank and condition. Later we shall see that, in spite of law and coercion, crimes continue to be committed; that the laws are powerless to repress or prevent them, since they are the result of the vicious organization which governs us; and that, consequently, we must not seek to maintain or to modify the laws, but to change the social system.

===Chinese===
倘若承认法律可以更改，那便等于认定法律也可能变得倒退；而承认了这一点，也就是承认它就其本性而言可能伤害某些人，因为总有走在自己时代前面的个人。可见法律并不公正；它并不具有人们力图赋予它的那种可敬品性。倘若这部法律损害我的利益或侵犯我的自由，为什么我必须被迫服从它？又是哪一纸永不可易的契约，能为这些滥用辩护？在科学领域，当学者们经过大量研究和辛劳，终于表述出一条所谓的自然规律（natural law）时，那并不是因为一个由自以为高于其余凡人的成员组成的多数或“议院”，曾凭其成员的意志裁定：自然力应遵照这样或那样的演化方式。谁若发此狂言，我们定会当面耻笑这个白痴。一条自然规律之所以被宣布，是因为人们已经发现：某一现象若经产生、某一化合若经实现，乃是凭借某某之力，或某某亲和力（affinity）的存在；既定产生该现象的环境，它便不可能另成样子。既定的力在既定条件下发生作用，产生既定的结果；这是数学般确定的。因此，新发现的规律并不是登台来统辖现象，而是来解释现象的成因。这些规律尽可以被发现、被怀疑、甚至被否认；构成我们地球的种种物质，仍将照样按其性质或亲和力继续化合，地球仍将运转，无需任何强力来保护这一演化过程，也无需惩罚那些想“违犯规律”的人。

在我们这个社会里，情形便不同了。这些法律仿佛生来就是供人违犯的；因为立法者所咨询的，只是自己的个人偏好、他们所代表者的利益，以及他们那个时代道德演化的平均程度，全不顾及将受其支配的人们的性情、趋向与亲和力——何况这也根本不可能，因为个人性情与趋向本来就千差万别。每个等级各有其法律；社会学里也不可能有物理学里那种单一而普适的规律，否则它就难免专断而不适用。事实上，在我们这个社会里，没有一部法律不损害其某些成员——或损其物质利益，或损其观念；没有一部法律不曾被每一个得胜的政党掉转头来打击自己的对手。权力一旦到手，一切非法的政党便都成了合法的，因为正是这个政党在通过它的爪牙执掌“法律”。于是可以断言：法律无非是最强者的意志，人只在弱得无力抵抗时才不得不服从它；没有任何东西真正使它合法化，这著名的“合法性”（legality）不过是强力多寡的问题。所以，当这帮无赖祭出他们的至高论据——“合法性”——来对付工人时，工人尽可当面耻笑他们，并且问一句：可曾有人来征询过劳动者，问他们同不同意制定这些法律？退一步说，即便人民曾一度归附这些法律，它们也只在接受者继续信其有益、并愿意遵从的期间内才有效力。倘若借口我们一生中某个时刻曾赞成过某种行为准则，便强迫我们此后终身奉行、不得更改，只因为更改便要开罪于某些人——这些人出于这样那样的缘故，从现存秩序里找到了自己的好处，巴不得把现状凝固起来——那才滑稽呢。但更荒唐的，还是想把我们置于往代人的法律之下，妄想我们应当相信自己负有敬意与服从的义务——对五十年前某些蠢货一时高兴编纂起来、奉为法律的那些奇想！竟想把现在奴役于过去的种种观念之下，这是何等的狂妄！

话说到这里，我们便听见全体立法者以及靠法律吃饭的人们的一片责难；他们天真地随声附和，跟着旁人一起叫嚷：一旦不再有法律，社会就无法存在；人们若没有一种监护性的权威（tutelary authority）令其畏惧、令其敬重既得的等级与地位，便会互相割断喉咙。往后我们将看到：尽管有法律与强制，罪行仍在不断发生；法律既镇压不了、也防止不了罪行，因为罪行正是统治着我们的那个邪恶组织的产物；因此，我们不该谋求维持或修改法律，而该谋求改变社会制度。
===Original===
But what makes us still more indignant is that certain persons are audacious enough to set themselves up as judges of others. So long as authority leaned upon its divine source, so long as justice passed for an emanation from God, we can understand that those invested with authority should have believed themselves peculiar beings, endowed by the divine will with a portion of its omnipotence and infallibility, and should have imagined themselves fit to distribute rewards and punishments to the herd of vulgar mortals. But in our century of science and free criticism, when it is recognized that all men are kneaded out of the same dough, subject to the same passions, the same caprices, the same mistakes, today when an agonizing divinity no longer comes to animate with its breath the ever fallible reason of mortals, we ask ourselves how it comes that there are men ignorant, enough, or presumptuous enough, to dare to assume in cold blood and with deliberate intent the terrible responsibility of taking away another man’s life or any portion of his liberty. When, in the most ordinary affairs of daily life we are most of the time unable to succeed in analyzing not only the causes which prompt our immediate neighbors to act but very often the true motives of our own acts, how can anybody have the self-sufficiency to believe himself capable of disentangling the truth in an affair of which he knows neither the beginning, nor the actors, nor the motives which prompted their actions, and which comes before the tribunal only after being magnified, commented upon, distorted by the misrepresentations of those who participated in it in any way whatsoever or, more frequently, have heard of it only through the repetitions of others?

You, who pose as severe and infallible judges of this man who has killed or robbed, do you know the motives which prompted him? Do you know the circumstances of environment, heredity, or even chance, which influenced his mind and led him to commit the act with which you reproach him? You, the implacable men that hurl your anathema against the accused whom public force has brought before your bar, have you ever asked yourselves whether, if placed in the same circumstances and surroundings under which this man acted, you would not have done worse? If, even, you were the impeccable, austere, and stainless men you are supposed to be, you, who with a word pitilessly cut off human life and liberty, you would not dare to utter your decisions if you had thoroughly reflected on human frailty; were you conscious of what you are doing, you would recoil appalled before your task! How could you help being troubled with nightmares! How could your dreams help being peopled with specters of the victims which your pretended justice creates every day! Were it not for that official unconsciousness which stupidity and habit give, you would end by succumbing to the weight of remorse and the haunting of phantoms evoked by your judgments. Our epoch of criticism and positive science no longer admits the principle of distributive justice, nor recognizes the legitimacy of a superior authority rewarding the good and chastising the wicked. Against this ancient doctrine, which the conceptions of the age during one period of humanity’s evolution rendered logical, we promulgate the opposite idea. We no longer see actions as good or bad, except as they are agreeable or disagreeable to us, and in consequence act accordingly. We approve or become enthusiastic, defend or attack, according to the benefit or injury received by our interests, our passions, and our conceptions of the ideal. The common need of solidarity which leads people subjected to the same attacks to unite for their defense is to us the guarantee of a future social order less troubled than our own. We do not judge, but work and struggle; and we believe that universal harmony will result from the free action of all men, when once the suppression of private property no longer permits a handful of persons to enslave their fellows. Hence we cannot admit that, six weeks or six years after an act has been committed, a group of persons supported by armed force should assemble to judge, in the name of some entity or other, and reward or punish the author of the act. That is hypocrisy and cowardice. You reproach a man with having killed, and to teach him that he was wrong you have him killed by the executioner, society’s hired assassin! The executioner and you have not even the excuse of having risked your own necks, since you proceed under cover of an armed force which protects you. We are at war with the ruling caste: recognize, gentlemen of the magistracy, that you are its retainers, and let us alone with your big words and fine phrases. Maintain the privileges whose care is confided to you, use the force which ignorance concedes to you, but leave justice in peace; she has nothing to do with you!

That you might be able to judge appreciatively of the ignominy of your role in beating down others, we would like, O judges, that it might happen to you that, being innocent, you should fall into the clutches of your fellows, to be judged in your turn. In such a situation you might learn what anguish and terror they have had to pass through who have filed before your bar, and whom you have tortured, you, magistrates, as the cat tortures the mouse. With the floods of eloquence from the prosecuting attorney pleading against you rolling about your ears, you might see passing before your eyes the specters of those unfortunates that, during your career, you have immolated upon the altar of social vengeance; you might ask yourselves then, with terror, if they also were not innocent. O yes, we would heartily wish that there might be one among you falsely accused, who should go through the terrors of those that come before your bar. For if, his innocence being one day admitted, he were reinstated in his functions, it is strongly to be presumed that he would reenter his place in the tribunal only to tear his robe and apologize for his criminal life as magistrate, judging haphazard and trafficking in human lives.

===Chinese===
但更令我们愤慨的是，竟有人狂妄到自封为他人的裁判官。当权威尚倚傍其神圣来源、当正义尚被视作神的流溢之时，我们尚能理解：那些身任权威之人自以为是特殊的生灵，蒙神意分得了一份全能与无谬，并自命配向庸众凡人分发赏与罚。可是在我们这个科学与自由批评的世纪，在人人公认所有人都由同一块面团揉成、同受同样的情欲、任性、谬误支配的今天，在一位垂死的神明不再以气息吹活凡人那永远易谬的理性的今天——我们要问：世上怎么还会有愚昧到那种地步、或狂妄到那种地步的人，竟敢冷着血、蓄着意，承担起剥夺他人生命、或剥夺其自由之任何一部分的可怖责任？在日常生活最寻常的事务里，我们多半连促动近在身旁之人行动的原因都分析不清，甚至常常连自己行为的真实动机也分析不清；这时候，一个人怎么还能自负到相信自己有能力从一桩案子里理出真相——这案子，他既不知其发端，又不识其当事人，更不明促使当事人行动的种种动机，而且这案子还是被各色参与其事者的夸大、评点与歪曲——更常见的，是被只从旁人转述中听说此事者的层层歪曲——涂抹一番之后，才被搬到法庭上来的？
