你们这些摆出严厉而无谬架子的裁判官，来审这个杀了人或劫了财的人——你们可知道促使他动手的动机？你们可知道那些影响了他的头脑、引他犯下你们此刻正责难于他的行为的种种境况——环境的境况、遗传（heredity）的境况、乃至偶然的境况？你们这些毫不容情的人，对着被公共武力押到你们法官席前的被告掷出你们的诅咒，可曾扪心自问：倘若置于此人作案时所处的同样境遇与环境之中，你们未必不会干得更坏？退一步说，即便你们真是人们设想的那种完美无瑕、严峻冷峻、清白无垢的人——你们，这些凭一句话就无情斩断人的生命与自由的人——倘若你们彻底思量过人性的脆弱，也绝不敢宣出你们的裁决；倘若你们意识到自己干的是什么，你们定会惊骇得在这份职守面前却步！你们怎么可能不被噩梦纠缠！你们的梦乡怎么可能不挤满冤魂——你们那冒牌的正义每天都在制造新的冤魂！若不是愚蠢与习惯养出的那种官式浑噩，你们终究会被悔恨的重压、被你们一声声判决召唤来的鬼影的纠缠所压垮。我们这个批评与实证科学的时代，已不再承认分配正义（distributive justice）的原则，也不再承认某种更高权威奖善惩恶的合法性。针对这条古老的学说——在人类演化的某一时期，时代的种种观念曾使它显得合乎逻辑——我们宣示相反的观念。我们不再把行为看作善的或恶的，而只看它们于我们是可喜还是可厌，并据此行事。我们赞许或为之热烈喝彩，捍卫或攻击，全看我们的利益、我们的情欲、我们的理想观念所受的是益还是损。共同的团结（solidarity）需要，促使遭受同样侵害的人们联合起来自卫——在我们看来，这正是一种比我们的社会较少纷扰的未来社会秩序的保证。我们不下裁判，我们劳动、斗争；我们相信，一旦废除私有制（private property），一小撮人再不能奴役其同类，普遍和谐（universal harmony）便会由一切人的自由行动中产生。因此我们不能承认：一桩行为实施了六周或六年之后，一伙有武装力量撑腰的人应当集合起来，以这个或那个实体的名义对它作出裁判，奖赏或惩罚行为人。这是伪善，是怯懦。你们责难一个人杀了人，而为了教他明白他错了，你们却叫刽子手——社会雇来的刺客——把他杀掉！刽子手和你们甚至没有押上自家脖颈作借口，因为你们是在一支保护你们的武装力量掩护下行事的。我们正同统治等级交战：司法当局的先生们，承认吧，你们是它的仆从；收起你们的大话与漂亮辞句，别来纠缠我们。守住那托付给你们照管的特权，动用无知让给你们的强力，但请让正义安宁；她与你们毫无干系！

为了教你们能够真切掂量你们在打压他人时所扮角色的奇耻，哦，裁判官们，我们但愿你们遭逢这样的事：身为无辜，却落入同僚的魔爪，轮到你们受审。处在这种境地，你们或许才能体会：那些曾在你们席前鱼贯而过的人，那些被你们——你们这些司法官——像猫折腾老鼠那样折腾过的人，经历了怎样的痛苦与恐怖。当控诉你们的检察官那滔滔雄辩在你们耳际翻滚时，你们或许会看见那些不幸者的鬼影从眼前掠过——在你们的生涯里，你们把他们宰献在社会复仇的祭坛上；那时，你们或许会心怀恐怖地自问：他们是否也是无辜的。哦，是的，我们由衷希望你们中间有一个蒙冤受诬的人，去亲历站上你们席前的人所经历的种种恐怖。因为，倘若他的无辜终有一日得到承认，他得以官复原职，那么极有可能，他重登法庭席位，将只是为了撕碎自己的法袍，只为向他身为司法官的那段罪恶生涯——凭臆断下判、以人命做交易的生涯——谢罪。

## IX The Right to Punish and the Savants / IX 惩罚之权与学者

===Original===
The Right to Punish and the Savants

Science, today, admits without dispute that man is the sport of a multitude of forces to whose play he is subjected, and that free will does not exist. Environment, heredity, education, climatic and atmospheric influences, act upon man in turn, now clashing with each other, now combining, but exercising an undeniable influence upon his brain, and whirling him about under their impact as the teetotum spins under the gyratory motion of the fingers of the player who sets it agoing. According to his heredity, his education and environment in which he lives, the individual will be more or less docile to the stimulus of certain forces, more or less refractory to certain others; but it is none the less sure that his personality is but the product of these forces. Having stated these facts, a number of savants, whose acknowledged chief is C. Lombroso, tried to establish a criminal type. They applied themselves to a search for anomalies that should characterize this type, which they claim to have discovered; and after having wrangled a good deal over the aforesaid type, created by themselves, they decide for energetic repression, life imprisonment, etc.﻿—Man acts under the influence of causes external to himself; hence he is not responsible for his acts. The savants recognize this, and therefore decide for﻿—repression!

===Chinese===
惩罚之权与学者

今日的科学已毫无争议地承认：人是无数力量摆布之下的玩物，受着这些力量嬉戏的支配，而自由意志（free will）并不存在。环境、遗传（heredity）、教育、气候与大气的影响轮番作用于人，时而相互冲突，时而彼此结合，却都对人的头脑施加着无可否认的影响，在它们的撞击之下把人旋得团团打转，恰如陀螺在那把它发动起来的玩者手指的回旋拨弄之下不住旋转。依其遗传、所受的教育与所处的环境，个人对某些力量的刺激或较驯顺，对另一些力量或较顽抗；但有一点仍确定无疑：他的人格不过是这些力量的产物。在陈述了这些事实之后，一批学者——其公认的领袖是龙勃罗梭（C. Lombroso）——便试图确立一种犯罪类型（criminal type）。他们埋头寻找足以刻画这一类型的种种异常特征，并声称已经找到；而在围绕这个由他们亲手造出来的上述类型大吵了一阵之后，他们作出的决定却是：严厉惩治、终身监禁，等等。——人的行为受着自身之外种种原因的影响；因此他对自己的行为并不负责。学者们承认这一点，于是便决定——惩治！

===Original===
Hereafter we shall have occasion to explain this contradiction. For the present let us examine the principal anomalies designated by the criminologists as the characteristic of criminality:

- 
					

Old wounds;

- 
					

Anomalies of the skin;

- 
					

Anomalies of the ears and nose;

- 
					

Tattooing.

===Chinese===
此后我们将有机会来解释这一矛盾。眼下且让我们检视一下犯罪学家（criminologists）指定为犯罪性之特征的几种主要异常：

- 旧伤；

- 皮肤的异常；

- 耳朵与鼻子的异常；

- 文身。

===Original===
There are many others which seem to us to have no more relation to a person’s mentality than the foregoing, but our ignorance of anatomy does not permit us to discuss them thoroughly. Let us rest content with those we have just enumerated. Wounds:﻿—It is quite evident that a person who bears the marks of old wounds may be something else than a regular criminal, especially if he received those wounds in an accident, while at work, or in risking his life to save one of his fellows. Until now we had believed that criminality consisted rather in giving blows than receiving them; it appears that the contrary is the case for science﻿—that it is he who gets wounded! Brothers, let us bow! As to anomalies of the nose and ears, we have sought in vain for what relation they could have to the brain; we have not found it. But there is better to follow. Lombroso concedes that many cases which he instances as anomalies are frequently found among those whom he calls honest people. These, then, are anomalies tending to become generalities! Till now we had been inclined to believe that an anomaly was a case of departure from the generality. Lombroso’s science tends to prove the contrary. Sad inconsequence, which proves, more than anything else, that men who have gotten astride a hobbyhorse, shut themselves in one corner of science, finish by losing a proper conception of things in their entirety, and have but one object: to include all things under those particular studies which they have embraced.

To have an ear or a nose badly shaped﻿—the *nose* especially! Nothing can be more disagreeable﻿—above all if this defective conformation is carried to the extreme limit of the ludicrous! There is nothing very gratifying in carrying around a sack of lard on one’s face, or a wine-spot on one side of it; it is often unpleasant enough both to those who look at them and those who have them; however, we should have thought that persons so afflicted were affected painfully enough, without being regarded as criminals besides! But since Lombroso says so, stretching his theory to its furthest consequences, we are led to demand that midwives and accoucheurs be obliged to put to death all the newly born who shall come into the world with a pug-nose or a deformed ear. Every pigmentary spot, evidently, can be naught else but an indication of our black perversity. Thus I, too, (it seems to me I remember having some of these spots﻿—somewhere﻿—I am an Anarchist, which is by some people considered an indication of criminality to begin with)﻿—I﻿—the thing fits! I am destined to be but a common criminal! Death to him, death to him! The theory predicts that I shall die on the scaffold!

===Chinese===
此外还有许多别的异常，在我们看来，它们与一个人的心智的关系并不比上述几项更密切；但我们对解剖学的无知不容许我们对它们详加讨论。且让我们满足于方才列举的这几项吧。旧伤：——很显然，一个身上带着旧伤疤痕的人，未必就是一个惯犯，他完全可能是别的什么人，尤其是当这些伤是在事故之中、在劳动之中、或是在冒死抢救同伴之时留下的，那就更不必说了。直到如今，我们一向相信，犯罪与其说在于挨打，不如说在于打人；可在科学看来，情形似乎恰恰相反——罪犯竟是那个自己挨了伤的人！弟兄们，让我们躬身一拜吧！至于鼻子和耳朵的异常，我们徒劳地寻找过它们与头脑可能有什么关系；我们没有找到。不过更妙的还在后头。龙勃罗梭自己承认，他所举为异常的许多情形，在他所称的正派人中间也屡屡见到。如此说来，这些异常竟正趋于变成通例！直到如今，我们总倾向于认为，所谓异常，就是对通例的偏离。龙勃罗梭的科学却倾向于证明相反的情形。可悲的言行失据！它比什么都更清楚地证明：凡是骑上了木马不肯下来、把自己关进科学一隅的人，到头来便丧失了对事物整体的确当概念，心里只剩一个目标：把一切事物都归到他们所投身的那几门专门研究之下去。

耳朵或鼻子长得不像样——尤其是*鼻子*！再没有比这更叫人败兴的了——尤其是当这种缺陷的构造被推到荒唐可笑的极点的时候！脸上顶着一袋猪油，或者半边脸上带着一块酒渍，实在没有什么可风光的；这对看的人和对长着它们的人，都往往够难堪的了；然而我们本该以为，这样受着折磨的人已经够痛苦了，何至于还要被当作罪犯看待！可是，既然龙勃罗梭这么说，既然他把他的理论推到了最远的结论，我们便只得要求：助产士与产科医生有义务把一切生下来就塌鼻或耳朵畸形的婴儿统统处死。每一块色素斑，显然，都只能是我们内心黑暗邪僻的一个标记。这么说，连我本人也是——（我仿佛记得自己也有几块这样的斑——在什么地方来着——我是个无政府主义者（Anarchist），而这在某些人看来，本就先已被视为犯罪性的一个标志）——我——事情对上号了！我命中注定只能做一个寻常罪犯！处死他，处死他！这理论预言我将死在断头台上！

===Original===
After applying the theory to all amenable thereto, there would probably be but very few survivors; but how perfect would humanity be, morally and physically! We should never recoil before the consequences of a theory founded upon observation as this is!

As to tattooing, we had not up to the present taken it as an indication of very elevated aesthetics. O no. It is a remnant of atavism which leads certain men “to heighten their natural beauty” by means of embellishments pricked into the skin, precisely as our ancestors of the stone age might have done. This same atavism still leads many women to have their ears pierced in order to hang pieces of metal or brilliant pebbles from them, exactly as the Botocudos of Brazil, or certain Australian and African tribes, cut their lips, the cartilage of the nose, or the lobes of the ears, in order to insert wooden or metal rings, which, so at least it seems to them, have the effect of bestowing unequaled beauty upon them. We decidedly look upon such proceedings as a trifle primitive; but we had not seen any character of ferocity in the custom. However, since Lombroso informs us that there is, we certainly hope that we shall get rid not only of those who tattoo themselves, but of those who have their ears pierced and dye their hair!

Lombroso has also tried very hard to discover a type of the political criminal, supporting the theory upon information quite as imaginary;3 but to follow him into this region would carry us too far away from our subject: we shall keep to the criticism of criminalism properly so-called.

===Chinese===
把这条理论应用到一切可以应用的人身上之后，幸存者大概寥寥无几；可是人类在道德上和体格上将会多么完美啊！对于一条像这样建立在观察之上的理论所引出的后果，我们永远不该退缩！

至于文身，我们至今从未把它当作一种很高雅的审美的标志。哦，没有。它是返祖现象（atavism）的残余，驱使某些人借刺入皮肤的装饰来“增益其天然之美”，恰如我们石器时代的祖先可能做过的那样。也正是这种返祖现象，至今还驱使许多妇女扎穿耳朵，好往上头挂金属片或亮晶晶的小石子，恰如巴西的博托库多人（Botocudos）、或澳洲与非洲的某些部落割开嘴唇、鼻软骨或耳垂，好嵌进木环或金属环——至少在他们自己看来，这些环子具有赋予他们无与伦比之美的功效。我们断然认为这类行事未免有点原始；但我们从未在这习俗里看出什么凶残的品性。不过，既然龙勃罗梭告诉我们其中确有凶残，我们自然希望清除掉的就不只是刺文身的人，还有那些扎耳孔的人、染头发的人！

龙勃罗梭还曾十分卖力地试图发现一种政治罪犯的类型，为这条理论张目的论据同样纯属凭空想象；3 不过，跟着他进入这个领域，会把我们带得离本题太远：我们还是限于对名副其实的刑事学（criminalism）本身作批评吧。

===Original===
For that matter, some few more enlightened savants themselves have not been slow to offer criticism upon the by far too fanciful theories of the criminalistic school, and have victoriously demonstrated the lack of consistency in the pretended criminal characters sought to be attributed to those designated by that label. Among others Dr. Manouvrier, in his course on “Criminal Anthropology,” before the Anthropological Society in 1890, ’91, refuted, in an admirable manner, the theories of Lombroso and the criminalistic school concerning the alleged born criminal. After having demonstrated the falsity of the observations upon which the Italian savant and his imitators depended in creating the criminal type, by taking as subjects of observation only individuals already deformed by prison life or by an abnormal existence, Manouvrier declared that persons might have such or such aptitudes as would adapt them to such or such acts, but that they are not, by the conformation of their brain or their skeleton, predestined to accomplish those acts and become what are called criminals. A certain sort of aptitudes might indifferently, according to the circumstances, prompt the person to do an act reputed honorable, as well as one reputed criminal. For instance a powerful muscular organization may, in a moment of fury, make a vigorous man a strangler; but quite as easily it may make one of the officers who arrest the criminal. Violent instincts, contempt of danger, carelessness of death, whether it be give or take, are indifferently the vices of the criminal or the virtues demanded of the soldier. A crafty disposition, inclined to deceit, cunning, and insinuating, may make the swindler who thinks of nothing but schemes for robbery and fraud; but they are also the qualities required to make an admirable detective or examining magistrate.

Drawn on by the truth of his argument the doctor did not, moreover, hesitate to acknowledge that, very often, it is difficult to distinguish the alleged criminal from the alleged honest man; and that many an individual out of prison ought to be in it, and vice versa. And after having, with the other savants, admitted that man is but the sport of circumstances, according to the sum total of which he acts at any given moment; after having denied free will; after having recognized that justice is but a figment, and is, in fact, nothing but revenge exercised by society, which substitutes itself for the individual wronged, the doctor unfortunately, stops short; after having given utterance to perceptions which bring him very nearly in touch with the Anarchists, he thence comes to the conclusion that present penalties are not severe enough and that they must be increased. He entrenches himself, it is true, behind social preservation. Those acts reputed criminal, he says, shake society; society has the right to defend itself, by substituting itself for individual revenge, and smiting those who trouble it with a penalty severe enough to take away from them any desire to continue. Whence comes this flagrant contradiction between perceptions so broad and conclusions so narrow, since the latter demand the maintenance of what is shown by the premises to be absurd? This contradiction, alas, is not to be ascribed to their authors; it is essentially in the nature of human imperfection. Man cannot be universal. The savant who devotes himself passionately to a study attains prodigies of sagacity in that particular groove of science which he has hollowed out. By deduction after deduction he succeeds in solving the most arduous problems coming under that domain, which he has undertaken the task of cultivating; but as he has not been able to keep abreast in the study of all the sciences, of all social phenomena, the result is that he remains behind the progress of the other sciences; therefore, when he seeks to apply the admirable discoveries which he has made to other human conceptions, it follows that he most frequently applies them wrongly and draws an erroneous conclusion from a truth which he has demonstrated. In fact if the anthropologists who have studied man, analyzed him, and reached some comprehension of his true nature, had studied sociology with equal success, passed all the social institutions which govern us through the sieve of reason, no doubt their conclusions would have been different. Since they have admitted that man acts under the impulse of external influences, they should be led to seek what these influences are. In considering the reputed criminal and his acts, the study of the nature of these acts should necessarily force itself upon their minds and make them seek to find out why they are in antagonism to the laws of society. Here it is that the influence of environment, the prejudices of education, comparative ignorance of scientific questions which they have not studied, unknown to themselves combine to dictate to them conclusions so favorable to the existing order of things. These make it impossible for them, though they recognize that order as bad, though they demand some ameliorations in favor of the disinherited, to conceive anything better outside of authority. Accustomed to stir only with the chain around their necks and under the stings of the whip of power, it seems the more independent ones should certainly like to be rid of these themselves, or that a small minority should; but their conceptions cannot allow that humanity is able to go forward without leading-strings, dungeons, and chains.

===Chinese===
话说回来，少数较为开明的学者自己，也并不迟于对刑事学派那些过于异想天开的理论提出批评，并且大获全胜地证明了：硬要加给那些被贴上罪犯标签的人的所谓犯罪性格，是站不住脚的。其中，马努弗里耶（Manouvrier）博士于 1890、1891 年间在人类学学会（Anthropological Society）讲授“犯罪人类学”课程，以令人赞叹的方式驳斥了龙勃罗梭和刑事学派关于所谓天生罪犯的理论。他先证明了那位意大利学者及其模仿者在创立犯罪类型时所依据的观察之虚假——他们取作观察对象的，只是一些早已被监狱生活或反常生存弄走了形的人——随后马努弗里耶指出：人可能具备这样那样的禀性，使之适于这样那样的行为，但凭其头脑或骨骼的构造，他们并非预先注定要去干出那些行为、沦为所谓罪犯的人。某一类禀性依境况之不同，既可以促使人去干一件被目为体面的行为，也同样可以促使人去干一件被目为犯罪的行为。譬如，一副强有力的肌肉组织，在狂怒的一刻可以使一条壮汉变成扼杀者；但它也同样轻易地可以造就出逮捕罪犯的那班警官中的一员。暴烈的禀性、对危险的蔑视、对死亡的毫不在乎——无论是叫人死还是自己去死——既无差别地是罪犯的恶德，也无差别地是对士兵的要求的美德。一种刁猾的天性，倾向于欺诈、狡黠而善钻营，可以造出那个一门心思只想劫掠诈骗的骗子；但这些也正是造就一名出色的警探或预审法官所要求的品质。

这位博士被自己论证的真理带着往前走，于是索性毫不犹豫地承认：所谓罪犯与所谓正派人，往往难以区分；许多身在狱外的人本该蹲进狱里，而有些蹲在狱里的本该在外面。而在与其他学者一同承认了人不过是环境的玩物、随时都依这些环境的总和而行动之后；在否定了自由意志之后；在认识到正义不过是一种虚构、实际上无非是社会取被侵害的个人而代之、代行报复之后——这位博士不幸却到此止步；在道出了这些几乎使他同无政府主义者站到一起的见解之后，他由此得出的结论竟是：现今的刑罚还不够严厉，必须加重。诚然，他躲到了“社会自卫”的背后。他说，那些被目为犯罪的行为动摇着社会；社会有权保卫自己，其方式是取代个人的报复，用一种严厉得足以夺去他们继续为恶之念的刑罚，打击那些搅扰社会的人。见识如此阔大而结论如此狭隘——后者所要求维持的，恰恰是前提已表明为荒谬的东西——这触目的矛盾从何而来？唉，这一矛盾不能归咎于其作者；它本质上植根于人类的不完善之中。人不可能是无所不通的。热忱献身于某一门研究的学者，在他亲手开凿出来的那条科学沟槽之内，能达到敏识的极致。经过一重又一重的演绎，他得以解开他所耕耘的那个领域之内最艰深的难题；但由于他无力在一切科学、一切社会现象的研究上齐头并进，结果便是在其余科学的进展面前落了后；于是，当他试图把自己做出的那些可观的发现应用到别的人类观念上去时，多半就应用错了，从一条他已证明的真理里引出了错误的结论。事实上，倘若那些研究过人、剖析过人、对其真实本性得到了几分理解的学者，也曾以同样的成功研究过社会学，把治理着我们的所有社会制度统统放到理性的筛子上筛过一遍，他们的结论无疑会是另一个样子。他们既已承认人是在外来影响的推动之下行动的，就理应被引着去追问这些影响究竟是什么。在考察所谓的罪犯及其行为时，对这些行为之性质的研究本该自然而然地盘踞他们的头脑，促使他们去查明：这些行为为什么同社会的法律处于对立之中。正是在这里，环境的影响、教育的成见、对他们未曾研究过的那些科学问题的相对无知，在他们自己不知不觉间汇合起来，向他们口授了对现存秩序如此有利的结论。正是这些东西，使他们纵然认识到那个秩序之坏，纵然要求若干有利于被剥夺者（the disinherited）的改良，也无法设想在权威之外还有更好的东西。他们习惯了只有脖子上套着链子、在权力的鞭笞之下才肯动弹；看来，其中较为独立的人自己一定巴不得摆脱这些，或者巴不得让一小撮人摆脱；但他们的观念容不得承认：人类没有学步牵带、没有地牢与锁链也能前进。

===Original===
If we study what crimes are the most antisocial, most common, and against which the code is chiefly directed, we shall soon discover that outside of crimes of passion, which are very rare, and concerning which judges and physicians agree that leniency should be used, attacks upon property furnish the largest contingent of crimes or misdemeanors. Hence arises the question to which only those who have studied society in its nature and effects can reply: “Is property just? Is an organization which creates such a number of crimes defensible?” If this regime involves so many crimes as an inevitable reaction it must be very illogical, it must crush out many interests; and the social compact, far from having been freely and unanimously agreed to, must be distorted by arbitrariness and oppression. This is what we have undertaken to prove in this work; and the fundamental vice of the social organization being recognized, we shall show by the evidence that in order to destroy criminals we must destroy the social conditions which beget them. Let society once be so arranged that every individual shall be assured of the satisfaction of all his needs; that nothing shall fetter his free evolution; that in the social organization there shall be no more institutions of which he may avail himself to enslave his fellows, and you will see crime disappear. If there remain a few isolated natures so corrupted or degenerated through our existing society as to commit crimes for which no other cause than folly can be assigned, such cases will be taken up by science and not by the executioner, the paid assassin of capitalistic and authoritarian society.

You say you make war upon thieves and assassins; but what is a thief, or an assassin? Persons who claim the right to live without being useful, at the expense of society, you will say. But cast a glance over your society and you will discover that it is swarming with thieves, and that, far from punishing them, your laws are made for the express purpose of protecting them. Far from punishing laziness, society holds it up as an ideal, and awards the pleasure of doing nothing to those who can, by no matter what means, succeed in living well without being useful. You punish as a thief the unfortunate who, having no work, risks imprisonment to get hold of a piece of bread to appease his hunger; but you take off your hat and bow to the millionaire monopolist who by the help of his capital has cornered at a bargain those things necessary for the consumption of all, that he may sell them back at an enormous profit! You are eager to present yourselves, very humbly and submissively, in the antechamber of the financier who, by a stroke on the bourse, has ruined hundreds of families to enrich himself from the spoil! You punish the criminal who, to gratify his taste for idleness and debauchery, victimizes somebody; but who inculcated in him this idleness and debauchery, if not your society? You punish him who operates on a small scale, but you support whole armies that you may send them oversea to operate on a large scale against peoples unable to defend themselves. And the exploiters who kill not only a few persons, but exhaust entire generations, crushing them with overwork, cutting down their wages day by day, driving them into a corner with the most sordid poverty﻿—Oh, for such exploiters you reserve your sympathies, and will, if need be, put all the forces of your society at their service. And the law, whose timid guardians you are﻿—when the exploited, tired of suffering, lift up their heads and demand a little more bread, a little more rest, you make that law the humble servant of the privileged against the “untimely” demands of the barefooted mob. You punish the imbecile caught in your nets, but the adventurer strong enough to break through their meshes﻿—him you let go in peace! You imprison the tramp who steals an apple in passing, but you put at the disposal of the proprietor all the machinery of your law, that he may be enabled to rob the poor devil who owes him a few cents on the article which has cost him hundreds in labor, and which represents a part of his very life! Your justice cannot find rigors enough for the thieves in rags, but it protects those who operate upon a class, an entire nation! Have not all your institutions been established to assure to the possessors undisputed possession of what they have taken from the dispossessed?

===Chinese===
倘若研究一下哪些犯罪最危害社会、最常见、法典主要针对的是哪些，我们很快便会发现：撇开十分罕见、且法官与医生一致认为应当从宽的情欲犯罪不谈，侵犯财产的行为构成了罪案与轻罪中最大的一支。由此便产生了这样一个问题，唯有研究过社会的本质与效应的人才能回答它：“私有制（property）是公正的吗？一种制造出如此众多犯罪的组织，还辩护得住吗？”倘若这一制度作为不可避免的反弹竟牵涉如此众多的犯罪，那它必定非常不合逻辑，必定碾碎了许许多多的利益；而社会契约（social compact）也绝非出自自由而一致的同意，必定已被专横与压迫扭曲。这正是我们在本书中着手要证明的；而一旦社会组织的根本弊病得到承认，我们将以事实证明：要消灭罪犯，就必须消灭滋生他们的社会条件。只要把社会安排成这样：每个个人都确保能满足自己的一切需要；没有什么东西束缚他的自由发展；社会组织中不再有任何可供他利用来奴役其同伴的制度——你们就会看到犯罪销声匿迹。倘若那时还剩下几个孤立的本性，被我们现今的社会腐蚀、败坏到除疯狂之外给不出任何别的原因去犯罪的地步，那么，这样的个案将交由科学来接手，而不是交由刽子手——那资本主义与权威主义社会雇下的刺客——来接手。

你们说你们在向窃贼与杀人犯开战；可是，什么是窃贼，什么是杀人犯？你们会说：是那些自命有权不干有用之事而靠社会供养的人。可是，往你们的社会上扫一眼，你们便会发现它遍地都是窃贼，而且你们的法律非但不去惩罚他们，恰恰是专为保护他们而制定的。社会非但不惩罚懒惰，反而把懒惰奉为理想，把无所事事的快乐颁赏给那些无论用什么手段、只要能不干有用之事却活得优哉游哉便算成功的人。你们把那不幸者当窃贼惩办，他因为没有活干，才冒坐牢之险去抓一块面包平一平饥火；可对那位百万富翁垄断者，你们却脱帽躬身——他倚仗自己的资本，贱价囤购了众人消费所必需的一切，好再以惊人的利润卖还给他们！你们争先恐后、又卑又顺地跑到那位金融家的前厅里去递名帖，而他靠着交易所里的一记操作，毁掉了数以百计的家庭，从这份掠获里养肥了自己！你们惩办那为满足懒惰放纵之嗜好而加害于人的罪犯；可是，若不是你们的社会，又是谁把这懒惰与放纵灌输到他身上的？你们惩罚小打小闹地作案的人，却豢养着整军整师的军队，好把他们派到海外，去对无力自卫的各族人民大规模地作案。至于那些剥削者，他们杀死的不是区区几个人，而是耗尽整整几代人，用过度的劳作把他们压垮，一天一天削减他们的工资，用最早贱的贫困把他们逼进死角——噢，对这样的剥削者，你们却留着你们的敬意，必要时还甘愿把你们社会的全部力量交给他们驱使。还有法律——你们做着它战战兢兢的守卫——当被剥削者苦受够了、抬起头来要求多一点面包、多一点歇息的时候，你们便使这法律俯首去做特权者的恭顺仆人，去对付赤脚贱民那些“不合时宜”的要求。你们惩罚落进你们罗网里的低能者，可那强悍得足以挣破网眼的冒险家——你们却太太平平地放他过去！你们把路过时偷了一只苹果的流浪汉关进监狱，却把你们法律的全套机器交到有产者（proprietor）手里任他支配，好让他能够掠夺那可怜虫——后者不过在那件东西上欠了他几个铜子，而那件东西花去了他成百的劳力，代表着他的部分生命！你们的司法对付衣衫褴褛的窃贼，再严酷也不为过；可它保护的，却是那些对整个阶级、对整个民族作案的人！你们的一切制度，难道不都是为了保障占有者们不受争议地占有他们从被剥夺者手里夺来的东西而设立的？

===Original===
But still more revolting to us are all these hypocritical forms employed to make us consider sacred the theatrical buffooneries, with which the bourgeoisie surround their sinister motives, and which they have not the courage to avow frankly. And what is most revolting to us is the attitude of all these mountebanks who, under pretense of attacking the existing regime, attack only the men who apply its texts and the manner in which they apply them, but take good care to respect the essence itself, making believe that there may be a number of methods of applying the law and that among that number there is but one good one; that among the men who climb into power some may be found honest enough, broad enough in their views, men, in short, the like of whom does not exist, who will be able to disentangle this one good method and make use of it to the satisfaction of all. Truly, we know not which to admire most: the knavery of those who utter these stupidities, or the naivete of those who continue to look up to this farce, the entire weight of which they alone support. It is hard to understand that, amongst the countless number of persons who have undergone the examinations of “justice,” not one has yet been found sufficiently free from prejudice to go and lift up the robes of those who had struck him and show the public that all these togs serve but to mask men subject to the same weaknesses and errors as the rest of humanity, not counting the crimes inspired by their class interests.

Hence for us Anarchists, who attack authority, legality is one of those hypocritical forms which we must most energetically assail, in order to tear off the tinsel which serves to hide the recantations and the shames of those who govern us. Too long have these mummeries been respected; too long have the people believed that these institutions emanated from some superior essence which, causing them to float in an ethereal sphere, enabled them to soar above human passions. Too long have people believed in men distinct from their fellows, men of a special mould, charged with distributing here below﻿—“from each according to his necessities, to each according to his needs”﻿—that ideal justice which each regards from his own point of view, according to the condition in which he is placed; justice which these men filled as they are with the most backward and superannuated ideas, have codified in order to defend the exploitation and enslavement of the weak by those who have managed to create and force upon others their own predominance. It is time to break with these absurdities and openly attack these worm-eaten institutions whose aim is to lessen human personality; the free man does not admit this claim of individuals arrogating to themselves the right to judge and condemn other individuals. The idea of justice, such as existing institutions imply, has fallen with that of divinity; the one involved the other. The idea of God’s inspiring magistrates with the verdict to be pronounced caused the infallibility of man’s justice to be accepted, as long as the masses were backward enough to believe in a super-terrestrial existence, in some benevolent being existing outside of the material world, busying itself with what went on on our planet, and regulating the actions of all the people who inhabited it. But the belief in God being destroyed, faith in the supernatural having disappeared, human personality alone remaining with all its defects and passions, this inviolability and supreme character which are the essence of divinity, and with which the magistracy re-invested itself in order to keep itself above society, must likewise disappear, and allow those whose eyes have been opened to see what is really hidden by these﻿—oppression and exploitation of one class by another, fraud and violence elevated into a principle and transformed into “social institutions.”

===Chinese===
但对我们来说，更为可憎的还是所有这些被用来使我们把那一台台戏台滑稽戏奉为神圣的虚伪形式——资产阶级正是用这些滑稽戏，包裹他们那些不敢坦率承认的阴暗动机。而其中最令我们憎恶的，是这一班江湖骗子的态度：他们装作攻击现存制度，攻击的却只是适用法律条文的人以及他们适用条文的方式，而对制度的本质本身，则小心翼翼地敬而远之；他们假装相信法律可以有好些种适用的办法，而这么多办法里头只有一种是好的；又假装相信在爬上权力的人中间，或许找得到几个足够诚实、见识足够阔大的人——总而言之，是些世上再无第二副模子的人物——能够把那唯一的好办法梳理出来，运用得叫人人满意。说真的，我们不知道更该佩服哪一头：是抛出这些蠢话的人的刁滑，还是继续仰望这出闹剧的人的天真——这闹剧的全部分量，可是单单压在他们自己肩上的。难于理解的是，在受过“正义”审问的数不清的人当中，至今竟没有冒出一个成见洗刷得足够干净的人，肯走过去掀起那些打过他的人的长袍，向公众指明：这一身行头遮掩着的，不过是一群同人类其余部分一样身负重弱与谬误的人，更不必说再加上由他们的阶级利益所怂恿的种种罪行。

因此，对我们这些攻击权威的无政府主义者来说，合法性（legality）正是我们必须最猛烈攻击的那些虚伪形式之一，好撕下那层用来遮掩治理我们者的变节与耻辱的假金箔。这些假面戏被敬奉得太久了；人民相信这些制度发源于某种更高的本质、这本质使它们飘浮在以太之境、从而凌驾于人类情欲之上，也相信得太久了。人们相信有超然于侪辈之外的人、有特殊模子铸出来的人，受命在尘世间分发——“取之于人以其所须，予之于人以其所需”——那种理想正义，也相信得太久了；而对这种正义，人人都是依其所处的境况、从自己的观点来看待的。这种正义，这班满脑子尽是最落后、最陈腐观念的人已把它编纂成典，为的是捍卫那些设法造出自身优势并强加于他人的人对弱者的剥削与奴役。是同这些荒唐东西决裂的时候了，是公开攻击这些以贬损人格为宗旨的蛀空了的机构的时候了；自由的人不承认这样一种僭妄：几个个人擅自把审判并惩罚其他个人的权利抓到自己手里。现存制度所蕴含的那种正义观念，已同神性观念一道倒塌；一方牵动着另一方。只要大众还落后得足以相信超尘世的存在，相信在物质世界之外另有某个乐善的神明，它操心着我们这颗星球上发生的一切，调度着栖居其上的所有人的行动，那么，“上帝把应宣之判词启示给司法官”这一观念，便一直使人接受着人的司法之绝无谬误。可是，对上帝的信仰既已摧毁，对超自然的信心既已消失，只剩下了带着全部缺陷与情欲的人类人格，那么，那种作为神性之本质的不可侵犯性与至高无上性——司法当局（the magistracy）曾重新给自己披上它，好凌驾于社会之上——也必当随之消失，好让睁开了眼睛的人看清这些东西底下真正藏着的是什么：一个阶级对另一个阶级的压迫与剥削，被抬升为原则、被改制成“社会制度”的欺诈与暴力。

===Original===
Science has helped us to lift the veil; it has furnished us with the weapons which have assisted to strip the colossus. It is too late for it to be able effectually to turn backward and endeavor to reconstitute in the name of the metaphysical entity, society, what it has wrested from the metaphysical entity, divinity. The savants must manage to eliminate from themselves, completely, the bourgeois education they have received, and study social phenomena with the same strictness and disinterestedness with which they have approached the study of any special science. Then, when they are no longer influenced by considerations or prejudices foreign to science, they will no longer conclude in favor of the condemnation of criminals, but like we, rather, in favor of the destruction of a social state which makes it possible that there should be within its bosom, and because of its own vicious organization, some persons reputed honest, and others reputed criminal.


===Chinese===
科学帮助我们揭起了这重帷幕；它向我们提供了武器，帮助我们把这尊巨像剥了个精光。如今它再想有效地掉头后退、企图以形而上学实体「社会」的名义，重建它已从形而上学实体「神」手里夺走的东西，已经太迟了。学者们必须设法把自己所受的资产阶级教育从身上彻底清除干净，以他们研究任何一门专门科学时那样的严格与无私，去研究社会现象。到那时，当他们不再受那些外在于科学的考量或成见的影响时，他们就将不再作出有利于惩办罪犯的结论，而是像我们一样，作出有利于摧毁这样一种社会状态的结论——正是凭着它自身恶劣的组织，在它的怀抱之内，才既有一些人被目为正派，又有另一些人被目为罪犯。

## X The Influence of Environment / X 环境的影响

===Original===
The Influence of Environment

This is a truth which is beginning to be recognized and is making its way in the scientific world; the modifying influence of environments upon organized beings is no longer combated save by the old fogies of official science. It is acknowledged today that the soil and climate, the obstacles or advantages in the way of living found by the organisms of a continent, have an influence upon their development as great as the other laws by which, exclusively, their adaptation or their tendencies to variability have heretofore been sought to be explained, if not indeed, greater. As to man, who has always been made a separate and distinct being, the new truth was harder to admit; the more so that he, also, is able to transform the environment by which evolved. But at length it was admitted that man, like all other animals, is subject to the same influences and evolved under the pressure of the same original causes. When it became necessary to explain his moral evolution according to the same laws, the task was still more difficult; and even some of those who deny free will, who recognize that man acts only under the pressure of external circumstances﻿—even some of those cannot accept the law in all its consequences﻿—that is to say, so far as to trace the causes of man’s criminality to the entire social organization and to demand the transformation of the latter. The boldest﻿—and they are rare﻿—admit indeed in principle, that the social organization is bad, that it needs reforms, that some of its institutions beget misdemeanors; but to them the grand culprit is still the evil nature of mankind which necessitates a bridle upon their passions, and which society, defective as it is, can alone succeed in repressing. Moreover, in order to minimize the responsibility of society as a whole, they cut up the social environment into several slices, which they likewise baptize with the name of environments, and upon which they saddle the evil effects of the influence produced. As to society, they say, it does, perhaps, leave something to be desired; but such as it is, it protects the weak against the wicked, guarantees individuals in the free exercise of their right to labor, and furnishes them a surer, more effective, and cheaper protection than as if they were forced to defend themselves. In a word, they conclude, society is a contract of mutual insurance established between individuals; if misdemeanors occur, they are much more attributable to the evil nature of man than to the social organization itself.

Certainly we are far from pretending that man is a model of perfection: indeed he is a sorry animal enough, who, when he is not crushing his fellows under his heel, licks the heels of those who crush him; but summing it all up, man does not act exclusively under the influence of bad instincts, and the beautiful sentiments of love, charity, fraternity, devotion, and solidarity, sung and exalted by poets, religionists, and moralists, prove to us that, though he sometimes act under the impulse of evil sentiments, he has a fund of idealism, a yearning after perfection; and it is this yearning which society represses and prevents from developing.

Man is not created unique, either morally or physically. Like other animals, of which he is but a superior specimen, he is the product of a concourse of circumstances, of combination and association of matter. He has struggled to develop himself, and if he has contributed in a large measure to the transformation of the environment wherein he is situated, the latter has in turn influenced the customs he has adopted, his manner of living, thinking, and acting. Under the empire of his character and passions, therefore, he established society, and continues to have a certain amount of influence upon its operations. But it must not be forgotten that he has continued to evolve since the establishment of society, while the latter, after being organized in various groups, has always remained based upon authority and property. Changes of detail have been brought about by revolutions; power and property have changed hands, passed from one caste to another; but society itself has not ceased to be based upon the antagonism of individuals, the competition of their interests; nor has it ceased to press down with all its weight upon the development of their minds. Surrounded by society they are born into the world, within the environment it offers them they acquire their first ideas, and learn a mass of prejudices and lies which they come to recognize as false only after many centuries of criticism and discussion. Hence we are bound to acknowledge that the influence of the social environment upon the individual is immense, that it weighs upon him with all the heft of its institutions, with the collective strength of its members and that acquired by the long duration of its existence, whilst the individual, in reacting upon it, is reduced solely to his unaided strength.

===Chinese===
环境的影响

这是一个正在开始获得承认、并在科学界逐渐打开道路的真理：环境对有机生物的改造性影响，如今除了官方科学的老顽固之外，已再无人对之开战。今日已经公认：土壤与气候，一洲的生物在求生之路上遇到的种种障碍或便利，对它们发展的影响，即使实际上不比那些法则更大，也至少与之不相上下——迄今人们正是仅仅诉诸那些法则，来解释它们的适应或它们的变异倾向。至于人——他一向被塑造成一个分离而独特的存在——这条新真理就更难获承认，更何况他自身也有能力改造那促成他演化的环境。但人们终究还是承认了：人同其他一切动物一样，受同样影响的支配，在同样一些原始原因的压力下演化。而当必须依照同样的法则去解释他的道德演化时，任务就愈发困难；甚至连一些否认自由意志、承认人只在种种外部境况的压力下方才行动的人——连他们当中的一些人——也不能把这法则接受到其全部后果的地步，也就是说，不能接受到把人之犯罪性的根源追索到整个社会组织、并要求改造后者的地步。最大胆的人——他们寥寥无几——确实在原则上承认社会组织是坏的、需要改良、它的某些制度滋生不法行为；但在他们看来，罪魁祸首仍然是人性之恶：这恶性要求给情欲套上笼头，而能够成功压制它的——纵使社会千疮百孔——也唯有社会。此外，为了尽量减轻社会整体的责任，他们还把社会环境切成若干片，照样给它们冠以“环境”之名，并把所产生的影响的恶果统统驮到这些片上。至于社会，他们说，或许确实尚有可议之处；但就其现状而言，它保护弱者免遭恶人毒手，保障个人自由行使其劳动权，并且为个人提供的保护，比他们被迫自己保卫自己时要更可靠、更有效、也更便宜。一言以蔽之，他们得出结论：社会是个人之间建立起来的一种相互保险契约（contract of mutual insurance）；倘有不法行为发生，那也远当归咎于人性之恶，而非社会组织本身。

诚然，我们绝不想佯称人是完美的典范：他确实是个够可鄙的动物——不把同胞踩在自己脚跟之下的时候，就去舔那踩他之人的脚跟。但把一切总括起来看，人并非只在坏本能的影响下行事，而爱、慈善、博爱、奉献与团结（solidarity）这些美好的情感——它们被诗人、宗教家与道德家们歌颂、抬高——向我们证明：尽管人有时在恶情感的冲动下行事，他却禀有一份理想主义的底蕴、一种对完善的渴求；而社会所压制、所不许其发展的，恰恰就是这种渴求。

人并非被造得独一无二，无论在道德上还是身体上。同其他动物一样——他不过是其中一个较高级的标本——他是种种境遇的汇合、物质的化合与结合的产物。他曾奋力发展自身；如果说他在很大程度上促成了他所处环境的改造，那么环境也反过来影响了他所采纳的风俗，影响了他生活、思考与行动的方式。因此，在自身性格与情欲的支配之下，他建立了社会，并继续对社会运转保有一定的影响。但不应忘记：自社会建立以来，人一直在继续演化；而社会在组织为各种群体之后，却始终以权威（authority）与私有制（property）为基础。革命带来的只是细节的变更；权力与财产屡屡易手，从一个等级转到另一个等级；但社会本身从未停止以个人之间的对立、以他们利益的竞争为基础，也从未停止以其全部重量压在他们心智的发展之上。人被社会包围着降生于世；在社会提供给他们的一切环境里，他们获得最初的观念，学得一大堆成见与谎言——只是经过许多世纪的批评与讨论，他们才终于认出这些东西的虚假。因此我们不能不承认：社会环境对个体的影响是巨大的，它以自身全部制度的分量、以其成员的集体力量、以其存在的悠长岁月所积攒的力量压在个体身上；而个体在对它反作用之时，却只能归缩于自己孤身之力。

===Original===
Society, which is a first essay at solidarity, should have for its object the betterment of individuals, teaching them to practice this solidarity in view of which they have come together, to love each other as brothers, leading them to put all things in common: joys, pleasures, gratifications, pains, sorrows, and sufferings, toil and production. Society has, on the contrary, found nothing better to do than to divide them into a number of castes, which may be resolved into two principle ones: governors and possessors on the one side, the governed and non-possessors on the other. On the side of the first contentment and plethora; on the side of the second misery, privation, and anaemia; the result of which division is to pose these two categories of individuals as enemies, between whom a ferocious war is perpetuated﻿—a war which can end only in the irretrievable enslavement of the second or the complete destruction﻿—so far as concerns class privilege at least﻿—of the first.

But the defective and ill-conceived organization of society into two distinct classes does not stop here in its pernicious effects. Based upon antagonism of interests it opposes individual against individual within each class; it sows warfare among them by its institution of private property which forces people to hoard in order to secure themselves against the morrow those necessaries which society cannot guarantee to them. Private competition is the great actuating force of the present society; whatever be the business, profession, or kind of work to which people devote themselves, they have to fear the competition of those who choose the same department of activity. To increase their incomes, their chances of success, or sometimes simply not to go under themselves, they are forced to speculate in the ruin of their competitors. Even when they league together it is always only to the detriment of those dependent upon their special occupation. Founded upon this struggle between individuals, society makes of every creature the enemy of all others; it provokes war, crime, theft, and all the misdemeanors which are attributed to the evil nature of man, though they are but the consequence of the social order, and which society helps to perpetuate, though under the new moral notions acquired by humanity they would totally disappear.

This struggle between individuals has the effect of leading the possessors to make war upon each other, to divide them and prevent them from seeing their caste interest, which would be to work to ensure their powers of exploitation by avoiding and stalling everything which would open the eyes of the exploited﻿—a war which causes them to commit a multitude of mistakes that contribute largely to their downfall. If the capitalistic classes were truly united among themselves, if their members no longer had private interests and were moved solely by the interests of caste, given the power which the possession of fortune, authority, and all the administrative machinery, coercive and executive, secures to them, given their intellectual development, necessarily superior to that of the workers the nourishment of whose brains they apportion to the nourishment of their bodies, the bourgeoisie might, for an indefinite period, rivet upon the exploited the yoke of poverty and dependence under which it now holds them. Happily the thirst to own, to shine, to parade, and to amass, makes them give themselves up to a warfare among themselves not less cruel than that in which they engage the workers. Eager to possess, they heap error upon error; the workers finally take an account of things, become acquainted with the causes whence flows their misery, and conscious of the subjection in which they are held.

===Chinese===
社会本是团结的一次初试，本应以个体的改善为宗旨，教导人们践行那使他们聚合起来的团结，彼此如兄弟般相爱，引导人们把一切共有：欢乐、愉悦、快意，痛苦、悲伤与苦难，劳作与生产。社会却反其道而行，再也想不出比把人们分成若干等级更好的事，而这些等级可以归结为两大主要等级：一方是治者与占有者，另一方是被治者（the governed）与非占有者。一方是心满意足与过度丰盈；一方是贫困、匮乏与贫血。这种划分的结果，是把这两类人摆成仇敌，他们之间永续着一场凶残的战争——这场战争只能以第二类人遭到无可挽回的奴役、或以第一类人——至少就阶级特权而言——被彻底消灭而告终。

然而，把社会组织成两个截然不同阶级的这一有缺陷而拙劣的组织，其有害后果并不止于此。它建立在利益对立（antagonism of interests）之上，在每个阶级内部使个人与个人互相对立；它以私有制的制度在他们中间播下战乱——私有制迫使人们去囤积，好替自己保下那些社会无法向他们保障的必需品，以备明日之需。私人竞争是现今社会的巨大驱动力；无论人们投身何种生意、职业或工作，他们都不得不惧怕选择同一活动领域者的竞争。为了增加收入、增加成功的把握，有时干脆是为了自己不致沉没，他们不得不在竞争者的倾覆上打投机的主意。即便他们彼此联合起来，那也始终只是为了损害那些依附于他们这一专门行当的人。建立在这种个人间斗争之上的社会，使每一个生灵都成为其余一切生灵的敌人；它挑起战争、犯罪、盗窃，以及一切被归咎于人性之恶的不法行为——其实这些不过是社会秩序的后果——而且社会还在助其长存；尽管按照人类已经获得的新的道德观念，它们本会彻底绝迹。

这场个人之间的斗争，其结果是引得占有者们彼此开战，使他们分裂，看不见自身的等级利益——这利益本应在于：规避并拖住一切会使被剥削者睁眼看清之事，以确保自己的剥削权力——这场战争使他们犯下一大堆错误，而这些错误大大促成了他们的垮台。倘若资本家阶级（capitalistic classes）内部真正团结，倘若其成员不再有私人利益、只受等级利益的驱动，那么，鉴于对财富、权威以及全部强制性与执行性行政机器的占有为他们确保的权力，鉴于他们的智识发展必然高于工人——工人大脑的养分，被他们配给得只与身体的养分相称——资产阶级本可以把他们如今压在被剥削者身上的贫困与依附之轭，无穷期地铆牢。所幸，占有欲、显赫欲、炫耀欲与积攒欲，使他们投入一场内部厮杀，其残酷不亚于他们对工人发动的那场战争。急于占有，他们便错上加错；工人们终于把事情清算明白，认识了他们的贫困从何而来，也意识到了自己所处的被支配境地。

===Original===
But the same war which goes on among the capitalists goes on also among the workers; and while the first compromises the stability of the bourgeois edifice, the second helps to secure its continued existence. Forced to struggle among themselves in order to snatch the vacancies in these dungeons which the capitalists offer them, the workers regard each other as so many enemies while they are led to consider him who exploits them as a benefactor. Starved by the bourgeoisie, who in exchange for their toil give them just enough to keep them from dying of hunger, they are, at the very start, led to treat as an enemy the one who comes into the workshop to compete with them for the place they have had so much trouble to obtain. The scarcity of these vacancies again sharpens the competition, causing them to offer themselves at a lower price than their competitors. So that the anxiety of the daily struggle for daily bread makes them forget that their worst enemies are their masters. For the bourgeoisie, strengthened, it is true, by fortune, intellectual supremacy, and the possession of the governmental forces, are, after all, but a feeble minority in comparison with the multitude of workers; nor would the former be long in surrendering to the more numerous class, had they not found means for dividing the latter and making the same contribute to the defense of their privileges.

All this, therefore, certainly shows us that man is far from being an angel. He has even been a brute in the fullest acceptation of the word; this is true enough also. When men first became organized into societies they based these societies upon their instincts for struggle and mastery; and this explains why society is so badly constructed. Only, society has remained bad. Its authority resting in the hands of a minority, the latter have turned it to their own profit; and the more society has evolved the more this concentration of power in the hands of a few has tended to increase and develop the evil effects of these ill-omened institutions. Man, on the contrary, in proportion as his brain has developed, as facilities for procuring the means of subsistence have increased, has felt evolving within him that sentiment of solidarity which he had already obeyed in founding the first groups. This sentiment of solidarity has become such a necessity that religions have carried it to the extreme of sacrifice, preaching charity and self-renunciation, and therein finding a new element for exploitation. To what dreams of social reorganization, of plans for the happiness of humanity has the longing to live harmoniously with our fellows not given birth! But society was there, stifling with all its weight the good instincts of man, reviving in him his savage primitive egoism, forcing him to consider other people as so many enemies whom he must overthrow in order not to be overthrown himself, accustoming him to look with a dry eye upon those who disappear, ground up in the monstrous gearing of the social mechanism, he being powerless to help them under pain of being caught himself in the same insatiable jaws, which mainly devour the good and the innocent who yield to their humanitarian sentiments, allowing the survival only of the malicious who have learned how to push others into those jaws in order to delay their own fall.

You make a great outcry against the lazy, against thieves and assassins; you berate the “fundamentally evil” side of human nature; and you do not perceive that these vices would most naturally disappear were they not supported and developed by the social organization. How can you expect a man to be a worker when, in the organization which governs us, work is considered degrading, reserved for the Pariahs of society, and since the cupidity of those who exploit him has made it a torture and a slavery? How can you expect to be free from lazy people when the ideal, the goal of attainment for everybody who wants to rise in the world is to succeed in amassing, by no matter what means, money enough to live without doing anything or by making others work? The greater the number of slaves a person manages to exploit, the higher his situation and the more respect he receives; the greater, likewise, the amount of income he gets out of it. You have made society a hierarchy, with the top of the social scale (considered as a reward for merit, intelligence, and industry) reserved precisely for those who have never done anything! Those who by one means or another have succeeded in perching on the summit, eat, drink, and wanton, without the slightest employment for their ten fingers. They offer the spectacle of their idleness and indulgence to the exploited, who, at the bottom of the ladder, sweat, suffer, and produce for them, receiving in exchange just enough to keep from starving to death, without being able to hope to get out of their condition but by some stroke of chance. And you are astonished that people have a tendency to want to live without doing anything! For our own part we are astonished at one thing only: that there are still people stupid enough to work! In the presence of the example furnished him by society, the individual’s ideal cannot be anything else but to succeed in making other people work, in exploiting others in order not to be exploited himself. And when the means of legally exploiting him of his labor fail, other devices are sought. Commerce and finance are also licit methods, accepted by the law, yielding enormous incomes when followed on a big scale, but to which, when one is able to go in only on a small scale, are added certain proceedings which enable one to walk between the borders of the code and even excuse one for stepping on them a little if one can do it without getting caught. Fraud and deceit are the exceedingly useful auxiliaries which enable one to increase his income manifold.

===Chinese===
然而，在资本家之间进行着的那场战争，在工人之间也同样进行；前者动摇资产阶级（bourgeoisie）大厦的稳固，后者却有助于保障大厦的继续存在。工人们为了抢夺资本家提供的那些囚牢里的空缺而被迫彼此相争，于是互视为一个又一个敌人，却被引导着把剥削他们的人奉为恩主。资产阶级拿他们的劳动做交换，给他们的仅够不致饿死，把他们饿得半死；于是从一开始，他们就把进入工场、来争夺那块他们历尽艰辛才谋得的位置的人当作敌人。这些空缺的稀缺又加剧了竞争，促使他们以低于竞争者的价钱出卖自己。就这样，为每日面包而日日挣扎的焦虑，使他们忘记了最坏的敌人正是自己的主人。因为资产阶级尽管确实有财富、智识霸权与政府武力的加持，比起工人大众终究只是一个微弱的少数；倘若不是找到了分裂工人大众、并使其反过来捍卫自身特权的手段，他们不会多久就得向这个人数更多的阶级俯首投降。

这一切当然向我们表明：人远非天使。他甚至曾是十足字面意义上的畜生，这也够真实。人类最初组织成社会的时候，把这些社会建立在了斗争与统治的本能之上；这便解释了社会为何构造得如此糟糕。只是，社会一直坏到了今天。它的权威操在少数人手里，后者便把它用于自肥；而社会愈是演化，这种把权力集中于少数人之手的状况，便愈是助长那些不祥制度的恶果。人则相反，随着大脑的发育、随着谋生手段之便利的与日俱增，他感到那在建立最初群体时便已遵循过的团结情感，正在自身之中发育。这团结情感已成为这样一种必需，以致各宗教把它推到了牺牲的极端，宣讲慈善与自我弃绝，并从中找到剥削的一个新要素。与同胞和谐共处的渴望，曾催生出多少社会重组的梦想、多少人类幸福的蓝图啊！然而社会横在那里，以其全部重量扼杀人的善良本能，复活他身上野蛮而原初的利己主义（egoism），迫使他视他人为一个又一个必须推倒、以免自己被推倒的敌人，使他习惯于冷眼看着那些在社会机制这怪异的齿轮组中被碾碎而消失的人——他无力施救，否则自己也会被同一副贪得无厌的巨颚咬住；这巨颚吞噬的主要是那些屈从于人道情感的善者与无辜者，而只让那些学会了把他人推进巨颚、以拖延自己坠落的恶徒存活下来。

你们对懒人大声疾呼地声讨，对窃贼与刺客口诛笔伐；你们痛斥人性“根本为恶”的一面；你们却没有察觉：这些恶行若不是受到社会组织的支撑与培植，本会极其自然地消失。在现今统治我们的这个组织里，劳动被视为卑贱之事，专留给社会的帕利亚（Pariah，贱民）们，剥削者的贪欲又已把劳动变成一种折磨与奴役，在这种情况下，你们怎能指望一个人成为劳动者？当每个想在社会上往上爬的人，其理想与奋斗目标都是不择手段地攒够钱财、从此无所事事或役使他人代劳，你们又怎能指望世上没有懒人？一个人役使的奴隶越多，地位就越高，受到的尊敬就越多，从中攫取的收入也就越大。你们把社会做成了一座等级阶梯，而阶梯的顶端——被当作对功绩、才智与勤勉的酬赏——恰恰留给了那些从未做过任何事情的人！那些凭这种或那种手段成功栖上顶点的人，吃、喝、纵乐，十指分毫不用动。他们把自己的游惰与纵乐展示给被剥削者看：后者在阶梯的底端为他们流汗、受苦、生产，换得的仅够不至饿死，除了撞上某种侥幸，别想脱离自身的处境。而你们竟然惊讶人们倾向于无所事事地活着！就我们而言，只对一件事惊讶：居然还有人愚蠢到去劳动！在社会给他做出的榜样面前，个人的理想便只能是设法役使他人劳动，剥削别人以免被别人剥削。而当合法剥削他人劳动的手段失效时，人们便去另寻门路。商业与金融也是合法的手段，为法律所认可；大规模经营时产出巨额收入，而只够小规模经营时，便须辅以某些手法，使人得以行走于法典条文的边界之间，甚至——只要不被抓住——稍稍越线一步也可以得到原谅。欺诈与蒙骗便是那极其有用的辅佐，能使人把收入翻上几番。

===Original===
For those who cannot operate under these conditions another resource is left: the exploitation of human credulity, swindling, and other analogous methods. Lower still there remain brutal robbery and assassination. According to the means at one’s disposal, according to the environment in which one has grown up, one or another of the methods just enumerated is made use of, or they may perhaps be combined in order to escape as long as possible, the severities of the code which is supposed to defend society. Poverty and suffering, this is the lot of the workers; leisure and all sorts of indulgence to those who by force, cunning, or the right of birth, have become their parasites. Here is solidarity for you!

How can you expect people not to tear each other in pieces, when they must ask themselves how they and theirs are to eat on the morrow if their competitor obtain the place in the workshop which they themselves covet? How can you expect solidarity in them when they reflect that the mouthful of bread which they sometimes give to the beggar passing by, may fail them later? How could they think about solidarity when they are forced to struggle every day for the conquest of bread; when there are a multitude of enjoyments which will ever remain a closed paradise to them? It may be, perhaps this necessity for locking elbows in the struggle which has brought them nearer together, little by little transformed this sentiment into the desire to love one’s neighbor; but however that may be, it is to society that we must trace the responsibility for the survival of the war between individuals and the animosities which flow from it. How can you expect that men will not desire what is bad, when they know that the disappearance of such or such a person will allow them to go up another round of the ladder, that the disappearance of such another is a chance in favor of their getting the place they covet, the elimination of a dangerous competitor? How should a man resist the evil instigations of his nature, when he knows to a certainty that what will be an injury to his neighbor must be a benefit to himself? You say that man is evil! We say that he must have strong tendencies to become good or society would get on worse than it does, and crimes and disasters would be of more frequent occurrence.

===Chinese===
对于那些无力在此类条件下经营的人，还剩一条出路：利用人类的轻信进行诈骗，以及其他种种类似的手段。再往下，就只剩赤裸裸的抢劫与谋杀了。视各人手中掌握的手段、视各人成长于其中的环境而定，上述方法中被用上这一种或那一种，或者索性兼施并用，以求尽可能久地躲开那部号称保卫社会的法典的严惩。贫困与苦难，这是工人的份；安逸与种种纵乐，归那些凭强力、狡诈或出生之权变成他们寄生虫的人。瞧，这就是你们的团结！

当人们必须自问——如果竞争者抢得了他们自己也在觊觎的那份工场位置，他们和他们的家人明天吃什么——你们怎能指望人们不彼此撕成碎片？当他们想到，自己有时递给过路乞丐的那一口面包，日后或许正是自己缺少的那一口，你们怎能指望他们心怀团结？当他们被迫天天为夺取面包而斗争，当种种享乐对他们永远是封闭的乐园时，他们又怎么谈得上团结？也许，正是这种斗争中挽臂相依的必要使他们彼此靠近，又渐渐把这情感化成了爱护邻人的愿望；但无论如何，个人之间的战争以及由它流出的种种仇隙之所以得以延续，责任必须追到社会头上。当人们知道，这样或那样一个人一消失，自己就能沿阶梯再爬上一级；知道另一个人的消失，是自己谋得觊觎职位、除掉一个危险竞争者的一线机会，你们怎能指望人不盼望恶事？当一个人确切知道，凡有损于邻人的必有利于自己，他又怎能抗拒得了本性中恶的怂恿？你们说人是恶的！我们说：人必有强烈的向善倾向，否则社会的境况会比现在更糟，罪案与灾祸也会更加频仍。

===Original===
In spite of all the stimulus of evil surroundings, man has been able to develop aspirations towards solidarity, harmony, and justice; and even these good sentiments have been exploited by those who live at his expense. These dreams of happiness, these tendencies towards something better, have given rise to a class of parasites who have speculated upon such aspirations by promising their realization. Still worse, these good sentiments have been punished as subversive of the social order; and in spite of all, the tendency of humanity is to move in the direction of their realization. And you dare to talk about the evil nature of man! The noble sentiments of humanity, its aspirations after liberty and justice have been hunted down and punished, because those who had succeeded in ridding themselves of the narrow and ferocious egoism which helps to perpetuate the present society, having begun to dream of an era of contentment and general harmony, ended by asking themselves how it happens that society, having been constituted for the advantage of all, turns out only to secure the privileges of the few. The unavoidable conclusion was that society is badly organized, that its institutions are vicious, that they must disappear in order to give place to a more equitable and rational organization. But, as those who are in possession do not wish to abandon their privileges, they have prohibited these aspirations as subversive; whence new struggles, new causes for the development of bad instincts.

The pernicious influence of society upon the morals of the individual being discovered, it is easy to suppress the bad instincts and develop the good. Your society based on antagonism of interests having produced the struggle between individuals, procreates the malevolent beast called “civilized man.” Conceive, then, an organization based, on the contrary, upon the strictest solidarity. Make it so that private interests shall no longer be opposed to each other, nor contrary to public interests. Make it so that personal well-being shall flow from the general well-being, or produce it. Make it so that, in order to live and to enjoy, people need not fear the competition of their fellows. Make it so that by associating their energies and aspirations they may find their expectations realized thereby. Make it so that this association shall not be turned to the detriment of neighboring groups.

You are afraid of the lazy! Make work attractive. Instead of riveting it upon a small minority of society to whom it becomes a torture, do away with all your State machinery, your useless offices, and organize your society in such a way that each shall be led, by mere force of circumstances and not by any authority whatever, to cooperate in social production. Make work useful, necessary, and so that it may be a hygienic exercise instead of a torture. From the present organization you reap a harvest of wars, crimes, thefts, fraud, and misery. This is the result of private property and authority; it is the influence of environment making itself felt. If you would have a society in which reign confidence, solidarity, and well-being for all, base it upon liberty, reciprocity, and equality.


===Chinese===
尽管有恶劣环境的种种刺激，人终究发展出了对团结、和谐与正义的向往；而连这些善良的情感，也被那些靠他供养的人拿来剥削利用。这些幸福的梦、这些朝着更好的东西趋进的倾向，催生出一个寄生虫阶级，靠许诺实现这些向往而投机渔利。更糟的是，这些善良情感竟被当作危害社会秩序之物而遭受惩罚；而尽管如此，人类的总趋势仍是朝着实现它们的方向前进。你们竟还敢侈谈人性之恶！人类的高尚情感、人类对自由与正义的向往之所以遭到追缉与惩罚，是因为：那些成功摆脱了助使现今社会长存的、狭窄而凶残的利己主义的人们，一经开始梦想一个普遍满足与普遍和谐（general harmony）的时代，最终便不免自问：社会既然是为了所有人的利益而组成，怎么到头来只保障少数人的特权？不可避免的结论是：社会组织得很坏，它的制度是恶劣的，它们必须消失，让位于一个更公平、更合理的组织。但是，占有者们既不愿放弃自己的特权，便把这些向往当作危害秩序之物加以禁止；于是又有新的斗争，又有滋生恶本能的新缘由。

社会对个体道德的有害影响既已发现，抑制恶本能、发扬善本能便容易了。你们这个建立在利益对立之上的社会，既已造成个人之间的斗争，便孕育出那头名为“文明人”的恶兽。那么，请设想一种恰恰相反、建立在最严格的团结之上的组织吧。做到让私人利益不再彼此对立，也不与公共利益相悖；做到让个人的福祉（well-being）从普遍的福祉中流出，或者反过来促成普遍的福祉；做到让人们为了生存与享受，无须惧怕同伴的竞争；做到让人们通过联合各自的精力与向往，从中看到自己的期望得以实现；做到让这种联合不被挪用来损害毗邻的群体。

你们害怕懒人！那就把劳动变得吸引人吧。与其把劳动铆死在社会的一小撮人身上——对这些人它已成折磨——不如废掉你们全部的国家机器、那些无用的官职，把你们的社会组织成这样：每个人都仅仅由于情势之力、而非任何一种权威的驱使，就去协作参加社会生产。使劳动有用、必要，使它成为一种有益卫生的操练而非一场折磨。从现今的组织中，你们收获的是一茬又一茬的战争、犯罪、盗窃、欺诈与苦难。这便是私有制与权威的结果；这便是环境影响的显现。你们若想要一个信任、团结与人人的福祉当道的社会，就把它建立在自由、互惠（reciprocity）与平等之上吧。

## XI “The Country” / XI “祖国”
===Original===
“The Country”

Religion, property, authority, the family, having slowly evolved from human aspirations, became gradually defined; but as they became precise in conception, as their purposes grew clear, they became the nucleus of an evolution which, as it developed, led them to concentrate more within themselves, and gradually transformed them into well-defined castes, each having its attributes and privileges. Of these the military caste was not the last to form, develop, and become preponderant everywhere. For wherever it was compelled to cede the foremost rank to the sacerdotal caste, it yielded merely an honorary precedence. Was it not at bottom the military caste which could, by its cooperation, ensure stability of power in the hands of those who held that power? Did it not furnish the nominal or real chiefs in whom was summed up the omnipotence of caste?

In all this conflict of interests the idea of “the country” held very little place. Group fought against group, tribe against tribe, and, in historic times, city against city; whole peoples, even, sought to enslave other peoples; nations, indeed, commenced to be distinguished; but the notion of a “fatherland” was still very vague and uncertain. We must come down to modern times before we see the idea of “the country” formulated, exact, and setting its authority above that of kings, priests, or warriors, who are no more than servants of this new metaphysical entity, “the country,” priests of the new religion. In France it was in 1789 that the idea of the country, together with that of the law, revealed itself in all its potency. It was an idea congenial to the bourgeoisie to substitute the authority of the nation for that of divine right, to present it to the workers as a synthesis of all rights, and to lead them to defend the new order of things by affording them the belief that they were struggling for the defense of their own rights. (For it is well to observe that the idea of the country, the nation, as it is called, summed up the whole of the people, their rights and institutions, rather than the soil itself. It was only little by little, and under the influence of ulterior causes, that the idea of the country shrunk and shriveled to the narrow sense taught today, of *love of the soil* without concern for those who live upon it or the institutions in operation among them.) But whatever the prevalent idea of the country, the bourgeoisie found it too much to their interest to cultivate that idea not to seek to develop it in men’s minds and make a religion of it, in the shelter of which they could preserve their sturdily contested authority. At all events the defense of the soil was but too good a pretext for maintaining the army necessary to the support of their privileges, and the “collective interest” an invincible argument for compelling the workers to contribute to the defense of said privileges. Happily the spirit of criticism grows and spreads day by day, and man no longer content with words wants to know their meaning. If he does not grasp it at the first attempt, his memory is capable of storing up the facts, deducing consequences and drawing a logical conclusion from them.
===Chinese===
“祖国”

宗教、私有制（property）、权威（authority）、家庭，从人类的种种愿望中缓慢演化而来，逐渐获得确定的形式；但随着它们在观念上日趋精确、目的日趋明朗，它们成了一种演化的核心；这一演化愈是发展，便愈促使它们向自身内部凝聚，并渐渐把它们改造为界限分明的各等级，每一等级各有其属性与特权。其中，军事等级（military caste）并不是最晚形成、发展并进而称雄各地的。因为，无论何处，它即便被迫把首席让与僧侣等级（sacerdotal caste），让出的也仅仅是一种荣誉上的优先。归根结底，能够以自身的协力确保权力稳固地握在掌权者之手中的，不正是军事等级吗？等级的全能所汇聚于其身的那些名义上的或真正的首领，不正是由它提供的吗？

在这一切利益冲突之中，“祖国”（the country）的观念几乎没有什么位置。人群与人群相斗，部落与部落相争，及至有史时代，又是城市与城市交战；甚至有整个民族寻求奴役其他民族的；各个国族（nation）固然已开始相互区分；但“父国”（fatherland）的观念仍然十分模糊而不确定。必须下溯到近代，我们才看到“祖国”观念被明确表述出来，变得确切分明，并把自身的权威凌驾于国王、教士或武士的权威之上——而这些国王、教士和武士，不过是这个新的形而上学实体“祖国”的仆役，是新宗教的祭司。在法国，正是在 1789 年，“祖国”观念连同“法律”观念一道，显露出了它的全部威力。用国族的权威取代君权神授（divine right）的权威，把它当作一切权利的综合呈现给工人，并让工人相信他们是在为保卫自身的权利而斗争，从而引他们去捍卫新的秩序——这对资产阶级（bourgeoisie）来说是一个正中下怀的观念。（因为值得注意：祖国观念，即所谓的国族，所概括的是全体人民、人民的权利与制度，而不是土地本身。只是渐渐地，在种种幕后动因的影响之下，祖国观念才收缩、干瘪成今天所传授的那种狭隘意义——*对土地的爱*，而全不顾及生活在土地上的人，以及通行于他们中间的制度。）但无论通行的祖国观念为何，资产阶级都发现，培植这一观念于他们的利益太大，不可能不力求在人们头脑中发展它、把它变成一种宗教，好在这一宗教的庇护下保全他们那备受顽强抗拒的权威。无论如何，保卫国土对于维持那支支撑其特权所必需的军队而言，实在是个再好不过的借口；而“集体利益”则是逼迫工人为保卫上述特权出力的一条无可辩驳的论据。所幸批评的精神日甚一日地成长、扩散，人不再满足于辞句，而要追问辞句的含义。即便第一次没能领会，他的记忆也能把事实积存起来，从中演绎出种种后果，得出合乎逻辑的结论。
===Original===
What, in reality, does the word “country” represent, beyond the natural affection one has for his family and his neighbors, and the attachment engendered by the habit of living upon one’s native soil? Nothing, less than nothing, to the major portion of those who go off to get their heads broken in wars of whose causes they are ignorant and whose cost they alone pay, as workers and combatants! Successful or disastrous, these wars cannot alter their situation in the least. Conquerors or conquered they are the ever-to-be-exploited, submissive cattle, subject to impress, which the capitalist class is anxious to keep under its thumb.

If we agree to the interpretation given it by those who talk the most about it, “the country” is the soil, the territory belonging to the State of which one is a subject. But States have only arbitrary limits; such limitation most frequently depends upon the issue of battles. Political groups were not always constituted in the same manner as they exist today, and tomorrow, if it pleases those who exploit us to make war, the issue of another battle may cause a portion of the country to pass under the yoke of another nationality. Has it not always been the same throughout the ages? As, in consequence of the wars they have made upon each other, nations have appropriated, then lost again or retaken the provinces which separated their frontiers, it follows that the patriotism of these provinces, tossed first to this side then to that, consisted in fighting sometimes under one flag, sometimes under another, in killing their allies of the day before, in struggling side by side with their enemies of the day after:﻿—first proof of the absurdity of patriotism!

And, moreover, what can be more arbitrary than frontiers? For what reason do men located on this side of a fictitious line belong to a nation more than those on the other side? The arbitrariness of these distinctions is so evident that nowadays the racial spirit is claimed as the justification for parceling peoples into distinct nations. But here again the distinction is of no value and rests upon no serious foundation, for every nation is itself but an amalgamation of races quite different from each other, not to speak of the interminglings and crossings which the relations operating among nations, more and more developed, more and more intimate, bring about every day. According to such a method of calculation, the ancient division of France into provinces was more logical, for it took into account the ethnic differences of the populations. Yet today even this consideration would no longer have any value; for the human race is moving too rapidly towards unification and the absorption of the variations which divide it, to leave any distinctions remaining save those of climate and environment which will have been too profound to be completely modified.
===Chinese===
“祖国”一词，除了人对家庭与邻人的天然情感，除了久居故土所养成的眷恋之外，究竟还代表什么？对那些前去在战争中被打碎脑袋的大多数人——他们不知战争的起因为何，而作为工人和战斗人员，战争的代价又全由他们独自偿付——它什么也不是，比什么也不是还不如！这些战争无论得胜还是惨败，都丝毫改变不了他们的处境。无论做征服者还是被征服者，他们永远是任人剥削、俯首帖耳、听凭强征入伍的牛马，而资本家阶级正急于把他们钳制在自己掌心之下。

倘若我们接受那些把“祖国”谈得最多的人所下的解释，那么“祖国”就是土地，就是一个人作为其臣民所隶属的那个国家的领土。但诸国的疆界纯系任意划定；这种划界多半取决于战事的胜负。政治集团的构成并非一向如今天这般；明天，倘若那些剥削我们的人一时兴起要开战，另一场战役的结局便可能让国土的一部分转到另一个民族的枷锁之下。千秋万世，情形不向来如此吗？既然各个国族因彼此交战而先吞并、后又丢失或夺回那些隔开彼此疆界的省份，那么，这些忽而被抛向这边、忽而被抛向那边的省份，其爱国主义便无非是：时而在这面旗帜下作战，时而在那面旗帜下作战；杀死昨日的盟友，又同后日的敌人并肩苦战：——这便是爱国主义之荒谬的第一个证明！

再者，还有什么比国界更加任意的呢？凭什么道理，身处一条虚构界线这一边的人，就比身处那一边的人更多地属于某个国族？这些区分的任意性太明显了，以致如今竟有人抬出种族精神来为把各民族瓜分为一个个判然有别的国族辩护。但在这里，这种区分同样毫无价值、毫无严肃的根基，因为每个国族本身不过是彼此迥异的诸种种族的混合体，更不必说各国族之间日益发达、日益密切的交往每天都在造成的混杂与交融了。依照这样一种算法，古时法国划分为各省反倒更为合乎逻辑，因为它顾及了各地居民在人种上的差异。然而今天，就连这一层考虑也不再有任何价值了；因为人类正以极快的步伐走向统一、走向对那些分裂着人类的种种差异的吸收，以致除了气候与环境的差异——它们因过于深刻而无法被彻底改变——之外，任何区分都不会再留存。
===Original===
But wherein the inconsistency is still greater, on the part of the major portion of those who go to get themselves killed without having any motive for hatred against those designated to them as their enemies, is that this soil which they thus go forth to defend or to conquer does not and will not belong to them. This soil belongs to a minority of property-owners, who, sheltered from all danger, bask tranquilly in their chimney-corners, while the workers foolishly go out to slay each other, stupidly permitting themselves to take up arms for the purpose of wresting from others the soil which will serve﻿—their masters, as a means to exploit themselves﻿—the workers﻿—still further. We have seen in fact that property does not belong to those who possess it: robbery, pillage, assassination, disguised under the pompous names of conquest, colonization, civilization, patriotism, have been its not least important factors. We shall not, therefore, repeat what we have already said concerning its formation; but if the workers were logical, instead of defending “the country” by fighting﻿—*other workers*, they would begin by getting rid of those who command and exploit them; they would invite all the workers, of whatever nationality, to do the same, and would all unite in production and consumption at their ease. The earth is vast enough to support everybody. It is not lack of room nor the scarcity of provisions that has brought about these bloody wars in which thousands of men have cut each other’s throats for the greater glory and profit of a few; on the contrary, it is these iniquitous wars to which the desires of rulers, the rivalries of the ambitious, the commercial competition of the great capitalists have given birth, which have fenced off the peoples as distinct nations, and which, in the middle ages, brought about those plagues and famines that mowed down those whom the wars had spared.

Just at this point, however, the capitalist, and with him the gullible patriot, interrupt, exclaiming: “But if we no longer had an army the other great powers would come in and make laws for us, massacre us and impose conditions upon us still harder than those we are now subjected to.” Some, even though not believing in patriotism, exclaim: “We are not patriots; certainly property is badly divided, society does need reformation; but admit with us at least that France is in the vanguard of progress. To let it be dismembered would be to permit a step backward, to lose the fruit of past struggles; for, vanquished by a despotic power, what would become of our liberties?”

Most assuredly we have no intention at this time of tracing a line of conduct for Anarchists in case of war. Such conduct must depend upon circumstances, condition of mind, and a multitude of things which it is impossible to foresee; we desire only to treat the question from the standpoint of logic, and logic tells us that wars being enterprises for the profit of our exploiters solely, we can take no part in them.
===Chinese===
但自相矛盾得更厉害的还在这里：那些前去送死的大多数人，对被指认为敌人的那些人并无丝毫仇恨的动机；而他们前去保卫或征服的这片土地，并不属于他们，将来也绝不会属于他们。这片土地属于一小撮有产者：他们远避一切危险，安然自得地偎在自家壁炉边取暖；工人们却愚蠢地出去互相残杀，愚蠢到甘愿受人驱使拿起武器，去从别人手里夺取那块土地——而它将效劳于他们的主子，作为进一步剥削他们这些工人的手段。我们实际上已经看到，私有制并不属于占有它的那些人：抢劫、劫掠、暗杀，顶着征服、殖民、文明、爱国主义这些冠冕堂皇的名号，向来就是它绝非次要的构成因素。因此，关于它的形成，我们不再重复已经说过的话；但倘若工人们合乎逻辑，他们便不会靠厮杀——同*其他工人*厮杀——来保卫“祖国”，而会先动手除掉那些命令他们、剥削他们的人；他们会邀请一切工人，不分民族，都照此办理，然后大家联合起来，从容自主地从事生产与消费。大地足够辽阔，足以养活所有人。造成这些流血战争的，既非空间之不足，亦非给养之匮乏——成千上万的人在这些战争中为了少数人更大的荣耀与利润互相割断喉咙；恰恰相反，正是统治者们的贪欲、野心家们的倾轧、大资本家们的商业竞争，孕育出了这些罪恶的战争；正是这些战争，把各民族圈隔成一个个判然有别的国族；也正是这些战争，在中世纪酿成了那些瘟疫与饥馑，刈倒了战争所宽免的人。

然而恰在此时，资本家——还有跟着他一路的轻信的爱国者——便插进来叫喊道：“可要是我们不再有军队，其他列强就会闯进来替我们立法，屠杀我们，把比我们现在所受的更加苛刻的条件强加到我们头上。”有些人尽管并不信爱国主义，也喊道：“我们不是爱国者；诚然，财产分配得很不公正，社会也确实需要改造；但至少请同我们一起承认，法国是站在进步前列的。任凭它被肢解，就是容许倒退一步，就是丧失以往斗争的成果；因为，一旦被某个专制强权打败，我们的诸般自由将落得什么下场？”

诚然，我们此刻绝无意为无政府主义者（Anarchists）在战争爆发时划定一条行动路线。这样的行动，必须取决于形势、心境，以及无数无从预见的事情；我们只想从逻辑的立场来处理这个问题，而逻辑告诉我们：战争既然是专为我们那些剥削者谋利的事业，我们对之便无从参与。
===Original===
We have seen that no matter whence authority proceeds, he who is subjected to it is always a slave. The history of the proletariat proves to us that national governments are not afraid to shoot down their “subjects” when the latter demand a few liberties. What more, then, could foreign exploiters do? Our enemy is *the master*, no matter to what nationality he belongs! Whatever the excuse with which a declaration of war be decorated or disguised, there can be nothing in it at bottom but a question of bourgeois interest: whether it be disputes on the subject of political precedence, commercial treaties, or the annexation of colonial countries, it is the advantage of the privileged alone﻿—of rulers, merchants, or manufacturers﻿—which is at stake. The republicans of today humbug us nicely when they congratulate us upon the fact that their wars are no longer made in the interest of dynasties, the republic having replaced kings. Caste interest has replaced dynastic interest﻿—that is all; what difference does it make to the worker? Conquerors, or conquered, we shall continue to pay the tax, to die of hunger when out of work; the almshouse or the hospital will continue to be our refuge at old age. And the capitalistic class would like us to interest ourselves in their quarrels! What have we to gain by it?

As to fearing a worse condition, the stoppage of progress in case a nation should disappear, this is failing to take into account what international relations are nowadays, and the general diffusion of ideas. A nation, today, might be divided, parceled out, dismembered, its name taken away, yet you could not succeed, short of utter extermination, in changing its proper foundation, which is diversity of character and temperament, the very nature of the races composing it. And if war were declared, all these liberties, real or pretended, which are claimed as our especial lot, would be speedily suspended, the Socialist propaganda muzzled, authority reinstated in the hands of the military power; and we should no longer have anything for the most thorough absolutism to envy.

War, consequently, can bring no good to the workers; we have no interests engaged in it, nothing to defend but our skins; it is our lookout to defend them still better by not exposing ourselves to get holes put through them, for the greater profit of those who exploit and govern us. The bourgeoisie, on the other hand, have an interest in war; it enables them to preserve the armies which keep the people respectful, and defend their institutions; through it they can succeed in forcing the products of “their industry” on others, opening up new markets with cannon shots. They alone subscribe to the loans which war necessitates, the interest upon which we, the workers, alone pay. Let the capitalists fight themselves, then, if they want to; once more: it is no concern of ours. And, moreover, let us revolt once for all; let us endanger the privileges of the bourgeoisie, and it will not be long till we see those who preach patriotism to *us*, appealing to the armies of their conquerors, be they German, Russian, or of no matter what country. They are like Voltaire, their patron: he did not believe in God, but judged that some religion was necessary to the common people; they have frontiers between their slaves, but for themselves they mock at such when their interests are at stake.

There is no “country” for the man truly worthy of the name; or at least there is but one﻿—that in which he struggles for true right, in which he lives and has his affections; but it may extend over the whole earth! Humanity is not to be chucked into little pigeonholes, wherein each is to shut himself up in his corner, regarding the rest as enemies. To the genuine individual all men are brothers and have equal rights to live and to evolve according to their own wills, upon this earth which is large enough and fruitful enough to nourish all. As to your countries by convention, the workers have no interest in them, and nothing in them to defend, consequently, on whichever side of the frontier they may chance to have been born, they should not, on that account, have any motive for mutual hatred. Instead of going on cutting each other’s throats, as they have done up to the present, they ought to stretch out their hands across the frontiers and unite all their efforts in making war upon their real, their only, enemies: authority and capital.
===Chinese===
我们已经看到，无论权威出自何处，受制于它的人永远是奴隶。无产阶级（proletariat）的历史向我们证明：当“臣民”们要求几项自由时，国族的政府并不害怕把他们枪杀。那么，外国的剥削者还能做出什么更甚于此的事来呢？我们的敌人是*主人*，不管他属于哪个民族！无论宣战书装点或掩饰着怎样的借口，其底里除了资产阶级利益的问题之外，不可能有任何别的东西：无论是围绕政治优先地位的争执、通商条约，还是对殖民地的吞并，利害攸关的都只是特权者——统治者、商人或制造业主——的好处。今日的共和派向我们道贺，说共和国既已取代国王，他们的战争再也不是为王朝的利益而打——这真是把我们哄骗得漂亮。等级利益取代了王朝利益——如此而已；这对工人又有什么分别？无论身为征服者还是被征服者，我们照样要纳税，照样要在失业时饿死；济贫院或医院照样是我们老年的归宿。而资本家阶级竟还想让我们去关心他们的争吵！我们能从中得到什么？

至于担心一个国族消亡、进步便会停顿、境况便会更糟，这是没有把今日的国际关系、把思想的普遍传播考虑在内。今天，一个国族尽可以被分割、被瓜分、被肢解、被夺去名号，然而除非斩尽杀绝，你无法改变它固有的根基——性情与气质的多样，即组成它的各种族的本性。而一旦宣战，所有这些或真或假、被说成是我们独有份额的自由，都会被迅即中止；社会主义宣传会被封住口，权威会被重新交到军人权力手中；到那时，最彻底的专制主义也将没有什么可供我们艳羡的了。

因此，战争不可能给工人带来任何好处；我们在其中没有任何利益，除了自己这身皮囊之外别无可保卫之物；而把皮囊保卫得更好的法子，恰恰是：不去为了那些剥削我们、统治我们的人更大的利润，把皮囊暴露出去挨枪穿洞。资产阶级则不然，他们与战争有利害关系：战争使他们得以保有那些令人民保持恭顺、捍卫其制度的军队；借助战争，他们得以把“他们的工业”的产品强塞给别人，用炮火轰开新的市场。战争所必需的借款，由他们独自认购；而借款的利息，却由我们工人独自偿付。那么，资本家们想打，就让他们自己去打吧；再说一遍：这与我们毫不相干。再者，让我们索性大举造一次反吧；让资产阶级的特权尝尝危险的滋味，无须多久，我们便会看到：那些向*我们*宣讲爱国主义的人，正在向他们征服者的军队呼救——无论是德国的军队、俄国的军队，还是随便哪个国家的军队。他们像他们的庇护神伏尔泰（Voltaire）一样：伏尔泰不信上帝，却断定平民百姓总得有个宗教；他们在自己的奴隶之间筑起国界，可一旦利害攸关，他们自己却讥笑国界这种东西。

对于真正无愧于“人”这个名称的人来说，没有“祖国”；或者至多只有一个——他在其中为真正的权利而斗争、在其中生活并倾注情感的那个“祖国”；但它可以延展到整个大地！人类不该被塞进一个个小鸽笼格里，让每个人把自己关在自己的角落里，视其余的人为仇敌。在真正的个人看来，人人皆是兄弟，都有平等的权利，在这片足够广大、足够丰饶、足以滋养所有人的大地上，依各自的意志生活与演化。至于你们那些凭约定而立的祖国，工人在其中既无利益，也无任何可保卫之物；因此，无论碰巧出生在国界的哪一侧，他们都不该因此抱有互相仇恨的动机。他们与其像迄今所做的那样继续互相割断喉咙，不如越过国界伸出双手，把一切力量联合起来，向他们真正的、也是唯一的敌人——权威与资本——开战。

## XII The Patriotism of the Governing Classes / XII 统治阶级的爱国主义

===Original===
The Patriotism of the Governing Classes

We have shown that “the country” is a sonorous word designed to induce the workers to defend an order of things which oppresses them. We shall see now if the “love of country,” this “holy sentiment,” this “love of the soil which is born in everyone,” is so deeply rooted in those who make the declaration; whether it rises from purely subjective causes, as among the workers, or from purely material causes, from vulgar preoccupations of mercantile interests. It is among the writings published by themselves for their own use that we must search for their innermost conviction. It is edifying.

To hear them when they are addressing the workers there is nothing so sacred as the country; every citizen should be ready to sacrifice his life or his liberty for the defense of the country. In fine, according to them the country represents the highest degree of the general interest; to make sacrifices for it is to sacrifice for one’s own and one’s self. We have only to rummage among their treatises on political economy to convict them of lying; to see that all these high-sounding phrases, these sentiments which they parade, are nothing but bluffs for the benefit of the simpletons who let themselves be duped by the like, masks which they take care to leave in the dressing-room when among their intimates. Here is what one of their political doctors, whose authority is officially recognized, says:
===Chinese===
统治阶级的爱国主义

我们已经指明，“祖国”是一个响亮的字眼，造出来便是为了诱使工人去捍卫一种压迫着他们自身的事物秩序。现在我们倒要看看，这“爱国之情”，这“神圣的感情”，这“人人生而有之的对土地之爱”，在那些把这等话宣之于口的人身上究竟扎得有多深；它究竟是像工人中间那样发乎纯粹主观的原因，还是发乎纯粹物质的原因——发乎对商业利益的庸俗盘算。要搜寻他们内心深处的信念，就必须到他们自己刊行、供自己人使用的那些著述中去寻。那可真是富有教益。

听他们向工人讲话时的口气，世上再没有什么比祖国更神圣的东西了；每个公民都应当随时准备为保卫祖国献出自己的生命或自由。总而言之，照他们说来，祖国体现着普遍利益的最高程度；为祖国牺牲，就是为自己的东西、为自己本人牺牲。我们只消到他们的政治经济学（political economy）论著里翻检一番，便可以坐实他们在撒谎；便可以看清，这一切高响入云的词句、这一切被他们招摇过市的感情，无非是演给那些甘愿受此愚弄的呆子们看的唬人空话，是一副副面具——回到自家人中间时，他们可从不忘记把这些面具留在化妆室里。他们的一位政治郎中——其权威是官方承认的——便如是说：

===Original===
“It is the interest of the governing classes, of the preponderance which they hold and for which they are indebted precisely to a continuation of the state of war, which artificially maintains that state among civilized peoples.”4

Could anything be neater? And our good capitalists, who declaim so loudly against the frightful Anarchists that have the audacity to demonstrate to the workers how their interest is antagonistic to the interests of the bourgeois class, make no mistake among themselves in properly defining this antagonism, in order to find a basis for their governmental system. But here is a still more damaging admission:

“Motives or pretexts are no more lacking under the new regime than they were under the old; but under the one as under the other, the true motive of every war is always the interest of the class or party in possession of the government﻿—an interest which must not be confounded with that of the nation or the mass of consumers in the body politic; for as much as the governing class or party is interested in the continuation of a state of war, so much is the nation governed interested in the maintenance of peace.”5
===Chinese===
“正是统治阶级的利益——亦即他们所把持的那种优势地位的利益，而这优势地位恰恰拜战争状态的延续所赐——在人为地把这种状态维持在文明诸民族中间。”4

还有比这更干净利落的吗？我们的好资本家们，一面高声痛骂那班可怕的、竟敢向工人证明工人利益同资产阶级利益相对立的无政府主义者，一面在自己人中间却把这种利益对立界定得毫厘不爽，好为他们的统治体制找到立足的根据。且看下面这条更要命的自供：

“动因也好，借口也罢，在新制度下同在旧制度下一样从不缺少；但无论在这一制度下还是那一制度下，每场战争真正的动因，永远是把持着政府的那个阶级或政党的利益——这一利益切不可同国族的利益、同政治体（body politic）中广大消费者的利益混为一谈；因为统治阶级或统治政党在多大程度上关心战争状态的延续，被统治的国族也就在多大程度上关心和平的维持。”5

===Original===
As to the advantage which the governing class finds in the continuation of a state of war, the same author goes on to tell us:

“War without implies peace within; that is to say, a period of easy government, during which the opposition is reduced to silence under pain of being accused of complicity with the enemy. And what is more desirable, above all when the opposition is troublesome and its forces nearly balance those of the government? In fact if a war be unsuccessful, it inevitably involves the downfall of the party which undertook it; but if, on the other hand, it be successful, (and it is not undertaken unless some favorable chances are assured) the party which engaged in it and carried it to a satisfactory issue, acquires, for a time, a crushing preponderance. How many motives are there, not to speak of the small profits to which it opens the way, for not letting a favorable opportunity to make war escape!”6

As to the “small profits,” here is an enumeration of them:

“But, up to our own day, it has been the inferior classes, those whose influence counts the least, who have generally furnished the common soldiers. The wealthy classes have escaped by a money sacrifice; and this sacrifice, ordinarily very moderate, has been more than compensated for by the market which the state of war offered to members of the said classes, upon whom the proscription of foreigners and the obligation of passing through the military schools (access to which was, in fact, impossible to the poorer classes) ‘conferred the monopoly of the remunerative offices’ of the military profession. Finally if war be cruel to the conscripts who, according to the forcible popular expression, furnish ‘meat for the cannon,’ the departure of these impressed troops, brought up to farm labor or in the workshop, by diminishing the supply of hands has the effect of increasing wages, and thus palliating the horrors of war to those who escape military service.”7
===Chinese===
至于统治阶级在战争状态的延续中能得到什么好处，同一位作者接着告诉我们：

“对外开战，即意味着对内太平；也就是说，迎来一段治理顺遂的时期，其间反对派被压得鸦雀无声——动辄有被指通敌之虞。还有比这更可心的吗？尤其是在反对派碍手碍脚、其力量几乎可与政府分庭抗礼的时候！事实上，战争一旦失利，发动它的政党便难免随之垮台；可反过来，一旦得胜——而且除非确有几分成算，仗是根本不会打的——那发动战争并把它打到圆满收场的政党，便能取得一时的压倒优势。姑且不说战争为之敞开门路的那些小利，单是动机就有多少，足以叫人不肯放过任何一个有利的机会去开战啊！”6

至于“小利”，这里便有一份清单：

“然而直到今天，供应列兵的一向是下层阶级，是那些影响力最无足轻重的人。富裕阶级则花上一笔钱就脱了身；而这笔通常十分有限的牺牲，早已加倍得到补偿——战争状态为上述阶级的成员提供了市场：由于排斥外国人，又由于必须经过军事学校（穷人家的子弟事实上根本进不去）这道门槛，‘军人职业中报酬优厚的职位’的垄断便落到了他们手里。最后，战争对那些照民众那句有力的说法去充当‘炮灰’的应征者固然残酷；可这些自幼在农活里或工场里长大的被征士兵一开拔，人手供给便随之减少，工资因而上涨，战争的恐怖对那些逃脱兵役的人也就显得缓和了。”7

===Original===
This is categorical. We see that the “sacred love” of the metaphysical entity, “country,” is nothing more than exploitation and “small profits;” but the avowal is complete; it is a triumphant retort to those who would object that “there is a public opinion of which the governing are forced to take account,” that “a war may be just and obtain the assent of the public,” that “it is wrong to declaim against war in general,” that “there may be cases into which rulers are dragged in spite of themselves,” and moreover that “war is a consequence of the existing social state,” that “one may declaim against it or deplore its necessity,” but that “we are compelled to submit to it.”

Let us continue to quote:
