“Nevertheless, whatever be the power of the men who decide peace or war, and the influence of the class from which the political, administrative, and military staff is recruited, they are, as we have just observed, obliged to reckon, in a certain measure, with the much more numerous class whose interests are involved in the various branches of production, to whom war is a nuisance. Experience all the time demonstrates that the resisting force of this pacific element is in nowise proportionate to its mass. The vast majority of the men who compose it are absolutely ignorant, and ‘nothing is easier than to excite their passions or lead them astray as to their interests.’ The enlightened minority is less numerous; and besides, what means would these latter have of getting their opinions to prevail, in the presence of the powerful organization of the centralized State?”8
===Chinese===
这话说得毫不含糊。我们看到，对形而上学实体“祖国”的“神圣的爱”，说穿了无非是剥削和“小利”；而这番自供已是完整无遗；对那些要提出异议的人——他们要说“当政者不得不顾忌舆论”，“战争也可能是正义的、能博得公众认可的”，“笼统地抨击战争是不对的”，“也难免有统治者迫不得已被拖入的战事”，更还要说“战争是现存社会状态的一种后果”，“人们尽可以对它抗议或哀叹其不可避免”，但“我们不得不屈从于它”——对这些人，这番自供便是一记响亮的回击。

让我们继续引用：

“然而，不论决定和战的人权力多大，不论政治、行政、军事干部所从出的那个阶级影响多大，正如我们方才指出的，他们总得在一定程度上顾念那个人数多得多的阶级——其利益牵涉于各个生产部门、视战争为祸害的阶级。经验总是表明，这个主和因素的抵抗力与其规模远不成比例。构成这个因素的绝大多数人处于十足的无知之中，‘再没有比煽起他们的激情、或诱使他们在自身利益上迷失方向更容易的事了。’开明的少数人则数目更少；况且，面对中央集权国家那强大的组织，这班人又有什么手段能让自己的意见占上风呢？”8

===Original===
Thus our capitalists do not hide from themselves the fact that they see nothing in war but a means of continuing their exploitation of the workers; the massacres which they organize serve to rid them of the surplus which encumbers the market. To them armies are created with the sole view of furnishing place and rank to those of their dependents by whom they would otherwise be importuned. To them, finally, these wars which they pompously call “national,” making the hollow, sounding words “country,” “patriotism,” “national honor,” vibrate in the ears of the naive﻿—to them these wars are but pretexts for “small profits.”

War upon “small profits”! War upon all the wars undertaken in the name of the “country” or “civilization”!! For now that patriotism is beginning to decline, this new mockery﻿—“civilization”﻿—is used a great deal in launching the workers on a crusade against inoffensive peoples whom the capitalists would exploit and whose sole offense consists in being behindhand in reaching that degree of development which we have agreed to call present civilization.

Ostensibly it is to punish a band of imaginary marauders and secure our national preponderance that wars like the expedition to Tunis are undertaken, while the real object is to open up a new country to the rotten financial operations of a few dubious schemers; it is to secure a free field to these parasites upon the social revenue that the money wrung from the workers by taxation is expended in armaments; it is to realize “small profits” from the offices created in the conquered countries that these new markets, which enable the capitalists to get rid of their stale products, are opened with cannon shots, that a robust youth is impoverished, that a multitude of young men is sent to perish in an unaccustomed climate or be massacred by people who, after all, are at home, and are only defending what belongs to them.
===Chinese===
可见，我们的资本家们对自己并不隐瞒：在他们看来，战争里没有别的，只有延续他们剥削工人的手段；他们组织的那些屠杀，正好替他们清除拥塞市场的过剩人手。在他们看来，设立军队的唯一目的，是给那些依附于他们、否则便要来缠扰他们的人安排位置与官阶。最后，在他们看来，这些被他们大言不惭地冠以“国族”之名的战争——他们一面让“祖国”“爱国主义”“国族荣誉”这些空洞响亮的字眼在天真的人耳边嗡嗡震响——到头来不过是捞取“小利”的借口。

向“小利”开战！向一切假“祖国”或“文明”之名发动的战争开战！！须知如今爱国主义既已开始衰颓，这新的诓骗——“文明”——便被大量用来驱使工人，去讨伐那些并无冒犯之心的各族人民——资本家想要剥削的正是他们，而他们唯一的罪过，不过是迟迟未能达到我们业已约定称作当今文明的那种发展程度而已。

像远征突尼斯（Tunis）一类的战争，名义上是为了惩办一伙子虚乌有的流寇、保住我们的国族优势，真正的目标却是为几个来路可疑的阴谋家的腐朽金融勾当开辟一片新的疆土；正是为了给这些吮吸社会收入的寄生虫让出一片自由天地，用捐税从工人身上榨出来的钱才被挥霍在军备上；正是为了从被征服国家里新设的官职中捞取“小利”，这些能让资本家出清积压陈货的新市场才被炮火轰开，健壮的青年一代才被弄得贫弱，一大群年轻人才被送往异乡，死于不适应的气候，或被那些毕竟是在自己家里、只是在保卫属于自己之物的人们屠戮！9

===Original===
War upon these “small profits,” these expeditions to Senegal, Tonkin, the Congo, Madagascar, forever being undertaken in the name of “civilization,” which has nothing to do with such expeditions, that are brigandage, pure and simple! We exalt patriotism at home, and shoot or decapitate, as brigands or pirates, those who are guilty of nothing but defending the soil on which they live, or of having revolted against those who have imposed their rulership upon them in order to exploit and enslave them!9

But we shall have to return to this question in a special chapter upon colonization; let us confine ourselves for the moment to the patriotism of the governing classes. Recent events have laid it bare in all its hideous reality. The secrets of our armaments and defenses betrayed, through the complicity of the employees of the bureau of the minister of war; the most disgraceful intrigues, operating with this whirlpool of billions to the detriment of the taxpayers’ pocketbooks and the security of the country! The government, instead of hunting down the guilty, sought to cover them up and throw a veil over the most shameless turpitudes!10 We behold the great manufacturing metallurgists﻿—deputies for the most part, having old military officers at the head of the list﻿—becoming furnishers of arms, cannon, armor-plate ships, powder, and other explosives, to foreign nations, and delivering to them the latest engines of destruction, without concerning themselves that these may one day serve against our army and contribute to the massacre of those of our compatriots whom they, in their capacity of governors, will have sent to the frontier to be pierced by bullets. Is it not the Grand International Swindling Association of Jewish and Christian Bankers which owns our railways, holds the key of our arsenals, and has the monopoly of our supplies? O bourgeois! Talk no more, then, of your patriotism! If you could parcel out your “country” and sell it in shares you would do it speedily!

What did you do in 1871, in the Franco-Prussian war, which terminated for us, as everybody knows, in paying an indemnity of five billion francs? To whose interest was it to pay this indemnity, if not to that of the bourgeoisie alone in order to remain sole master of the power to exploit the “country”? Now, in order to pay this indemnity upon whom did they “draw at sight”? Upon the workers! A loan was made, reimbursement for which was guaranteed by the taxes which had to be levied, and which the workers alone have to pay since they alone work, and since work alone is productive of wealth. Let us pause to admire this sleight-of-hand trick. The bourgeoisie, having to pay the war indemnity, in order to get the Prussians out of power and pocket the taxes themselves, had to borrow the money necessary to pay it; but as this money was not immediately in the pockets of the famishing workers, the capitalists alone subscribed to the loan, thus lending to themselves the money which they needed. But the workers alone will have to toil for ninety-nine years to repay this loan, principal and interest, which never entered their pockets. Behold capitalistic “patriotism” in all its splendor!
===Chinese===
向这些“小利”开战！向这些永远假“文明”之名发动、通往塞内加尔、东京（Tonkin）、刚果、马达加斯加的远征开战——“文明”与这类远征毫无干系，它们是匪行，不折不扣的匪行！我们在国内把爱国主义捧上了天，却把那些其全部罪过不过是保卫自己生息的土地、不过是反抗那些为了剥削他们、奴役他们而把统治强加于他们的人，当作匪徒或海盗枪毙、斩首！

不过，这个问题我们还得留待专论殖民的一章再回头来谈；眼下且限于统治阶级的爱国主义。最近的一些事件已把它那全部狰狞的真相揭了个底朝天。我们的军备与防务机密遭人出卖，靠的是陆军部长（minister of war）官署职员的串通；还有最不体面的种种阴谋，就在这数十亿的漩涡里运作，坑的是纳税人的钱袋，害的是国家的安全！政府不去缉拿罪人，反倒设法为他们遮盖，往最无耻的卑劣行径上罩一重遮羞布！10 我们还眼见那些制造业的大冶金商——大半是议员，名单之首还尽是些老军官——当上了卖给外国枪械、大炮、装甲舰、火药及其他爆炸物的军火供应商，把最新式的毁灭性武器交付给他们，全不放在心上：这些东西有朝一日也许会用来打击我们的军队，会助成对我们同胞的屠杀——而这些同胞，正是他们以治理者的身份送上国界去挨枪弹的。拥有我们的铁路、握着我们兵工厂的钥匙、垄断着我们的军需供应的，难道不正是那个犹太银行家与基督教银行家合办的大国际诈骗协会（the Grand International Swindling Association of Jewish and Christian Bankers）吗？噢，资产阶级！从今往后，休要再谈你们的爱国主义了！倘若你们能把你们的“祖国”切成股份零卖，你们是会毫不迟疑地立刻动手的！

1871 年，在普法战争（Franco-Prussian war）中，你们干了些什么？众所周知，这场战争落到我们头上的结局，是赔款五十亿法郎。支付这笔赔款符合谁的利益？无非是只符合资产阶级一家的利益，好让他们独占剥削“祖国”的权力。那么，为了付这笔赔款，他们“见票即付”地开票向谁支取？向工人支取！他们发了一笔公债，其偿还由必须开征的捐税作保；而这捐税又只有工人不得不缴纳，因为只有工人在干活，也因为唯有劳动才生产财富。且让我们停下来欣赏这一手戏法。资产阶级既须支付战争赔款——为的是叫普鲁士人交出权力、自己把捐税装进腰包——便只得去借支付赔款所必需的钱；可这笔钱一时又不在挨饿的工人口袋里，于是只有资本家认购了这笔公债，等于把他们自己需要的钱借给了他们自己。然而，为了偿还这笔从未进过工人口袋的公债的本息，却唯有工人得苦干上九十九年。瞧，这就是资本主义的“爱国主义”的全副辉煌！

===Original===
After this let anyone deny that “virtue is always rewarded.”
===Chinese===
事至于此，谁要还能否认“德行总有善报”，那就请便吧。

## XIII Militarism / XIII 军国主义

===Original===
Militarism

It is impossible to speak of the fatherland and patriotism without touching on that frightful plague of humanity, militarism. In studying mankind’s origin and the course of its evolution, we noted that the warrior caste was one of the first to be constituted and to impose its authority upon other members of the clan or tribe. A little later this caste was re-divided into chiefs and simple warriors, as a former step in advance had divided the tribe into warriors and non-warriors, all members of the clan having originally been warriors in case of need. We do not know whether humanity followed a regular course of progress; that is to say whether it passed successively through the three stages of hunting, pasturing, and agriculture. That it began by hunting and fishing, the gathering of plants and wild fruits, there is no doubt. As to knowing whether tribes passed from this first stage into the pastoral, then into the agricultural, after the manner one takes his bachelor’s degree in a course in science and letters, we are not so sure. We believe, however, that these different ways of procuring food must have been combined according to the natural resources of the regions inhabited. Thus hunters might continue to live principally by the chase, even after having found the means of cultivating some alimentary plants, before they succeeded in domesticating animals. But be that as it may, the warrior caste continued to remain preponderant and to preserve a large proportion of power even when forced to share it; and this caste has remained the firmest support of those who have succeeded to authority. So long as it remained a closed caste, recruiting itself from within, making war upon its own account, the population suffered greatly from its depredations, the armed man standing on no ceremony about taking from the peasant at his pleasure; but the tithe once paid, and no troops or castle being in his neighborhood, the peasant might hope for a little respite; at all events he was not constrained to give the best years of his life to reinforce the battalions of his exploiters. There came an epoch, however, when the lords began to arm the peasants on their domains during emergencies. Then by means of bounties or by stratagem, they attracted such as it was desired to have enlist in the king’s armies. But it was left to the bourgeoisie to throw the burden of its defense entirely upon its slaves. It is the bourgeoisie which has perfected the system by forcing the workers to devote a certain portion of their youth to the defense of their masters. But since it could not, without some danger, put arms into their hands and say to them: “Protect me in my possessions,” it invented the worship of “the country.” And it is by the help of this lie that it has succeeded in getting the workers to submit ever since to this bloodsucking tax. It is by help of this sophism that for several generations it has been able to take away the strongest and healthiest of their youth, to send them to rot, morally and physically, in the dungeons called barracks, without anyone dreaming of resisting or escaping, without a single voice being raised to inquire by what right people are asked to be transformed for seven, five, or, in the last resort, three years, into automatons, machines for killing and food for cannon.

===Chinese===
军国主义

谈论祖国（fatherland）与爱国主义（patriotism），就不可能不触及人类的这场可怕瘟疫——军国主义（militarism）。在研究人类的起源及其演化历程时，我们曾指出，战士等级（warrior caste）是最早组成、并把自己的权威（authority）强加于氏族或部落其他成员之上的等级之一。稍后，这个等级又再度划分为首领与普通战士，正如更早的一次进步曾把部落划分为战士与非战士一样——最初，凡属氏族成员，需要时人人皆是战士。我们并不知道人类是否走过一条规整的进步历程；也就是说，是否相继经历了狩猎、畜牧与农耕三个阶段。人类始于狩猎捕鱼、采集草木与野果，此点并无疑问；至于各部落是否像人们修完文学与科学的课程、依次取得学士学位那样，由第一阶段进入畜牧阶段、再进入农耕阶段，我们就不那么有把握了。不过我们相信，这几种不同的谋食方式，必定是按照所居地区的自然资源彼此结合着施行的。因此，猎人即便在找到栽培某些食用植物的办法之后、尚未成功驯化动物之前，也可能继续主要以狩猎为生。但无论如何，战士等级始终保持优势地位，即便被迫分权，也仍保有权力的一大部分；而这一等级，历来是那些承袭权威者最坚实的支柱。只要它还是一个封闭的等级，从内部自行补充兵员、为自己的利益而征战，民众便深受其劫掠之苦——披坚执锐之人向农民伸手索取，是全然不讲客气的；但什一税一旦缴清，附近又无军队驻扎或城堡盘踞，农民尚可指望稍得喘息；无论如何，他还不会被逼迫交出一生中最好的年华，去充实剥削者们的营伍。然而到了一个时代，领主们开始在危急时刻武装自己领地上的农民。随后又以赏金或诡计，把那些希望其应征加入国王军队的人吸引过来。但是，把自身防卫的负担整个转嫁到自己奴隶身上的，却非资产阶级（bourgeoisie）莫属。正是资产阶级完善了这套制度，强迫工人把一部分青春奉献于保卫自己的主人。然而它不能毫无风险地把武器塞进工人手中，对他们说：“保护我的财产”，于是它发明了对“祖国”的崇拜。正是借这个谎言之助，它才得以从此叫工人们屈服于这项吸血的赋税。正是借这套诡辩之助，几个世代以来，它才能夺走工人青年中最强壮、最健康者，把他们送到那名为兵营的地牢里，在道德上与身体上双双腐烂，而竟无一人梦想抵抗或逃脱，竟无一个声音起来追问：凭什么权利，要求人们用七年、五年、或至多三年的光阴，把自己改造成自动机器、杀人机器和炮灰？

===Original===
Nevertheless there have been protests. There have always been some; desertion and insubordination were necessarily born with the institution of standing armies, but these were scarcely conscious acts; the deserter, the insubordinate, did not appeal directly to individual rights; their acts were undoubtedly due to feelings of personal repugnance which they hardly took the trouble to analyze. Let us go further: the protests raised in literature against war and militarism were scarcely more than explosions of feeling, and hardly, if at all, supported by logical deductions based upon human nature and individual rights.

The army! The country! But the bourgeoisie and the litterateurs, its censer-bearers, had intoned so many praises in their honor, heaped up so many sophisms and lies in their favor, as to succeed in making them appear embellished with all the qualities with which they had decorated them, and nobody dared to question the existence of the said qualities; it was posited as a fact that the army was the reservoir of all the civic qualities and virtues. Hardly a romance in which we do not find the portrait of “the brave old soldier,” model of loyalty and probity, attached to his old general, whose servant he had been, following his master through all the vicissitudes of the latter’s existence, helping that master to escape the snares spread by invisible enemies, and finally giving his life to save his superior’s. Or again﻿—for a change﻿—saving an orphan, hiding him and bringing him up, making a hero of him, and furnishing him the means of entering into possession of the fortune which the enemies of his family had stolen from him!

===Chinese===
然而，抗议并非没有出现过。抗议从来都有一些；逃兵与抗命必然是随常备军（standing armies）制度一同诞生的，但这些几乎算不上自觉的行动；逃兵者、抗命者并不直接诉诸个人权利；他们的行为无疑出自一种个人的厌恶之情，而这份情感，他们几乎不曾费心去剖析。再进一步说：文学中对战争与军国主义发出的抗议，也几乎不过是情感的迸发，很难说有什么基于人性与个人权利的逻辑推证作支撑。

军队！祖国！可是资产阶级和它的那班文人——捧香炉者（censer-bearers）——为它们唱了那么多赞歌，堆砌了那么多有利于它们的诡辩与谎言，竟当真使它们显得装点着他们加诸其上的种种品质，于是再没有人敢质疑这些所谓品质的存在；军队乃是一切公民品质与美德的蓄水池，这竟被设定为事实。几乎没有一部小说不给我们描绘“勇敢的老兵”的肖像：忠诚与正直的典范，眷恋着他的老将军——他曾做过将军的仆人——追随主人度过其一生的种种沧桑，帮助主人躲开无形敌人布下的圈套，最后献出自己的生命去救主人的性命。再不然——换个花样——救下一个孤儿，把他藏起来抚养成人，把他造就成英雄，还为他提供手段，使他得以收回被家族仇敌从他手里窃走的财产！

===Original===
Anon behold how the poets exalted the brave troopers! Military honor, devotion, fidelity, loyalty, were the least of their virtues. The bourgeoisie had to commit this tremendous blunder of forcing everybody to pass a longer or shorter time under its flags before men could see that, under the brilliant tinsel with which the poets and litterateurs had been pleased to cover their idol, were hidden nothing but infamies and rottenness. The volunteering for a year and twenty-eight days has done more against militarism than all that anybody had previously been able to say against it. As long as the workers were the only ones to sacrifice their youth, to become brutalized in the barracks, as long as the public knew nothing of the army but its stage-setting, the glittering of its brass, the rolling of its drums, the gilt of its stripes, the flapping of the flag in the wind, the clatter of arms, in fine all the apotheosis with which it is surrounded when exhibited to the people, so long did the litterateurs and poets help to swell this apotheosis in their works, to contribute their share of lies to the glorification of the monster. But with the day they were put in a position to study the institution closely; with the day they had to bow to the brutalizing discipline themselves; when they themselves had to endure the rebuffs and coarseness of the fellows with stripes on their sleeves;﻿—with that day respect departed; they commenced to pull off the mask from the infamy; they belittled these “virtues” which their forefathers had been so ready to extol; and the soldier, including the officer, began to make his appearance before the public in his true character﻿—that is to say in the character of an alcoholic brute, an unconscious machine!

Ah! One must have sojourned in that hell to understand all that a man of refined sensibilities can suffer there; one must have worn the uniform to know all the vileness and idiocy it engenders. Once matriculated you are no longer a man, but an automaton bound to obey the nod and beck of him who commands. You have a gun in your hands, but you must submit without flinching to the insolence of every petty officer who vents upon you his ill-humor or the fumes of the alcohol he has drunk. Not a move, not a word, or you may pay for it with your whole life or with many years of your liberty. In addition they will take care to read to you, every Saturday, the penal code, whose refrain “Death, death, death!” will haunt your brain whenever the instincts of rebellion begin buzzing beneath your skull.

===Chinese===
转眼又看诗人们如何颂扬那些勇敢的骑兵！军人的荣誉、奉献、忠贞、信义，还只是他们美德中最不足道的几样。资产阶级必须先犯下这个弥天大错——强迫所有人都到它的旗帜下度过或长或短的一段时光——人们这才能看清：在诗人和文人们乐于给他们偶像披上的那层华丽锡箔底下，藏着的无非是种种丑行与腐朽。一年零二十八天的志愿兵役对军国主义所起的打击作用，超过了此前任何人所能说的一切。只要牺牲青春、在兵营里变得粗野麻木的还只是工人，只要公众对军队的了解仅限于它的舞台布景——铜器的闪光、战鼓的擂动、袖章的金线、军旗在风中的翻卷、兵器的铿锵，总之，仅限于它向人民展示时环绕四周的那整套神化排场——文人与诗人便也一直在作品中为这神化推波助澜，为这头怪物的颂扬添上自己的一份谎言。可是，从他们得以就近考察这一制度的那天起，从他们不得不亲自屈服于这兽化纪律的那天起，从他们不得不亲身忍受那些袖上缀纹的家伙的呵斥与粗野的那天起——就从那天起，敬意荡然无存；人们开始撕下这丑行上的面具；开始贬抑这些被他们的先辈如此乐于称颂的“美德”；而士兵——连同军官——便开始以真实面目出现在公众面前：也就是说，以一个酗酒的畜生、一部没有意识的机器的面目出现！

啊！必须在那座地狱里住过，才能理解一个心思细腻的人在那里可能遭受的一切；必须穿过那身制服，才能知道它孳生出怎样的一切卑污与愚蠢。一旦造册入伍，你就不再是人，而是一部自动机器，只能听凭发号施令者点头招手地摆布。你手里虽有一支枪，却必须毫不退缩地忍受每一个军士的无礼——他把自己恶劣的心绪、把他灌下的酒气，都往你身上发泄。动一下不得，说一句不得，否则你可能要为此赔上整条性命，或者多年的自由。此外，每逢星期六，他们还不忘向你宣读刑法典；那“死，死，死！”的叠句，每当反抗的本能开始在你颅骨底下嗡嗡作响，就要在你的脑际萦绕不去。

===Original===
But what exasperates you most are the thousand and one minutiae of the trade, the meddlings, the annoyances of rule. And for the subaltern who bears a grudge against you, or who, without having a grudge, is simply an unconscious brute, there are numberless opportunities daily to find fault with you, to make you submit to vexations of every sort which his brutishness may find pleasure in inflicting upon you. At roll-call for a poor adjustment of a strap, one button more tarnished than the rest, for the probable neglect to put on braces, etc., you get a blackguarding; the guardhouse, and faultfinding inspections without end! Every seam of your clothes is inspected; you are even made to open your garments to let your underclothes be inspected.11 There is more to follow in the dormitories: a bed out of plumb﻿—a blackguarding! “Beds square as billiard-tables!” is the hideous expression continually dinned into your ears and well known to those who have been through the barracks. Your effects badly arranged on the floor﻿—blackguarding again! But the consummation of the art is to make you wax the soles of the extra pair of boots hung on the wall over the head of your bed, requiring that the heads of the nails shall appear without a spot of wax on them!

And the inspections! No end to these, either. Saturdays the inspection of arms, always with the same observations and epithets of “Dirty soldier”! “Pig”! and similar amenities. For a variation you have examinations as to your cleanliness, when your captain assures himself that your hands and feet are clean! Every month there is something still better﻿—the so-called “hospital inspection;”12 then the pork-butcher of the regiment examines your most private parts! Have delicacy of feeling, and they will make it a laughing stock in the army; your delicacy will soon be crushed under the ignoble paw of your commanders. “The army is the school of equality;” so say the hirelings of the bourgeoisie. Equality in brutalization﻿—yes! But that is not the equality we want.

===Chinese===
但最使你恼火的，是这门行当里千千万万的琐节，是种种的干预、种种规程的烦扰。而对那个记恨你的下级军官——或者那个虽不记恨、却本就是个没头脑的畜生的下级军官——来说，每天有的是数不清的机会来挑你的错，叫你忍受种种刁难，他的兽性正以施加这些刁难为乐。点名时，一条背带没有调整好，一颗纽扣比别的多暗了一点，八成是忘了系吊裤带，等等——你就得挨一顿臭骂，外加关禁闭，还有无休无止吹毛求疵的检查！你衣服的每一条缝都要被查看；甚至还要你解开衣裳，让他们检查你的内衣。11

还有那些检查！同样没有个完。星期六检查枪械，总是那几句话、那几个称呼：“脏兵！”“猪！”以及诸如此类的雅意。换个花样，还有清洁检查，届时你的上尉要亲自确认你的手脚是否干净！每个月还有更妙的——所谓“医院检查”12：届时团里的那位杀猪匠要检查你最私密的部位！你若有细腻的羞恶之情，他们就会让它在军队里沦为笑柄；你的细腻，很快就会被上司们卑劣的爪子碾碎。“军队是平等的学堂”——资产阶级的雇仆们如是说。兽化之中的平等——不错！可那不是我们要的平等。

===Original===
Our inspections continue; every three or six months (I no longer recollect which) there is a kitchen inspection by a commissary of some sort; every year a general inspection by the commander of the division. During the fortnight which precedes this latter there is a cleanup in the barracks; kitchens and premises are cleaned. For a diversion you have one day an inspection by the sergeant of the week,13 next day the company inspection, then regiment inspection, brigade inspection, division inspection, corps inspection14﻿—inspections are endless! At each of these inspections you must arrange your outfit on your bed: first a handkerchief﻿—which is religiously preserved for these occasions﻿—which you spread out delicately on your bed; on this handkerchief you must arrange your brushes, your extra pair of boots, your drawers﻿—which likewise are hardly ever taken out except on those particular days﻿—an undershirt rolled up in a certain way, and of a certain length, your nightcap, your grease-box, your bottle of polish, a needle-case, thread, and scissors. In order that this exposition may be made according to the rules, illustrative placards are posted in the bunk-room, which must be consulted every moment in order to know the exact place for the everlasting brush, the bottle of polish, and all the other equally important objects. For you must be very careful to put every object in its place! If not, you will soon hear a storm of imprecations bursting upon your ears, vomited forth by whichever of your chiefs happens to perceive the irregularity. Know that the death penalty would not be too heavy to expiate such negligence! Horror! Abomination and desolation! A bottle of polish in the place intended for the grease-box! It would be the ruin of France if the general should come to know of it! We have already spoken of the consummation of the art, but here is sublimity attained: they make you wax the feet of the bed!15

It is in those inspections at which a general presides that the servility of the subaltern and even of the superior officers is shown. Front the instant the general is spied, you behold these officers, so arrogant before the poor devil of a private, crawl and cringe, range themselves most humbly behind the general, who on the other hand draws himself up﻿—when he is not broken down with paralysis16﻿—proud as Lucifer! And his eyes! Fulminating lightnings upon the wretch who lays himself open to an observation from the grand chief! The officers are all topsy-turvy; there is a trooper with a needle short, or who, having forgotten that the fortnight ended the night before, has buttoned his overcoat on the left side when he ought to have buttoned it on the right! The colonel stammers with fury, the commander quakes under his tunic, the captain is green with fright; the corporal alone says nothing; he knows that every one of them, commencing with the sergeant, will take satisfaction out of him. His course is clear; he will turn around and revenge himself upon the delinquent.

===Chinese===
我们的检查还在继续：每三个月或六个月（是哪一种我已记不清了），有某位军需官之类来检查厨房；每年有师长主持的总检查。在总检查之前的两个星期里，兵营要进行大扫除；厨房和各处场所都得洗刷一遍。为了调剂，今天有值周军士的检查13，明天有连检查，然后是团检查、旅检查、师检查、军检查14——检查没完没了！每逢这类检查，你必须把全套装具摆到床上：先是一块手帕——它被虔诚地专留待这类场合——你要把它斯斯文文地摊在床上；在这块手帕上，你得摆好你的刷子、你的备用靴、你的衬裤——它同样几乎只在那些特别的日子才被拿出来——一件按规定方式卷起、按规定长度折好的衬衣，你的睡帽、你的油盒、你的鞋油瓶、一个针线盒、线，还有剪刀。为了使这一陈列合乎规程，寝室里张贴着示例挂图，必须时时查对，才能知道那把须臾不离的刷子、那瓶鞋油以及所有其他同样重要的物件的确切位置。因为你必须万分小心，把每件东西都放在它的位置上！否则，哪位上司碰巧发现了这不规整，你马上就会听见一阵咒骂的风暴劈头盖脑砸下来。要知道，这样的疏失，就是拿死刑来抵偿也不为过重！恐怖啊！可憎而凄惨！鞋油瓶放到了本该放油盒的位置上！这事要是让将军知道了，法兰西就要毁于一旦！这门艺术的登峰造极我们前面已经谈过，可这里简直是超凡入圣：他们还要你给床脚打上蜡！15

正是在将军亲临的那些检查上，下级军官乃至高级军官的奴性才暴露无遗。从望见将军的那一刻起，你就看见这些在可怜的小兵面前何等倨傲的军官，一个个匍匐屈膝、卑躬屏息，极其谦卑地排列在将军身后；而将军呢，则昂然挺立——当他没有瘫倒时16——傲如路西法（Lucifer）！还有他的眼睛！对着那个招来大主帅一句训斥的倒霉蛋，霹雳闪电齐发！军官们个个乱作一团：有个骑兵短了一根针；还有一个，忘了双周期前一夜已经结束，把大衣扣到了左边，而本该扣在右边！上校气得说话结结巴巴，少校在军装底下浑身发抖，上尉吓得面如死灰；唯独下士一言不发；他知道他们每一个人，从军士起，都要拿他来出气泄愤。他的路数清清楚楚：转过身去，找那个犯规的小兵算账。

===Original===
Between times, while there is no inspection in view﻿—usually on Saturday afternoon﻿—in order to liven you up a bit, they call for fatigue-duty in the quarter; this consists in making you walk up and down the barrack-yard gathering into heaps the stones and pebbles that may be found therein. After an hour of this agreeable pastime, you go up again to the bunk-room; the little piles of pebbles are scattered by the passers of the week, so you begin again the following Saturday. The military trade has a number of these spiritual little distractions.

And when in the evenings, after days thus spent, you feel a desire to chat with your companions in slavery, their conversation is not of a nature to uplift your morals or inspire you with ennobling thoughts. You perceive a group convulsed with laughter; you approach imagining you will hear something instructive: it is some idiot rehashing smutty jokes, neither new nor wittily told. You turn away and fall in with another group of imbruted creatures, who appear to have no pleasures except in recalling the gluttonies they have been indulging in, or in anticipation of the feast they are going to bury themselves in when the paltry bet they have made, (the amount for which was received from their parents) shall have brought in a few cents. Vulgar gluttony and debauchery! Do not try to go beyond these for they will not understand you! Nothing any longer exists outside of these two pleasures. After this are you astonished that, at the end of three years of this regime, so many men come out of the barracks fit for nothing but policemen and detectives? The army is nothing but a school for demoralization; it can produce nothing but spies, drones, and drunkards. Small indeed is the number of those who are unaffected by those three years of brutalization, and even they retain some traces of it for a long time after they have left the army.

===Chinese===
间隙里，当没有检查在望的时候——通常在星期六下午——为了给你提提神，营区里还要征调杂役（fatigue-duty）；这活计就是让你在营院里走来走去，把院里能找到的石头石子都捡拢成堆。这桩惬意的消遣做了一个钟头之后，你再回到寝室；那些小石子堆又会被值周的兵扒散，于是下个星期六你再从头来过。军事这门行当，这类妙趣横生的小消遣还多着呢。

白天就这样打发过去之后，到了晚上，你想同奴役中的伙伴们聊聊，可他们的谈吐却不是那种能提升你的道德、激发你高尚思想的东西。你看见一群人笑得前仰后合；你凑上前去，以为会听到什么有教益的东西：原来是某个蠢货在翻炒猥亵的笑话，既不新鲜，讲得也不俏皮。你转身走开，又遇上一伙兽化了的生灵，他们似乎除了回味自己纵情大嚼的往事，或盼着那点微不足道的赌注——赌本是从父母那里领来的——赢进几个铜子之后将要埋头鲸吞的那顿酒席之外，再没有别的乐趣。庸俗的饕餮与纵欲！别想超出这两样去谈别的，因为他们听不懂你！在这两种享乐之外，再也不存在任何东西。经过这些，你们还惊讶吗：这样的日子过上三年，这么多人走出兵营，除了当警察和警探（detectives），什么也做不了？军队不过是一所败坏道德的学校；它所能产出的，无非密探、懒汉与酒徒。能不受这三年兽化影响的人确实寥寥无几；就连他们，离开军队之后很久，身上也还留着这段生活的某些痕迹。

===Original===
Oh, this brutal and abject discipline, which breaks a man utterly, crushes his spirit, deforms his character, destroys his will! Horrible machine for brutalization, to which you deliver up a young man who only asks the opportunity to develop his sentiments towards the beautiful and the true, whose energies might be unfolded in the daily struggle for life, whose intellect might expand under the impulse of knowledge already acquired and the necessity of knowing more! Military discipline lays upon him a leaden weight which will cramp him and contract his brain forever, slackening even the rhythm of his heartbeats. After having ground him for three years in the multiple gearings of its hierarchy, it will give you back a shapeless rag, if it have not completely devoured him!

We have seen, O savage bourgeoisie, that this fatherland of which you wish to make us the defenders, is but the organization of your privileges; this militarism, that you teach is a duty to which all should conform, is instituted solely for your defense, all the burden whereof you cast upon those against whom it is directed. It furnishes you, into the bargain, with the chance to bestow rank, honors, and emoluments upon those of your relations incapable of performing more elevated functions, the aforesaid ranks and emoluments serving at the same time to stimulate the unhealthy ambitions of those who abandon the class whence they sprang to become your convict-keepers!

What are your country, your frontiers, your arbitrary boundaries between peoples to us? Your country exploits us, your frontiers stifle us, your nationalities are strangers to us! We are men, citizens of the universe; all men are our brothers; our only enemies are our masters, those who exploit us, who prevent us from evolving freely, developing the plenitude of our forces. We no longer wish to serve you as playthings, to be defenders of your privileges, to have the degrading livery of your militarism, the brutalizing yoke of your discipline thrust upon us. We want to bow our heads no longer; we want to be free.

===Chinese===
啊，这粗暴而卑贱的纪律！它把一个人彻底摧折，碾碎他的精神，扭曲他的性格，毁掉他的意志！可怕的兽化机器啊！你们交给它的是一个青年，他只求有机会发展自己对美与真的情感；他的精力，本可以在日常的生存斗争中舒展开来；他的才智，本可以在已获知识的推动与更求多知的必要之下扩展开去！军事的纪律给他压上一块铅砣，将永远地拘挛他、箍缩他的头脑，甚至连他心跳的节律也要放缓。在它的等级制度的重重齿轮里把他碾磨三年之后，它还给你的，将是一块不成形状的破布——倘若它没有把他整个吞掉的话！

野蛮的资产阶级啊，我们已经看清：这个你们想让我们充当其保卫者的祖国，不过是你们种种特权的一套组织；这个你们教导说人人皆应遵奉为义务的军国主义，建立起来只是为了保卫你们，而它的全部负担，你们却尽数转嫁到它所针对的那些人身上。它还额外为你们提供机会，把军衔、荣誉与俸禄颁给那些无力担任更高职司的亲眷；而上述军衔与俸禄，同时又刺激着另一类人的病态野心——他们背弃了自己出身的阶级，来充当你们看管苦役犯的狱卒！

你们的祖国、你们的国境、你们在各族人民之间任意划定的疆界，于我们算得了什么？你们的祖国剥削我们，你们的国境窒息我们，你们的民族性于我们形同陌路！我们是人，是宇宙的公民；一切人都是我们的兄弟；我们唯一的敌人是我们的主人，是那些剥削我们、阻止我们自由演化、不容我们发展全部力量的人。我们不愿再作为玩物为你们效力，不愿再做你们特权的保卫者，不愿再被强加上你们军国主义那有辱人格的号衣、你们纪律那兽化的枷锁。我们不愿再低下头颅；我们要自由。

===Original===
And you, poor devils destined to fall under the stroke of the military law, and who read in the newspapers the recitals of injustices committed every day in the name of discipline, who have not gone without hearing from time to time the story of other infamies of which those who were silly enough to enlist have been the victims, will you not indulge in some reflections on the life which awaits you in the barracks? And all you who had never, until now, beheld the military life save through the smoke of the incense burned before it by the poets, can you not understand all the knavery of these bourgeois writers who have celebrated in every key the “military virtues,” the “honor of the soldier,” and “warlike dignity?” Go, poor devils, who for the sake of the word “country,” or for fear of the court-martial, are going to waste the best years of your youth in these schools of corruption called “barracks.” Go, and know the destiny that awaits you! If you wish to finish your term of service without accidents, leave behind you with your civil clothes every sentiment of personal dignity; crush out of your heart every feeling of independence; the “virtues” and “military honor” require that you be nothing more than killing machines, passive brutes; for if you have unluckily preserved in your heart, under the livery with which they clothe you, the least grain of pride, it may prove fatal to you. If some drunken veteran is pleased to insult you, and if he have stripes upon his sleeves, take care to hide the jerk which in spite of you will twitch your muscle under the insult; the hand which you have lifted to strike the insulter in the face﻿—carry it with military precision to your cap and salute; if you open your mouth to reply to threat or insult, twist it into saying, “Brigadier, you are right!” And yet a gesture, a word, the slightest sign of emotion might be interpreted as irony, and draw down punishment upon you for want of respect to your superior! Whatever be the insult or outrage, nerve yourself against the anger which will prompt you to resent it; remain calm, insensible, inert﻿—your hand in its place, your heels close together! That’s well! You remain impassive under the injury? You do not flinch? No.﻿—Well and good! You are good soldiers. That is what the country demands of its defenders!

“But if it be impossible for us to remain calm,” you will ask; “if, in spite of us, the blood rise to our heads making us blush?” Then there is but one thing for you: do not set foot in this prison, whence you cannot reissue without being debased, brutalized, corrupted. If you wish to remain men, do not be *soldiers*; if you cannot stand humiliations do not don the uniform. If, however, you have already committed the imprudence of clothing yourselves therewith, and some day you find yourselves in the situation of being unable to control your indignation﻿—*neither insult nor strike your superiors*﻿—﻿ ﻿… ﻿ ﻿… **Let daylight through them!** You will pay no more for it.

===Chinese===
还有你们，这些注定要落到军法之下的可怜人——你们在报纸上读到以纪律之名每天制造的种种不公，也不时听人讲起那些蠢到应征入伍者所遭遇的其他种种丑事——你们就不肯对兵营里等着你们的生活略作一番思索吗？还有你们所有这些人——直到今天，除了诗人们在军旅生活面前焚香所腾起的香烟之外，从未见过它的真容——你们难道还看不透这些资产阶级作家的全部无赖行径吗？他们曾用尽各种调门歌颂“军人的美德”、“士兵的荣誉”与“尚武的尊严”。去吧，可怜的人们——为了“祖国”两个字，或者出于对军事法庭（court-martial）的畏惧——你们就要在这些名为“兵营”的堕落学堂里，耗掉青春最好的年华。去吧，去认识那等着你们的命运！你们若想服满役期而不出事故，那就连同你们的便装一起，把个人尊严的每一分情感都留在营外；把心中每一丝独立之感都碾灭；那些“美德”与“军人荣誉”要求你们只做杀人机器、消极被动的畜生；因为倘若你们不幸在他们给你们套上的号衣底下，心里还存留着最细小的一粒自尊，那对你们便可能是致命的。倘若哪个喝醉的老兵高兴侮辱你，而他袖上又缀着纹章，那就千万留神，掩住那一阵尽管不由你、却会在侮辱之下抽动你肌肉的痉挛；你本来抬起来要扇那侮辱者耳光的手——要以军人的精准举向帽檐，行一个军礼；你若张开嘴要回敬威胁或侮辱，那就把它拧成这样一句：“下士，您说得对！”然而，一个手势、一个字、一丝最细微的情绪迹象，都可能被解作讥讽，给你们招来对上司不敬的惩罚！无论那是怎样的侮辱或暴行，都要硬起心肠，顶住那驱使你们发作的怒火；保持平静、麻木、呆滞——手放在该放的地方，脚跟并拢！好极了！你们在凌辱之下无动于衷？你们不退缩？不。——很好！你们是好士兵。这就是祖国对它的保卫者们的要求！

“可要是我们不可能保持平静呢？”你们会问；“要是我们不由自主，血涌上头、涨红了脸呢？”那么你们只剩一条路：不要踏进这座监狱——从这座监狱里再出去的人，没有不遭贬损、不被兽化、不受腐蚀的。你们若想继续做人，就不要当*士兵*；你们若受不了羞辱，就不要穿上那身制服。然而，倘若你们已经轻率地把它穿上了身，而有朝一日又发觉自己实在按捺不住心头的义愤——*既不要辱骂、也不要殴打你们的上级*——…………**往他们身上开几个透光的窟窿吧！**反正你们为此付出的代价，也不会更多。

## XIV Colonization / XIV 殖民
===Original===
Colonization

Colonization is extending too widely, in the present epoch, for us to neglect to treat separately of this hybrid product of patriotism and mercantilism combined﻿—brigandage and highway robbery for the benefit of the ruling classes! A private individual goes into his neighbor’s house, breaks everything he lays his hands on, seizes everything he finds convenient for his own use: he is a criminal; society condemns him. But if a government find itself driven to a standstill by an internal situation which necessitates some external “diversion;” if it be encumbered at home by unemployed hands of which it knows not how to rid itself, of products which it cannot get distributed; let this government declare war against remote peoples which it knows to be too feeble to resist it, let it take possession of their country, subject them to an entire system of exploitation, force its products upon them, massacre them if they attempt to escape this exploitation with which it weighs them down﻿—oh, then, this is moral! From the moment you operate on a grand scale it merits the approbation of honest men. It is no longer called robbery or assassination; there is an honorable word for covering up the dishonorable deeds that government commits: this is called “civilizing” undeveloped peoples.

And let no one deem this exaggeration. No nation is reputed to be a colonizing one save when it has succeeded in getting out of a country the maximum product it is capable of yielding. Thus England is a colonizing country, because she knows how to “reward” her colonies with the prosperity of the people she sends out to rule them, how to gather back into her coffers the taxes with which she burdens them. In the Indies, for instance, those whom she sends out make colossal fortunes. The country, to be sure, is completely ravaged from time to time by frightful famines that decimate hundreds of thousands of people. But of what moment are the details so long as John Bull can market his manufactured products and thereby succeed in obtaining, for his own advantage, what the soil of Great Britain could not produce? Such are the benefits of colonization!

In France it is different; we are not colonizers. Oh, reassure yourself; that is not to say we are any the less brigands, that our conquered people are less exploited! No; only we are less “practical.” Instead of studying the peoples we conquer we deliver them over to the caprices of the sword; we subject them to the regime of the “mother country;” if these peoples cannot bend to it, so much the worse for them! They will disappear little by little under the degenerating influence of an administration to which they are not accustomed. What of it? If they revolt we hunt them like wild beasts, track them like deer! Pillage in that case is not only tolerated but approved; it is called a “raid.” The ferocious beast which we train and keep, under the name of “soldier,” is let loose upon inoffensive peoples. The latter behold themselves delivered over to every excess which these unchained brutes can conceive; their women are violated, their children’s throats are cut, whole villages are given to the flames, entire populations are driven into the plains where they are destined to perish miserably.
===Chinese===
殖民

殖民这回事，在当今时代蔓延得实在太广，我们不能不单独来谈一谈这个由爱国主义与重商主义（mercantilism）相结合而生出的杂交产物——为统治阶级的利益而行的匪行与拦路抢劫！一个平民闯进邻人的家，碰到什么砸什么，发现什么合用就抢走什么：他就是罪犯，社会要惩办他。可是一个政府，倘若被内部局势逼得走投无路，需要某种对外的“转圜”；倘若它在本土被失业的人手拖累——它不知怎样甩掉这些人手——又被积压的产品拖累——它无法把这些产品分销出去；那么，让这个政府向它明知虚弱得无力抵抗的遥远民族宣战吧，让它占领他们的国土，使他们屈服于一整套剥削制度，把它的产品强加给他们，一旦他们企图逃脱这套压得他们直不起腰的剥削，就把他们屠杀——哦，这样一来，这就是道德的了！只要你是大规模地干，便配得上正人君子的赞许。这不再叫抢劫或暗杀；掩盖政府所干无耻勾当的，自有一个体面的字眼：这叫做“开化”落后的民族。

谁也不要以为这是夸大。一个国族，只有当它成功地从一块土地榨取到那块地所能出产的最大限度的产品时，才算得上殖民的国族。比如英格兰（England）便是殖民国家，因为她懂得如何用她派出去统治殖民地的人的繁荣去“酬报”她的殖民地，懂得如何把她加在他们头上的赋税敛回自己的金库。譬如在东印度（the Indies），她派出去的人个个发下骇人的大财。诚然，那片国土时不时被骇人的饥馑彻底蹂躏，成十万人成十万人地被刈倒。可是，只要约翰牛（John Bull）卖得出他的工业品，并由此为自己捞到不列颠的土壤产不出的东西，这些细节又何足挂齿？这便是殖民的种种好处！

在法国（France），情形就不同了；我们不是殖民者。哦，请放心；这并不是说我们的匪气便轻些，我们征服的人民受的剥削便少些！不；只是我们不那么“讲求实效”罢了。我们不去研究我们所征服的人民，反倒把他们交给刀剑的性子去摆布；我们使他们屈从于“母国”的制度；这些民族若是弯不下这个腰，那就更加活该！他们会在一种自己不习惯的行政管理的败坏影响下渐渐消亡。那又何妨？他们若是造反，我们就把他们当野兽追猎，像逐鹿一般穷追！在这种情况下，劫掠不仅被容忍，还受到赞许；这叫做“讨伐”（“raid”）。我们豢养的那头名叫“士兵”的凶兽，被放出去扑向无害的人民。后者眼看着自己被交给这些挣脱了锁链的畜生所能想出的一切暴行去摆布：他们的妇女遭奸淫，他们的孩子被割断喉咙，整座整座的村庄被付之一炬，全族全族的人口被驱进平原，注定在那里凄惨地死绝。
===Original===
Is that all? Let it pass; it is a civilized nation carrying civilization to savages!

Certainly, upon thorough examination of what goes on around us there is nothing illogical or abnormal in all this; it is, in fact, the result of our present organization. It is nothing astonishing that these high feats of arms obtain the approval and applause of the bourgeois world. The bourgeoisie is interested in these strokes of brigandage; they serve as a pretext for maintaining permanent armies; they occupy the praetorians who, during these slaughters, set their hands to more serious “labor;” these armies themselves serve to unload a whole pack of idiots and worthless persons by whom the bourgeoisie would be much embarrassed, and who, by virtue of a few yards of gilt stripes, are made their most furious defenders. These conquests facilitate an entire series of financial schemes by means of which they may skim off the savings of speculators in search of doubtful enterprises. They will monopolize the stolen or conquered lands. These wars cause massacres of workers whose excessive numbers embarrass them; the conquered countries being in “need” of an administration, there is a new market for a whole army of office-seekers and ambitious persons whom they thus harness to their chariot, whereas had these latter remained unemployed its route might have been hampered thereby. Still better, there are peoples to exploit, to be yoked in their service, upon whom their products may be forced, whom they may decimate without being held accountable to anyone. In view of these advantages the bourgeoisie need not hesitate; and the French bourgeoisie have so well understood this that they have launched headlong into colonial enterprises. But what astonishes and disheartens us is that there are workers who approve of these infamies; who feel no remorse in lending a hand to these rascalities, and do not understand the flagrant injustice of massacring people in their own homes, in order to mould them to a way of living not natural to them. Oh, we know the ready-made rejoinders which it is customary to make to those who become indignant at too flagrant injustices: “They have revolted, they have killed our people; we cannot endure it.﻿ ﻿… They are savages, they must be civilized.﻿ ﻿… The needs of commerce require it.﻿ ﻿… Yes, perhaps it was wrong to go among them in the first place, but the colonies have cost us too many men, too much money, to abandon them now,” etc.
===Chinese===
这就是全部了吗？由它去吧；那是一个文明民族在把文明送给野蛮人嘛！

诚然，细细审视我们周围发生的一切，这一切毫无悖谬反常之处；事实上，这正是我们现今组织的结果。这些赫赫武功博得资产阶级世界的赞许与喝彩，丝毫不足为奇。资产阶级在这些匪行里有其利益：它们是维持常备军的借口；它们让那些禁卫军（praetorians）有事可忙——这些人在此等屠杀之余，正好腾出手来干更“严肃”的“劳动”；这些军队本身又可以用来安插一大帮白痴和废物——资产阶级本会被他们搞得十分难堪——而这些人凭几码镀金的绦带，摇身变成资产阶级最狂热的捍卫者。这些征服为整整一系列金融骗局开了方便之门，借这些骗局，他们可以把那些钻营可疑企业的投机者的积蓄撇捞到手。他们还要垄断偷来的和征服来的土地。这些战争造成对工人的屠杀——工人过剩的人数正使他们难堪；被征服的国家既然“需要”行政管理，对整整一大群谋官者和野心家便有了新市场，资产阶级就此把他们套上自己的战车——这些人若依旧闲着无事，战车的前路反倒可能受阻。更妙的是，有了可供剥削、可供套上辕为他们服役的民族，有了可以强销产品、可以任意屠戮而毋须向任何人交代的民族。有鉴于此，资产阶级无需迟疑；法国的资产阶级对此领会得如此透彻，以致已经不顾一切地扎进了殖民事业。但令我们诧异、令我们痛心的是：竟有工人赞成这些丑行；竟有工人助纣为虐而毫无内疚，竟不明白为了把人们塑造成一种悖逆其天性的生活方式而在他们自己的家园里屠杀他们，是何等彰明昭著的不义。哦，我们晓得那些现成的答辩，人们惯于拿去堵那些对太露骨的不义拍案而起者的嘴：“他们造反了，他们杀了我们的人；我们咽不下这口气。……他们是野蛮人，必须开化他们。……商业的需要要求如此。……是的，当初跑到他们那里去或许是错了，可殖民地已经耗费了我们这么多的人命、这么多的钱，如今断不能弃之而去。”云云。
===Original===
“They have revolted; they have killed our men!” Well, what else? What were we doing in their country? Why did we not let them alone? Did they ever come and ask anything of us? We have tried to impose laws upon them which they do not want to accept. They have revolted; they have done well. So much the worse for those of us who perish in the struggle; they should have refrained from participating in these infamies.

“They are savages; they must be civilized.” Let anyone take up the history of conquests, and then tell us which were the most savage﻿—those who were called so, or the “civilized.” Which are in greatest need of being civilized, the conquerors or the inoffensive peoples who generally welcomed their invaders with open arms, and as the reward for their advances have been tortured and decimated? Take the history of the conquests in America by Spain, of India by England, of Africa, Cochin China, and Tonkin by France, and then boast about “civilization.” Remember, too, that in these histories you will find recorded only the “great events,” whose importance has left traces; but if you were to picture to yourself all the “little events” of which these are composed and which pass by unperceived; if you were to bring to light all the turpitudes which are absorbed in the imposing mass of the principal facts, then what would it be? You would recoil affrighted before these horrors!

For ourselves, having spent some time in the naval service, we have listened to the description of numbers of scenes which prove that when a soldier arrives in a conquered country, he considers himself, by that mere fact, absolute master therein; for him the natives are beasts of burden, which he may order about at will; he has the right to seize upon every object which suits him; woe to the native that would oppose him! He will not be slow in teaching that the law of the sword is the only law;﻿—the institution which protects property in Europe does not recognize it in another latitude. And in all this the soldier is encouraged by the officers who preach by example, by the administration which puts the cudgel in his hand that he may superintend the natives it employs upon its works. How many repugnant actions are naively recounted to you as altogether natural occurrences! If you happen to say of some native who revolted and killed his oppressor, that he did well, you should hear the cries of stupefaction which greet your remark! “What! Since we are the masters, since we command them, they must obey us; if we let them alone they would all revolt, they would drive us out! After having spent so much money and so many men, France would lose the country! She would have no more colonies!” Behold what an effect military discipline and brutalization have upon the minds of the workers. They endure the same injustices, the same turpitudes, with which they are helping to burden others; and they no longer feel the ignominy of their conduct; they have come to serve, unconsciously, as the instruments of despotism and to boast of this role, not realizing its baseness and infamy.
===Chinese===
“他们造反了；他们杀了我们的人！”好，还有什么可说的？我们在他们的国家里做什么来着？我们为什么不放过他们？他们可曾上门向我们讨要过什么？我们硬要把他们不愿接受的法律强加给他们。他们造反了；他们干得好。我们当中有谁死在这场斗争里，那是他活该倒霉；他本就不该去参与这些丑行。

“他们是野蛮人；必须开化他们。”那么请谁去翻一翻征服的历史，然后告诉我们：究竟哪一边最野蛮——是被如此称呼的那些人，还是那些“文明人”？哪一边最需要开化——是征服者，还是那些通常张开双臂欢迎入侵者、而其亲善换来的报偿却是酷刑加屠戮的无害人民？去翻一翻西班牙（Spain）征服美洲、英格兰征服印度、法国征服非洲、交趾支那（Cochin China）和东京（Tonkin）的历史，然后再来夸耀“文明”吧。还请记住：在这些史册里，被记录下来的只是那些因分量重而留下痕迹的“大事”；但倘若你把这等大事所由构成、却悄然溜过去的全部“小事”一一想象出来；倘若你把被主要事实的堂皇体量所吞没的一切卑劣勾当统统揭到光天化日之下，那将会是怎样一番景象？你会在这等恐怖面前骇然却步！

至于我们自己，曾在海军里服过一段兵役，听人讲述过许多场面，足以证明：士兵一到被征服的国家，便凭这一层身份自居为那里的绝对主人；在他眼里，土著就是驮畜，任他随意驱使；凡是合他意的东西，他都有权攫取；哪个土著胆敢违拗，那就遭殃！用不了多久，他就会让人领教：刀剑的法则便是唯一的法则；——那在欧洲护卫着私有制的制度，换了一个纬度便翻脸不认。而在这一切之中怂恿着他的，是以身作则布道的军官，是把棍棒塞进他手里、好让他去监押本衙门工程所雇土著的行政当局。有多少令人发指的行径，被人当作再自然不过的事情天真地讲给你听！倘若你顺口夸一句某个造反杀死自己压迫者的土著干得好，你真该听听这句话会招来怎样的惊呼！“什么！既然我们是主人，既然命令由我们下，他们就得服从；若是放了他们，他们人人都会造反，会把我们赶出去！花了这么多钱、这么多条人命，法国就要丢掉那片地方了！她就再没有殖民地了！”瞧瞧军事纪律与兽化在工人头脑里造成的是怎样的效果。他们一面帮着把同样的不义、同样的卑劣压上别人的肩头，一面又亲身受着这同样的不义与卑劣；他们再也感觉不到自己行径的耻辱；他们已经不自觉地充当起专制的工具，还为这个角色自鸣得意，全然不悟其中下贱与可耻。
===Original===
As to “the needs of commerce,” here, indeed, we have the genuine motive. Messieurs the bourgeois being embarrassed with products which they cannot dispose of, find nothing better to do than to go and declare war against poor devils powerless to defend themselves, in order to impose these products upon them. To be sure it would be easy enough to come to an understanding with them; one might traffic with them by means of barter, not being overscrupulous, even, about the value of the objects exchanged; these latter being valueless to them save when attractive to the eye, it would be easy enough to get the best of them and realize fine profits therefrom. Was it not thus before the dark continent was penetrated? Were we not, through the intermediary of the coast tribes in communication with the tribes of the interior? Did we not get the same products then as we get now?﻿—“Yes, it is possible that it was so, but the devil of it is that to operate in such a way takes time and patience; it is impossible to go in on a grand scale; one must figure on competition; ‘commerce must be protected.’ ”﻿—We know what that means: two or three fast ironclads, in double-quick order, half-a-dozen gunboats, a body of troops to be landed﻿—salute! Civilization is going to perform its work! We have taken a people, strong, robust, and healthy; in forty or fifty years from now we shall have them turned into a horde of anaemics, brutalized, miserable, decimated, corrupted, who will shortly disappear from the surface of the globe. Then the civilizing job will be finished!

If anyone doubt what we here assert let him take the accounts of travelers, let him read the descriptions of those countries in which Europeans have installed themselves by the right of conquest: everywhere the native populations decrease and disappear; everywhere drunkenness, syphilis, and other European importations mow them down in great swaths, atrophy and anaemiate those who survive. And can it be otherwise? No, not when such means are employed! Here are peoples who have another mode of life than ours, other aptitudes, other needs; instead of studying these needs and aptitudes, seeking to adapt them to our civilization gradually, insensibly, not demanding that they take any more of it than they can assimilate, we try to bend them to it at a single blow, we break everything asunder; and not only do they become refractory but the experience is fatal to them.
===Chinese===
至于“商业的需要”，嘿，这里我们才碰到了真正的动机。资产者先生们被卖不出去的产品憋得发慌，于是想不出比这更好的主意：跑去向那些无力自卫的可怜虫宣战，好把这些产品强加给他们。诚然，同他们达成谅解本是再容易不过的事；本可以同他们以物易物地做买卖，甚至对交换之物的价值也不必过分拘泥；这些东西在他们手里本是一钱不值的，除非看着中意，所以要占他们的上风、从中捞取漂亮的利润，实在是易如反掌。在黑暗大陆（dark continent）被深入之前，情形不正是如此吗？我们不是通过沿海部落同内陆部落互通声气吗？我们那时到手的产品，不与现在到手的一样吗？——“是的，也许是那样，可糟糕就糟糕在：那样干既费时间又费耐心；没法子大规模地干；还得估量着竞争；‘商业必须受到保护。’”——我们晓得这是什么意思：两三艘快速铁甲舰全速进发，六艘炮艇，一支待登陆的部队——敬礼！文明这就开工了！我们拿到手的是一个强壮、结实、健康的民族；再过四五十年，我们就会把他们变成一大帮贫血孱弱、深受兽化、穷困潦倒、凋零过半、腐化堕落的人，他们不久就会从地球表面消失。到那时，开化的大功便告成了！

谁若怀疑我们这里的断言，就让他去读旅行家们的记述，读一读那些欧洲人凭征服之权立足之地的描写吧：处处都是土著人口在减少、在消失；处处都是酗酒、梅毒（syphilis）和种种欧洲舶来品把他们成大片地刈倒，把幸存者熬得肌体萎缩、血液贫竭。难道还能有别的结果吗？不，用这类手段便注定如此！这些民族有着与我们不同的生活方式，不同的禀赋，不同的需要；我们不去研究这些需要与禀赋，不去设法让他们循序渐进、悄无声息地适应我们的文明，不强求他们吸纳超出其同化能力的东西，反而企图一举把他们折弯，把一切都砸个稀碎；于是他们非但变得顽抗，这场经验对他们更是致命的。
===Original===
How glorious might the role of the so-called civilized man have been, had he but understood it, and had not he himself been afflicted with these two pests, government and mercantilism﻿—two frightful plagues, of which he would do well to consider how to rid himself before seeking to civilize others. The education of undeveloped tribes might go on peacefully and bring into civilization new elements, capable in the course of their adaptation, of putting new life into it. Let no one talk to us of the duplicity and ferocity of the barbarians. We have but to read the accounts of those truly courageous men who have gone into the midst of unknown tribes, urged on solely by the ideal of science and the desire for knowledge. Such persons have succeeded in making friends of these people, have gone among them, having nothing to fear; duplicity and ferocity came in only with these miserable traffickers who falsely decorate themselves with the name of travelers, seeing nothing in their travels but a good commercial or political deal. They have excited the animosity of these peoples against the whites by cheating them in their exchanges, by failing to keep their agreements, by massacring them, if need be, when they could do it with impunity. Go to, go to, philanthropists of commerce, civilizers by the sword! Forbear your tirades on the benefits of civilization! That which you call thus, that which you disguise under the name of colonization, has a name perfectly denned in your code, when it is the act of a few obscure individuals: it is called “pillage and assassination by armed bands.” But civilization has nothing in common with your highway-robber practices!

What the ruling classes must have is new markets for their products and new peoples to exploit; for this they send out their Solcillets, their de Brazzas, their Crampels, Triviers, etc., in search of unknown territories, there to open up factories which shall deliver these countries over to their unlimited exploitation. They commence by exploiting commercially and finish by exploiting in every way, when once these tribes have been brought under their protectorate. What they stand in need of is immense tracts of earth which they may gradually annex after having depopulated them;﻿—do they not need plenty of room whereinto they may divert the surplus population which embarrasses them? What have you done with the tribes of Polynesia, which all travelers agreed in depicting to us as strong and vigorous peoples, and who are now disappearing under your rule, you “civilizers”?

But at the rate your civilization is going on, if the workers are bound to succumb to the struggle to which you deliver them up, you, in your turn, will not be long in succumbing likewise under your indolence and laziness, even as fell the Greek and Roman civilizations, which having reached the pitch of luxury and exploitation, having lost all the faculties of struggle, in preserving the faculty of enjoyment, succumbed much more under the pressure of their own bloated nervelessness than to the blows of the barbarians, who, entering into the struggle in the fullness of their strength, had no great trouble to overturn this rapidly decaying civilization. As you have undertaken to destroy these races﻿—not inferior, as we shall show later on, but merely retarded﻿—you tend in like manner to destroy the working class, which you also qualify as inferior. Day by day you seek to eliminate the worker from the workshop, replacing him by machines. Your triumph would be the end of humanity; for, losing little by little the faculties acquired by the necessity for struggle, you would return to the most rudimentary ancestral forms of society and humanity would soon have no other ideal than that of an association of digestive sacs commanding a nation of machines, waited upon by automatons, having nothing human left but the name.
===Chinese===
倘若所谓的文明人当初懂得自己的角色，倘若他自己不曾染上政府与重商主义这两大痼疾——两场可怕的瘟疫，他最好先琢磨怎样把自己从它们那里解脱出来，再去开化别人——那么，他本可以扮演的角色该是多么光荣。对未开化部落的教育本可以和平地进行，为文明输送新的成分，这些成分在其适应过程中本可以给文明注入新的生命。谁也不要同我们奢谈野蛮人的奸诈与凶残。我们只消读一读那些真正勇敢的人的记述——他们单单被科学的理想和求知的欲望所驱策，深入到无名部落的腹地。这样的人成功地同那些人结成了朋友，走到他们中间而无所畏惧；奸诈与凶残，只是随着那帮卑劣的贩子才来的——他们盗用旅行家之名自饰，在他们的“旅行”里除了看见一桩好买卖或好政治交易之外什么也看不见。他们在交换中欺骗那些民族，背弃自己的约定，一旦无人能追究便对他们大加屠戮——正是他们激起了那些民族对白人的仇恨。去吧，去吧，商业的慈善家们，持剑的开化者们！收起你们那套关于文明恩惠的高论吧！你们如此称道、又用殖民之名装点的东西，在你们的法典里，当它出自几个无名之辈之手时，有着再明白不过的名目：它叫做“武装匪帮的劫掠与暗杀”。而文明与你们那套拦路抢劫的营生毫无共同之处！

统治阶级所需要的，是为他们的产品找到新市场，是可以剥削的新民族；为此，他们派出他们的索莱耶（Solcillets）、德·布拉柴（de Brazzas）、克朗佩尔（Crampels）、特里维耶（Triviers）之流，去寻找未知的疆土，在那里开设商馆（factories），好把这些国家交给他们去无限制地剥削。他们先从商业剥削开张，待这些部落一旦被收进他们的保护领（protectorate），便以各式各样的剥削收场。他们需要的是大片大片的土地，好在把人清空之后逐步兼并；——他们不是正需要充裕的空地，来疏导那些使他们难堪的过剩人口吗？你们拿波利尼西亚（Polynesia）的各个部落怎么样了？所有旅行家都曾众口一词地向我们描绘他们是强壮而生气勃勃的民族，如今却正在你们的统治之下消失——你们这帮“开化者”！

然而，照你们的文明这副走法，倘若工人们注定要败亡于你们把他们投入的那场斗争，那么轮到你们，也将在自己的怠惰与懒惰之下不久同样败亡，一如希腊、罗马文明的倾覆：那两个文明登上了奢侈与剥削的极顶，丧失了斗争的一切能力，只保全了享乐的能力，与其说亡于蛮族的打击，不如说亡于自身浮肿的萎靡所压出的重负——蛮族挟精力的全盛投入斗争，不费大气力便掀翻了这迅速腐烂的文明。正如你们已经着手毁灭这些种族——如我们后文将论证的，他们并非低劣，而只是迟滞——你们也同样在趋于毁灭工人阶级，你们还把工人阶级也说成低劣的。你们一天一天地设法把工人逐出工场，用机器取而代之。你们的胜利将是人类的末日；因为，随着你们一点点丧失那因斗争之必要而习得的能力，你们将退回到最粗陋的祖先式社会形态，人类不久便不会再有别的理想，只剩下一个消化囊的联合体，统辖着一个机器的国度，由自动机器伺候左右，除了“人”这个名目，再无半点属人的东西。

## XV There Are No Inferior Races / XV 并无劣等种族

===Original===
There Are No Inferior Races

This question of colonization immediately brings up that of the so-called inferior races: for have not those actions of the whites that have led to the extinction of these conquered peoples been attempted to be justified by the argument of so-called race inferiority? Is this not, moreover, the same argument employed against the worker to justify the exploitation to which he is subjected by taxing him with belonging to the inferior classes? Does it mean, then, that for the capitalist, and even for certain savants, the worker is but a beast of burden whose sole role consists in creating prosperity for the “elect,” and reproducing other beasts of burden who in their turn may work out the happiness of the descendants of the “elect,” and so on? However, we workers do not believe ourselves lower than anybody else; we believe our brains to be quite as capable of cultivation as those of our exploiters if we but had the leisure and means for it. Why should it not be the same with races said to be inferior?

===Chinese===
并无劣等种族

殖民这个问题立刻引出了所谓劣等种族（inferior races）的问题：白人的那些招致这些被征服民族归于灭绝的行径，不是有人试图拿所谓种族劣等来为之辩解么？再者，指责工人属于下层阶级、从而为他所遭受的剥削开脱的，不正是同一种论调么？莫非在资本家、甚至在某些学者（savants）看来，工人不过是一头驮畜，唯一的职责便是为“选民”（elect）创造富足，再繁育出别的驮畜，好让它们转而又为“选民”的后代操劳幸福，如此生生不已？然而我们工人并不自认比谁低一等；我们相信，只要有闲暇和条件，我们的头脑同剥削我们的人的头脑一样堪资栽培。那么，对那些被说成劣等的种族，为什么就不会是这样呢？

===Original===
If it were nobody but the politicians who asserted the inferiority of races an attempt to refute their assertion would be quite unnecessary; at the bottom of their hearts they care very little whether the assertion be proven or disproven, since it is but a pretext anyway; if it were shown to be false they would not fail to find others. But certain savants have tried to bring science to the support of this theory and prove that the white race is a superior race. There was a time when man believed himself to be the centre of the universe; he not only thought that the sun and stars revolved around the earth, but he declared that all this had been so created with a sole view to his advent. This is called the anthropocentric theory. Long centuries of study were required to eradicate these vain illusions, and make man understand the insignificant place he held in nature. But his ideas of domination are so strong and tenacious, he renounces them with so much difficulty, that after having lost the sceptre which he claimed over the stars he fell back upon the declaration that the terrestrial globe with all its products had been made on purpose to serve as a cradle for him, the king of creation. Again dispossessed of this fictitious kingdom by science, which shows that he is but the product of evolution, the result of a concurrence of fortuitous circumstances, that there was nothing premeditated in his coming into being, and consequently, that nothing could have been created with a view to his advent, man’s spirit of domination was incapable of resolving to accept the facts as they are and consider itself an intruder; summing everything up it re-entrenched itself in this idea of superior races, and as a matter of course every race declared itself to be the most intelligent, the most beautiful, the most perfect. It is in virtue of this declaration that the white race absorbs all others, and upon their elimination the savants base the declaration!

===Chinese===
倘若断言种族劣等的只是政客，那实在用不着费神去驳斥：在他们心底，这断言证实也罢、驳倒也罢，他们全不在乎，反正它本来就不过是个借口；纵使证明它站不住，他们也不愁找到别的。可是，某些学者却力图把科学拉来支援这套理论，证明白色人种（white race）是一个优等种族。曾经有一个时代，人自以为是宇宙的中心；他不仅以为太阳与群星绕地旋转，还宣称这一切的创造，唯独着眼于他的降临。这就是所谓人类中心论（anthropocentric theory）。需要绵延许多个世纪的研究，才得以根除这些虚妄的幻念，让人懂得自己在自然中所占的位置何其微末。然而他的统治观念是如此顽强而固执，要他放弃又是如此困难，以致在失去了自命对群星握有的权杖之后，他又退而宣称：地球连同它的全部出产，本是特意造出来给他——给这位造物之王——当摇篮的。科学再度剥夺了他这个虚构的王位，向他表明他不过是进化的产物，是一系列偶然情况汇聚的结果，他的出世没有丝毫预谋，因而也不可能有任何东西是着眼于他的降临而创造的；然而人的统治精神不肯下定决心按事实本来的样子接受它们、把自己看作一个闯入者；它通盘权衡之下，索性退守到优等种族这个观念里，于是顺理成章地，每个种族都宣称自己最聪明、最美、最完美。正是凭着这一宣告，白色人种吞并了一切其他种族，而学者们竟又把这一宣告建立在其他种族之被消灭上！

===Original===
The savants have, moreover, endeavored to justify their opinion by propping it upon the three following supports:

First, the antiquity of the inferior races is explicitly recognized by the scientific world as equal to that of the white race; consequently the stationary condition of the former, whereas the latter have progressed, proves absolute inferiority;﻿—

Second, undeveloped peoples generally inhabit the most favored climates which ought to have helped to hasten their development;﻿—

Third, savage children whom it has been sought to bring up in European fashion have in nowise fulfilled the hopes of their educators.

===Chinese===
此外，学者们还力图把他们的意见支在下面三根支柱上：

第一，劣等种族之古老，已为科学界明确承认与白色人种相等；因此，前者停滞不前而后者有所进步，便证明了绝对的劣等性；——

第二，落后民族大都栖居于最得天独厚的气候之中，而这种气候本应有助于加速他们的发展；——

第三，人们曾试图按欧式培养的那些野蛮人儿童，丝毫没有实现其教育者的期望。

===Original===
Further examples are given in the cases of those agglomerations of savages penned up in villages, which have remained unchanged for hundreds of years, as well as the negro republic of Haiti with its endless revolutions.

One need not go very far into history to discover that the “universal concensus” is not always proof. Up to the time that Galileo proved that the earth revolved around the sun it had been almost universally believed that it was the sun which revolved around the earth. Universal consent, therefore, proves nothing if it be not supported by facts; and again, in the case cited above the apparent facts seemed to support the erroneous opinion. Do the facts corroborate the opinion that all races are of equal antiquity? This is what we want to know.

===Chinese===
人们还进一步举出一些例子：那些圈禁在村落里的野蛮人聚落，数百年间一成不变；还有那个革命不休的黑人共和国海地。

用不着往历史深处走多远便可发现，“普遍的共识”并不总是证明。直到伽利略（Galileo）证明地球绕太阳旋转之前，人们几乎普遍相信是太阳绕地球旋转。可见，普遍的同意若没有事实支撑，便什么也证明不了；而在上面所引的例子里，表面的事实看似恰恰还在支持那个错误的意见。那么，事实果真能证实一切种族同等古老的见解么？这正是我们想要知道的。

===Original===
Upon the Egyptian monuments there have been found representations of certain African types still existing in our days, which would, indeed, prove a relative antiquity; it is also averred that these tribes, formerly subject to the Egyptians, do not appear to have progressed. At first glance this would seem to be in favor of the partisans of the inferiority of races, but a deeper examination would show this conclusion to be too hasty. As a matter of fact the admitted antiquity of the Egyptian monuments is about eight thousand years; let us put it in round numbers at ten thousand. Thus in ten thousand years these tribes do not appear to have progressed, while the white race has made that progress with which we are familiar. But at the epoch when these monuments were raised Egypt already represented a very advanced civilization; the difference between these backward tribes and the builders of the temples of Philae, Karnak, and Memphis was already enormous; the Egyptians had already passed through the prehistoric period which is estimated at hundreds of thousands of years.17 Slow indeed must have been the progress of man during the quarternary epoch, and the period of development is still longer if the existence of man during the tertiary epoch be admitted. The ten thousand years of stagnation of the tribes in question represent, then, but an insignificant amount in the history of the unfoldment of humanity, and it is probable that ten thousand years after he had cut the first stone the primitive Egyptian would have presented hardly any sensible improvement to the observer, and would also have appeared to be of a fundamentally inferior race. On the other hand these Egyptians, who made the great progress attested by their science and their monuments, are not whites; and this same people, which is classed among the “superior” races of antiquity, is now classed among the “inferior” races. Its English overlords have thoroughly demonstrated *that*. What a mass of contradictions! For the necessities of the argument the Egyptians are alternately both “superior” and “inferior.”

===Chinese===
在埃及的古迹上，人们发现了某些直至今日犹存的非洲人类型的形象，这确实可以证明一种相对的古老；人们还断言，这些昔日臣服于埃及人的部落，似乎并无进步。乍看之下，这似乎有利于种族劣等论的信徒；但更深一层的考察会表明，这个结论下得太仓促了。事实上，埃及古迹公认的古老程度约为八千年；我们且凑个整数，算作一万年。于是一万年过去，这些部落似乎毫无进步，而白色人种却取得了我们熟悉的那种种进步。可是，在这些古迹竖立起来的时代，埃及已经代表着一种十分发达的文明；这些落后部落与菲莱（Philae）、卡纳克（Karnak）、孟菲斯（Memphis）诸神庙的建造者之间的差距，那时已经极为悬殊；埃及人早已走过了据估计长达数十万年的史前时期。17 人类在第四纪（quaternary epoch）期间的进步想必确实缓慢，而倘若承认人类在第三纪（tertiary epoch）即已存在，发展的时期还要更长。可见上述部落那一万年的停滞，在人类开展的历程上不过微不足道的一小段；而且，原始的埃及人在凿下第一块石头之后的一万年里，多半也不会向观察者呈现出什么显著的进步，同样会显得属于一个根本劣等的种族。另一方面，这些以自己的科学与古迹证明了巨大进步的埃及人，并不是白人；而这同一个民族，昔日被列入“优等”种族，如今却被归入“劣等”种族。它的英国主子们已经把*这一点*演示得淋漓尽致。多么一大堆矛盾啊！为了论证的需要，埃及人时而“优等”，时而“劣等”。

===Original===
The skulls and jawbones of Cro-Magnon, Neanderthal, and Naulette, which date back to a remote epoch, present characteristics so simian that while studying them the anthropologists were in doubt whether to class their possessors among the ancestors of man or among the large anthropoid apes. In the presence of such humble beginnings are we happy in designating ourselves “the phoenix of humanity”? And by what right do we speak of the inferiority of other races when their present condition proceeds from our barbarous persecutions? Thus the present inferiority of the red-skinned race proves nothing; for let it not be forgotten that the aboriginal civilizations which were developing at the time of the European conquests were destroyed by their invaders; and the descendents of the aborigines, hunted, despoiled, massacred, were compelled little by little to retreat and be annihilated before the conqueror. Highly flourishing civilizations thus disappeared, no one knowing what they might have brought forth; we cannot judge of them by the brutalized and degenerate natives which the United States is in the course of wiping out. I will not instance in example the Empire of Mexico nor that of the Incas; upon the arrival of the Spaniards these empires were already on the highway to decay. For that very reason they were unable to resist. The Hurons, the Iroquois defended themselves with much greater energy than the Aztecs and Peruvians.

===Chinese===
克罗马农（Cro-Magnon）、尼安德特（Neanderthal）和瑙莱特（Naulette）的头骨与颌骨，年代可以追溯到遥远的地质时代，所呈现的猿类特征如此显著，以致人类学家研究它们时，一时拿不准该把它们的拥有者归入人类的祖先，还是归入大型类人猿。面对如此卑微的开端，我们以“人类的凤凰”自命，可还觉得光彩么？再者，其他种族今日的境况既然出自我们野蛮的迫害，我们凭什么侈谈他们的劣等？所以红种人（redskin）今日的劣等什么也证明不了；且不要忘记，欧洲人征服之时正在发展中的那些土著文明，是被入侵者摧毁的；土著的后裔被追猎、被劫掠、被屠戮，被迫一步步退却，在征服者面前归于消灭。高度繁荣的文明就这样消失了，无人知晓它们本可能孕育出什么；我们无从凭合众国正在着手剿灭的那些横遭摧残、业已蜕化的土人去评判它们。我且不举墨西哥的帝国、也不举印加人（Incas）的帝国为例；西班牙人到来之时，这两个帝国已经走在衰亡的通衢上了。正因如此，它们才无力抵抗。休伦人（Hurons）、易洛魁人（Iroquois）自卫时所表现的劲头，远比阿兹特克人（Aztecs）和秘鲁人顽强。

===Original===
One might suppose that in order to prove the equal antiquity of races there remained one last resource﻿—that of making researches in as yet unexplored strata and comparing the age of the skeletons which would certainly be found therein. But this resource is illusive; no possible means of establishing the exact concordance of the formation of earthy strata in the divers parts of the world exists. How then can we discover a perfect concordance between the remains found in these strata? To sum up, this question of the equal antiquity of races is an insoluble one and quite without value in the solution of the problem of virtual equality. Has it the least importance to those who consider all progress derived from the incessantly changing influence of environments?

“Undeveloped peoples generally inhabit the most favored countries,” declared Prof. G. Herve, one of the partisans of the theory of inferiority of races, in one of his courses on zoological anthropology at the Anthropological College. This declaration remains to be proven. Can it be applied to the Eskimo or the inhabitants of Tierra del Fuego; to the Redskins, destitute of all those animals which they might have domesticated; or to the negroes who live in the region of the marshes of the Nile, or the endless forests of the Congo; or to the Tungus of the Siberian steppes; or to the Bushmen of the waterless deserts of Kalahari? One should not distort the truth so violently. And besides there remains to be decided the momentous question: which *are* the most favored countries, those which require labor or those which do not?

===Chinese===
人们或许以为，要证明各种族同等古老，还剩下最后一条路——到尚未考察过的地层里去研究，比较其中必然会发现的骸骨的年代。但这条路是虚幻的；世上不存在任何可能的办法，能确立世界各地区泥质地层在形成上的确切对应。既然如此，我们又怎能查明这些地层中所见遗骸之间的完全对应呢？总而言之，种族同等古老这个问题是无法解决的，而且对解决潜在平等的问题毫无价值。在那些认为一切进步皆得自环境不断变迁之影响的人看来，它又有何等要紧呢？

“落后民族大都栖居于最得天独厚的国土”，种族劣等论的拥护者之一 G. 阿尔韦教授（Prof. G. Herve）在人类学学校（Anthropological College）讲授动物人类学的一门课程里如此宣称。这项断言还有待证明。它能用之于爱斯基摩人（Eskimo）或火地岛（Tierra del Fuego）的居民么？能用之于那些身边全无可供驯养之动物的红种人么？能用之于生活在尼罗河沼泽地带或刚果无边森林里的黑人么？能用之于西伯利亚草原上的通古斯人（Tungus），或喀拉哈里（Kalahari）无水荒漠里的布须曼人（Bushmen）么？总不该如此粗暴地歪曲真理吧。况且还有一个重大问题尚待裁决：究竟哪些国土*才是*得天独厚的——是需要劳动的国土，还是不需要劳动的国土？

===Original===
Furthermore this declaration may quite as easily be turned against the position which it is intended to defend. Is it not precisely this facility of existence which has left many tribes stationary? Having wherewith to satisfy their primary needs without work, men may very easily have failed to experience that quickening of faculties which continued to lie dormant, whilst other peoples forced to wrest their daily existence from soil and climate, have been led to develop instincts and faculties which in turn have awakened others, and thus launched them upon the pathway of progress. The others, being “favored,” could live without exertion.

Then follow the arguments drawn from the attempts to educate certain African tribes, or from colonies of savages which, it is claimed, have been left to develop themselves in villages on their reservations. It may be that there have been unfruitful attempts at such education; that would prove no general conclusion, seeing that questions would arise as to what were the conditions under which these attempts were made, in what situation was the group experimented upon found, and whether causes of degeneracy had not been suffered to remain. These examples prove so much less since there are also instances of the contrary. The Iroquois of Canada are in every respect equal to the whites around them. The greatest geographer of Mexico is an Aztec, and we have the satisfaction of knowing that the “greatest warriors of the world” were neatly turned out of Mexico by the descendants of the “inferior races.” Many generations are necessary to render any new acquisition permanent; however great its powers of development no individual brain can, during the course of its single existence, go through that evolution which it required entire generations for the race to go through. Negative results in individuals, therefore, prove nothing, even granting the attempt to have been made under practical conditions, for they may be offset by many positive results just as the progress of the whites may be disputed on account of many instances of retrogression. Do not ethnographical works quote the cases of redskins, negroes, or other “savages” who have been successfully instructed, and who had attained a considerable degree of knowledge, but who, seized with contempt for what had been taught them, have thrown aside the garments of civilization to resume their nomadic existence?

===Chinese===
再者，这项断言也同样容易反过来打到它想要捍卫的立场上。使许多部落陷于停滞的，不正是这种生存的便利么？既然不劳作便有办法满足首要的需要，人们便很可能始终不曾经历那种能力的激励——它于是一直沉睡着；而另一些被迫向土壤与气候夺取日常生存的民族，却不得不发展出种种本能与能力，这些本能与能力又唤醒了别的，于是把他们推上了进步的通路。另一些人呢，既然“得天独厚”，便可以毫不费力地活着。

接着便是从教育某些非洲部落的尝试中、或从那些据称被留在保留地（reservations）村落里自行发展的野蛮人聚落中引出的论证。这类教育的尝试可能有劳而无功的；但那证明不了任何普遍结论，因为随之便会生出问题：这些尝试是在什么条件下进行的？用作实验的群体当时处于什么境地？造成蜕化的种种原因是否被听任存留？这些例子就更加证明不了什么了，因为相反的实例也是有的。加拿大的易洛魁人在各方面都与周围的白人不相上下。墨西哥最伟大的地理学家是个阿兹特克人；而“天下最伟大的武士们”竟被“劣等种族”的后裔干净利落地撵出了墨西哥，知道这一点我们是颇为称快的。要使任何新的获得固定下来，须得许多世代；一个个体的头脑无论发育能力多强，都无法在其单独的一生之中，走完整个种族费了许多世代才走完的那段进化。因此，个体的负面结果什么也证明不了，即便承认尝试是在切实的条件下进行的；因为它们可以被许多正面结果抵消，正如白人的进步也可以因许多倒退的实例而遭人质疑。民族学著作里，不是引证过红种人、黑人或其他“野蛮人”受过成功教育、达到了相当知识程度的案例么？可他们一朝鄙夷起所受的教诲，便抛掉文明的衣装，重归其游徙生活了。

===Original===
That atavism may sometimes be more powerful than the faculty of perfectibility nobody denies; but such examples in nowise prove the imperfectibility of the race, since the same individuals subjected to European education did certainly, during that period of their existence, advance along the path marked out by their educators.

This same Herve, whom we once more quote, for it is by him that we have heard “the inferiority of races” best sustained, again instances the fact that the savage appears more apt in childhood than at an adult age. But what does that prove? The less developed the race the earlier must the little ones learn to provide for themselves, to evince an already dawning sagacity. As to the adults, if their cerebral development is arrested at an early age it does, indeed, involve the consideration of a physical fact, viz.: the obliteration of the cranial sutures. Contrary to the white races the consolidation first takes place in the anterior portions, so that the development of the brain is arrested, at the outset, in precisely the most active centers of intelligence. This early closing of the futures might be a proof of inferiority if it were proven that the white races had not passed through this stage. But it has been discovered by examination of prehistoric skulls that the sutures originally closed from the front backwards and at an early age, precisely as among our so-called inferior races. In our own days cases of atavism in which this same process occurs are instanced. What, then, remains of this argument?

===Chinese===
返祖现象（atavism）有时会比可完善性（perfectibility）更有力量，这一点谁也不否认；但这类例子绝不能证明种族之无可完善，因为那些受欧式教育的同一些个人，在其生命的那段时期内，确曾沿着教育者标出的路径前进。

还是这位阿尔韦——我们再次引用他，因为“种族的劣等性”这套道理，数他发挥得最透彻——又举出一件事实：野蛮人在童年比在成年显得更有天分。可这证明了什么呢？种族越是欠发达，幼小的孩子就必须越早学会自给自足，显出业已初萌的颖悟。至于成年人，他们的大脑发育若在幼年便告停顿，那确实牵涉一个生理事实，即颅骨骨缝（cranial sutures）的闭合。与白色诸种族相反，这种闭合最先发生于前部，以致大脑的发育从一开始便恰恰在那些最活跃的智能中枢停顿下来。骨缝的这种早期闭合，倘能证明白色诸种族不曾经历过这一阶段，倒未尝不可以作劣等之证。可是对史前头骨的考察已经发现，骨缝当初正是由前向后、在幼年闭合的，恰恰同我们所谓劣等种族里的一样。就在今天，也有人举出发生同一过程的返祖实例。那么，这套论证还剩下什么呢？

===Original===
The example of the republic of Haiti and its military revolutions is quoted for the purpose of turning it into ridicule; but need we go very far back in our own history to find similar examples less excusable since we pretend to be superior? At all events the Haitians reconquered their independence from the French: who were the superiors﻿—those who regained their liberty or those who wanted to keep a people in slavery? Besides one must ignore history completely not to recognize progress among the Haitians, notwithstanding their Souloques, counterparts of our Badingues. When we reflect that the greater number of our self-styled civilized people suffer and die in misery to enrich a minority of idlers and parasites, when we remember that the exploited furnish the force which defends their exploiters, can we believe that we have the right to be very proud and brag of our superiority?

===Chinese===
人们举海地共和国（republic of Haiti）及其迭起的军事政变为例，是为了拿它取笑；可在我们自己的历史里，用不着回溯多远，难道就找不出更不可原谅的类似例子么——何况我们还自命优越！无论如何，海地人是从法国人手里重新夺回独立的：究竟谁是优等者——是重新赢得自由的人，还是想把一个民族继续置于奴役之下的人？再者，除非把历史忘个干净，就不能不承认海地人中间的进步，尽管他们出过苏卢克（Souloques）——恰如我们出过巴丹盖（Badingues）。想到我们这些自诩文明的人民，大多数在贫困中受苦、在贫困中死去，只为让一小撮游手好闲者与寄生虫发财；想到被剥削者恰恰提供了保卫剥削者的武力——我们还能相信自己有权如此骄傲、夸耀自己的优越么？

===Original===
And as for the groups of savages which have been allowed to exist, can we believe that conditions have been procured for them which permit them to expand fully? Certainly we do not want to assert that all races are absolutely identical; but we are persuaded that all have certain aptitudes, certain moral, intellectual, and physical qualities, which, had they been allowed to evolve freely, would have enabled them to take their part in the labor of human civilization. Thus for example, did not these miserable Australians, so low in the scale of humanity, invent the boomerang, that projective missile with its curious power of returning to the thrower, which the Europeans, in spite of their ability, have not succeeded in imitating, and which all their science of ballistics is unable to explain? To be sure the invention of the boomerang has been of no great profit to the history of humanity, but since the ingenuity of its inventors was able to express itself in an object absolutely peculiar to themselves, while the lance, the tomahawk, and the arrow have been known to all other races, who will tell us that, under other conditions, this faculty might not have evolved in more important directions? But no; the white race﻿—aided by the Jewish race which has become white by the necessities of the case﻿—wants to invade all, exploit all. Wherever it has intruded itself the retarded races have had to disappear. In face of the ruins which its conquering fury has heaped up in the presence of the massacres which its exploitations have brought about, one may well inquire whether its role has not been quite as much pernicious as beneficent.

===Chinese===
至于那些被容许存续至今的野蛮人群体，我们能相信已经为他们备下了容其充分发展的条件么？我们当然不想断言一切种族绝对相同；但我们深信，一切种族都各有某些禀赋，某些道德、智识与体力上的品质，倘若听其自由演化，本足以使他们分担人类文明的劳作。譬如说，这些在人类阶梯上位居如此低微、境况如此可怜的澳大利亚土人，不就发明了回力镖（boomerang）么——那件能奇异地返回投掷者之手的投掷武器，欧洲人尽管身手不凡，始终未能仿制成功，他们全部的弹道科学也无法解释？诚然，回力镖的发明对人类历史没有多大益处；可是，既然发明者的巧思能够在一件绝对为其独有的物件上表达出来——须知长矛、战斧和箭为其他一切种族所共知——谁又敢说，在别的条件下，这种能力就不会朝着更重要的方向演化呢？可是不；白色人种——还有那因形势所需而变成了白人的犹太种族从旁相助——定要侵略一切、剥削一切。它所闯入之处，发展迟滞的种族便只得消失。面对它征服的狂怒所堆积起来的废墟，面对它的剥削所造成的屠杀，人们满可以问一问：它所扮演的角色，为害之处是否恰与造福之处一样多？

===Original===
One hundred and fifty thousand years, perhaps, were required for us to emerge from animality; and ten thousand years have witnessed the extinction of the Egyptian, Chaldean, Greek, Roman, Hindu, and Mauritanian civilizations, whilst the Mongolian race was continuing a parallel development. Today we witness the beginning of the decadence of the Latin races, which will shortly become a death-agony unless a social transformation occurs in time to rejuvenate the physical and moral decay entailed by the capitalistic system. Perhaps, if the nations continue to entrench themselves behind their frontiers, our prestige may be taken from us by the Slavic races, which appear younger to us, having more lately come into the current of European civilization. But how long will that period last? What will come after? What will be the regenerating current which will come to restore our anaemiated race, exhausted by the excesses of a badly directed civilization? Every civilization, in its decline, has seen a new race surging in, which, being capable of assimilating the knowledge of the race it replaced, yielded in exchange a new brain, new aptitudes, young and vigorous blood; and this disappearance of civilizations would seem to show that every race has but a certain amount of energy and ability to expend, after which it disappears or remains stationary.

===Chinese===
也许，我们用了十五万年才脱离动物界；而一万年间，埃及、迦勒底、希腊、罗马、印度和毛里塔尼亚诸文明相继熄灭，其间蒙古人种（Mongolian race）却在继续一种平行的发展。今天，我们目睹拉丁诸种族（Latin races）衰落的发端；除非有一场社会变革及时到来，使资本主义制度所造成的体质与道德的衰败重获新生，这场衰落不久便会沦为垂死的挣扎。倘若诸国族继续躲在各自的国界后面掘壕自守，我们的威势或许会被斯拉夫诸种族（Slavic races）夺去——它们在我们眼里显得更年轻，进入欧洲文明的洪流也更晚。但那个时期又将延续多久？其后又将是什么？那股将前来复兴我们这贫血衰弱、被一种导引失当的文明的种种过度耗竭了的种族的再生潮流，又将是怎样一股潮流？每一种文明在其衰落之时，都见过一个新种族涌进：这个新种族既能同化它所取代的种族的知识，便以新的头脑、新的禀赋、年轻而强健的血液作为回报。文明的这一个个消逝似乎表明：每个种族所能支出的精力与才具都只有一定的数额，用罄之后，它便消失，或者停滞。

===Original===
Relative to the foregoing, however, some friends object that today there are no more races, that the civilized world is divided into States, (remains of a past which is in discord with present reality) but constituting an indissoluble whole﻿—civilization from France to Russia and from America to Australia being the same civilization everywhere; that there are no longer any opposing races, but opposing classes. Assuredly we also are convinced that, given facilities of locomotion from one country to another, the enormous extension of international relations, races are bound to disappear by fusing, mixing with each other through intercrossings; that is the very reason we are choked with indignation at seeing entire tribes disappear before they have been able to contribute to our civilization the original share which they may have potentially possessed. When we reflect upon the massacre of inoffensive tribes, on whole races vanished or about to disappear, our thoughts overflow with sadness and melancholy; for we ask ourselves if these “inferior” brothers did not possess some of those qualities, so many of which are lacking in us. The white race could not understand the retarded races; it has broken them. If it had sought to bring them up to a higher phase of development, it could only have done so after the course of a long evolution; but it has never desired to educate; it has desired to exploit, and exploitation becomes extermination in the long run.

===Chinese===
然而，对于上述种种，有些朋友反驳说：如今已经没有种族了；文明世界分成了若干国家（一个与当前现实相扞格的往昔残余），却构成一个不可分割的整体——从法国到俄国、从美洲到澳大利亚，文明到处都是同一个文明；已经不再有相互对立的种族，只有相互对立的阶级。诚然，我们也相信：鉴于国与国之间往来的便利、国际关系的大为扩展，种族注定要通过融合、通过互相通婚的混血而归于消失；恰恰因为这个缘故，眼见整族整族的部落还来不及把他们或许潜在地拥有的那份独创贡献给我们的文明，便已消失，我们才愤懑得透不过气来。想到并无冒犯之心的部落惨遭屠戮，想到整族整族的种族业已湮灭或行将湮灭，我们的思绪便涨满了悲哀与忧伤；因为我们自问：这些“劣等”的兄弟，会不会正有着我们大大缺少的某些品质。白色人种不能理解这些迟滞的种族；它把他们摧折了。倘若它真想把他们提升到更高的发展阶段，那也只有经过漫长的演化方才做得到；可它从来就不想教育；它只想剥削，而剥削到头来便是灭绝。

===Original===
To sum up: in view of our rage for mastery let us ask ourselves whether the civilization of the Iroquois, for example, was much inferior to ours. Are we right in proclaiming ourselves superior to the Incas, who had at least learned how to secure food and shelter to all the members of their society, while poverty gnaws our modern civilization? Nothing justifies the theory of the alleged “inferior races;” it serves only to justify the crimes of the alleged “superior” races.

===Chinese===
总而言之：鉴于我们这股统治的狂热，且自问一句：譬如易洛魁人的文明，究竟比我们的低下多少？我们宣称自己优越于印加人，这对么？他们至少已学会让其社会的一切成员都得到食物与栖身之所，而贫困却在啃噬我们的现代文明。什么也不能为所谓“劣等种族”的理论张目；它只是用来给所谓“优等”种族的罪行作辩护罢了。

## XVI Why We Are Revolutionists / XVI 我们为什么是革命者

===Original===
Why We Are Revolutionists
